# Developer log

Every change to **AzurKit** in detail, for contributors: what it does, the step and decisions it comes from and how
it was checked. What players notice is in [the guide](GUIDE.md), in plain words. The format is loosely based on
[Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com).

## [Unreleased]

Each change's entry is its own file in `devlog/`, never a line here:
`devlog/<section>/<step>-<slug>.md`, its section `added`, `changed`, `removed` or `fixed`, its name in lower-case
letters, digits, dots and hyphens, the file one Markdown bullet. A release joins them into a section below.

## [1.0.2 (November 9, 2026)]

### Added
- **The Characters view lists your variants as its Yours group** (Step 4.6a): `sdk.pickers`' `custom`, `{"title": "Yours", "variants": [...]}` (the Variants view's name for them, Step 4.6c) after the community group, each of `library.listing`'s folders its name, title, base and grade colour (unseen: no sitting grades a recipe of yours), a folder that isn't a variant with its refusal; one picked shows its recipe as yours in the info panel (Source: yours), never as the community entry whose id begins its name (`booblord_mine` read as booblord's, asking to Install it), and a refused one picked names why. The page draws the group with the Variants view (Step 4.6c).
- **Your own variants are worn everywhere by name** (Step 4.6a, the Variants editor's engine; Modkit's variant library, D372, D480, D1087): `tools/characters/library.py` keeps a player's variants a folder each, `<name>/recipe.json` in Modkit's format (`{"<name>": {recipe}}`, the files it names beside it) in `AZURKIT_VARIANTS`, else `variants` in the user data folder, where Modkit saves them, so a Modkit variant opens unmoved (the site's worker reads `variants` in its working folder). `listing` names each folder's variant or its refusal (one recipe named as its folder, no built-in's or species' name, at most 64 deep, the loader's checks); `new`, `save`, `save_as`, `copy_as` (New from this and Save as: of a built-in an own copy, `own_copy`, its recipe laid whole with its files) and `delete` check first and write whole or not at all (D35). `_player_variants.resolve` finds a built-in, else a recipe file's, else with no file yours, so `azurkit build --character '{"species": "mine"}'`, a share link (its name and sha12, Q174), a mods file and the remembered setup wear one by name, and a link to one since changed or gone says what to import. The keys an answer for a character is kept under (the app's store, its work ahead, the sdk's dresses) stand on that folder's recipe and files by sha256 (`library.stamp`). `tests/test_variant_library.py`: the listing's table, the writers whole or nothing, the link and the keys, and the probe variant saved as yours building its golden (Modkit's first recording) file for file.
- **The Variants editor's colour views move into the package** (Step 4.6b): `tools/characters/colour_preview.py` is Modkit's `authoring/colour_preview.py` on AzurKit's engine (its archives handed in, no file read or written, its own sines and cosines fdlibm's, Modkit's pickled `keep` and `kept` left out since the store keeps no pickle): a body drawn front and back in a recipe's `colours`, each row's tile and palette, and its rows' textures whole as PNGs (`rows`, `pngs`), so the editor (4.6b-4.6g) needs nothing of Modkit's authoring side; `tests/test_variant_editor.py` holds its picture of town_female_2 in booblord_human's colours by sha256 on the recording host and close enough off it (D1016).
- **The Variants editor's engine calls** (Step 4.6b; Modkit's `variant_library` past its library's rules, on the new `tools/characters/editor.py`): an edit is `{"iso", "name", "recipe", "folder", "packs"}` (a built-in's by name alone), read as the loader reads a recipe file, every refusal by name; `sdk.variant_check` (Check with game data, a bar op) dresses it as a build does (its recipe written beside its files as a hidden file the dress's `variant_file` names, removed after; beamlord 3.4 s, a pelvis-scaling recipe on town_female_2 5.6 s here) and gives its log, sha, body and bones, its own load runs against its build's lines, its effects rows' bytes and its picture, keeping that dress before its colours for the process (the app's in its engine, `engine.JOBS`), on which `variant_colours` (the live recolour, 0.14 s), `variant_preview` (the Shape tab's quick preview, no dress: 0.09 s, the same picture a dress at those values draws), `variant_rows` and `export_pngs` stand; `add_png`, `add_wav`, `base_bones`, `own_sounds`, `kit_throw` and `move_sources` need no dress. `characters.worn_by` takes the files to compose and `picker.worn_body` names the worn body. The library's list, Save, Delete, Import and Export calls wait for Step 4.6a's library, and the site's worker handlers for Step 4.6c.
- **The Variants view's library calls** (Step 4.6c, on Step 4.6a's library; Modkit's Variants page's list, Save, Save as, New from this and Delete, D372, D480, D1087): `sdk.variants` lists the built-ins (each with the community entry whose files it needs, given the manifest), your folders (a refused one with its refusal), the species New starts on and each grade's words and colour; `sdk.variant_open` gives the recipe a variant opens as (a built-in's `from` it), `sdk.variant_read` reads an edit as the library would hold it (sha, base, mode, body, grade; a refusal returned, never raised), `sdk.variant_save` writes Save, Save as and New from this whole or not at all (the folder's files handed back for the site to keep), `sdk.variant_delete` removes a folder, and `sdk.variant_import` and `sdk.variant_export` move one as a zip (`library.import_`, `library.pack`: an Export's zip or a recipe.json alone in, its other files left out, a path outside its folder or past 256 MiB unpacked refused; out, the files not yours to share named, D393). `tests/sdk_api.txt` re-recorded by `scripts/sdk_api.py` for the seven calls; `tests/test_variant_library.py` holds them.
- **The Variants view** (Step 4.6c, the Variants editor's shell; Modkit's Variants page, D372, D480, D1087, D1144): the Characters tab gains Models · Variants view buttons. `web/variants.mjs` lists the built-ins (community ones marked) and yours (a refused folder marked with its refusal), each in its grade's colour and mark under the Models view's legend, opens one as the engine does (`sdk.variant_open`), and edits it: New (a species from the shared picker, in the page's own dialog: the species then the objects, each group counted, each in its grade's colour and mark), New from this (an own copy with its files), Save, Save as, Delete after a yes, Import and Export (a zip: the site's download, the app's Downloads folder or save dialog, naming the files not yours to share), Gameplay, the Body tab (size, scale, arms out, rotate, title, notes; a recipe `from` another read-only) and the JSON tab (Apply checks first and keeps the old recipe on a refusal or a new base, saying why). The status line reads each edit through `sdk.variant_read` (sha12, grade, saved, checked); Check with game data (`sdk.variant_check`, greyed until an image, D979; its bar the bottom bar's) draws the dress, and opening another variant drops a running Check's answer, that one shown as it ships. Unsaved edits are asked about before another opens, before a Build of that variant and (the site) before the page goes. Both hosts: the app's `api/variant_*` routes (the Check and Step 4.6b's dress-bound calls in its engine), the site's worker handlers for every editor call and its `{type: "library"}` handoff, IndexedDB's `azurkit-variants` (which the Tools tab's Storage names and its Clear removes; the content keeper now closes on a delete's version change). The Models view draws your variants as its Yours group, the list's name for them, with Edit… on a chosen variant. `tests/test_site.py` and `tests/test_hosts.py` hold it on each host; `tests/_perf.py` (protected) adds A87-A92 and S73-S78: the view's open, Save, New from this on booblord, Import, Export and Check of booblord's copy.
- **The Variants view's Shape and Jiggle tabs** (Step 4.6d; Modkit's Variants page's Shape and Jiggle tabs, D455, D445, D1087), on the site and the app, each a module of `web/variants/` (`shape.mjs`, `jiggle.mjs`) on a tab list `web/variants.mjs` hands each its view: Shape, a slider a shape (a track as a display hint, a box taking any number, D23; a recipe from another writes `shapes.<name>.value`, dropped back at that one's value) with what it sizes and its mesh's ops, the sized first; a drag shows the nearest shape held at once, marked Preview (D1011: the last Check's or a quick preview's pictures), and once let go the engine's quick preview of those values (`sdk.variant_preview`, now a bar op with its steps: the Check's dress, its vertices moved) or, with no Check yet, the Check, never a dress mid-drag (Modkit's AZ-793). Jiggle, a row a real-time spring (its stiffness, damping and strength), a recipe from another's copied into its own jiggle at the first edit, a springs row added on a bone no row swings (the last Check's body's, else its base's read from the image, `sdk.base_bones`) or removed, up to the game's 32 rows. `sdk.variant_read` adds the tabs' rows (`editor.shape_rows`, `editor.jiggle_of`, the track and the rows' cap), the same rows Modkit's tabs read. `tests/sdk_api.txt` re-recorded; `tests/test_variant_library.py`, `tests/test_site.py` and `tests/test_hosts.py` hold them; the perf gate's A94/S80 (the drag) and A95/S81 (let go, a bar) time them.
- **The Variants view's Colours tab** (Step 4.6e; Modkit's Colours tab and colour wheel, D772, D809 (a), Step 2.203, D544, AZ-797): `web/variants/colours.mjs` edits a recipe's `colours` on both hosts: a row (or every row) picked by its button or tile, its main colour moved on a colour wheel (a press or a drag: one `hsv` op's hue and saturation), its value on the bar beside it, hex, hue, saturation and value boxes (any finite number, D23; text that isn't one named), Only hues with a soft edge, swaps from its palette to a colour picked on the wheel picker (`pickColour`, the page's own dialog), Reset row; the engine's live recolour (`sdk.variant_colours`, one ask at a time, the newest edit asked once it answers) draws the rows' tiles, the palette and the front and back views, a portrait as wide as the body draws. `sdk.variant_colours` now gives the asked `row`'s palette before its ops and after them one for one (`colour_preview.swatches`, Modkit's page's reading, equal to it on the same inputs), the body and whether it was dressed; Export PNGs writes the rows' textures into a folder (the app: the dialog's, else a new `<name> PNGs` in Downloads) or, with no `out`, gives them as a zip (`sdk.export_pngs`, the site's download); Import PNG copies an edited texture into your saved variant's folder as the row's only op, after a yes where the row has edits (the app's `api/add_png` by path, a share refused; the site's worker from the picked file's bytes, kept in this browser beside the recipe). The view gains Undo (its button, ⌘Z or Ctrl+Z outside a text box): 100 steps, edits closer than 0.6 s one. `tests/test_hosts.py`, `tests/test_site.py` and `tests/test_variant_editor.py` hold it; `tests/_perf.py` (protected) adds A100-A103 and S100-S103: the tab opened on another of yours, a 30-move drag on the wheel, Export PNGs and Import PNG.
- **The Variants editor's Moves and Attacks tabs** (Step 4.6f; Modkit's MovesTab and AttacksTab, D768, D785, D480, D495, X33): `web/variants/moves.mjs` picks the clip each idle (the base, six fidgets each after the one before, the wait between them), victory (with Cancel after), jump and gait slot plays, from a menu in the page's own dialog of the build's pick, the body's own clips, the recipe's and, once an image is picked, every humanoid body's the bake can put on it (garret4's first, the townsfolk in one submenu, a lender's many clips grouped by their first word) and each built-in's Blender clips; a lent clip goes into the recipe's `clips` under Modkit's short name and is dropped once nothing plays it. `web/variants/attacks.mjs` picks each attack column's clip the same way, and writes life drain, the carry and held columns, each column clip's seek and its generator's numbers, the weapon (`sdk.variant_weapons`, the Models view's offers for the recipe less its weapon; refused while the recipe plays clips of its own, saying why) and the attack numbers and switches (any finite number, no clamp, D23). A recipe from another shows that one's moves read-only (`sdk.variant_read`'s new `laid`, `editor.laid`), booblord's saying her Axion staff plays over them; `sdk.move_sources` now names each lender body by its species. Both hosts (the app's `api/variant_weapons`, the site worker's handler). `tests/test_variant_editor.py` runs the tabs' logic under Node on the image's answers (Modkit's four tab tests' cases, every recipe read back through the loader); `test_site.py` and `test_hosts.py` drive them on each host; `tests/_perf.py` (protected) adds A110-A113 and S110-S113: each tab's first open, a pick of another body's clip and a weapon's.
- **The Variants view's Sounds and Effects tabs** (Step 4.6g; Modkit's Sounds and Effects tabs, Step 2.164b; D480, D538's E4, D771, D1087): `web/variants/sounds.mjs` sets a recipe's `sounds` (its own or Garret's) and `voice` (its own or silent), a WAV per role (`sdk.add_wav`: the app's dialog or a typed path, the site's file pick by its bytes, kept beside the recipe in this browser), × clearing a role, and a made sound's numbers (a whoosh's; a number back at its default dropping its key), greying what the build refuses with why (Garret's sounds, a base with sounds of its own: `sdk.own_sounds`); `web/variants/effects.mjs` adds, edits and removes `effects` rows (template, colour through the page's colour picker, columns, seek, each param's track and box, auto), greying a template the build has no room for (one projectile, none over the base's bare-hands kit in Balanced: `sdk.kit_throw`; three shields and beams; none with `fx_ring` false), shows each row's bytes and the own load after a Check (a refused row marked), and lists the `fx`, `fx_grafts` and `fx_ring` edits read-only. A recipe `from` another shows that one's sounds read-only and lays its rows over that one's. The engine gives the tabs what they read: `sdk.variants`' `editor` (the WAV roles, the templates; `editor.lists`) and `sdk.variant_read`'s sounds and effects as the loader lays them (`editor.heard`, with `from_effects`); `tests/sdk_api.txt` re-recorded. `web/variants/common.mjs` holds what the tabs share. The guide's Variants view line names the tabs.
- **A recipe guide for community authors** (Step 4.6g, P76; Modkit's `PATCHES.md` variants section, short and plain): `docs/VARIANTS.md` gives a variant's folder and files, the rules (unknown fields refused by name, numbers never clamped, what the checksum covers), each recipe field by group (labels, body, attacks, moves, sounds, effects, shape, looks, `from`) and an example; README's Characters line and the guide's Variants view line link it.
- **The Data tab's All files…** (Step 4.6h, Modkit's P58: its Game Data page's "All files…" and `gui/xbr_workspace.py`'s file list): under All tables' list, every .xbr of the picked image (its release's: 42 USA, 44 Europe, `index/index.xbr` among them), config.xbr first, each row opening to its keyed tables read from the image no further than its TOC (`core.xbr.document.toc_size` and `tags`), the search box narrowing files and tables; config.xbr's 18 by their own names (the shipped file, no image), none in any other vanilla file on either release (Modkit's AZ-124: only config.xbr has `tabl` entries). Greyed with its line until an image is picked (D979). `tools.data`'s `table` and `check` take any file's keyed table (its vanilla bytes and release; no schema notes, int32 rules or pack marks, which are config.xbr's), so a `set` operation may name any file; `sdk.data`'s `files` and `tables` asks and a `file` on `table` and `data_edit` (the app's `api/data` with the image's path, the site's worker mounting the File), `azurkit data --iso PATH --files | --file FILE`; perf rows A87, A88, S73 and S74. Modkit's workspace (its extract of the ISO's gamedata/ and its pending_edits.json) is not ported: AzurKit reads the image in place and keeps the edits in the recipe.
- **`azurkit entity diff`: two config.xbr entities side by side** (Step 4.6i, P59; Modkit's `entity diff`): `sdk.entity_diff({"a", "b", "all"?})` (`tools.data.compare`) joins two entities' cells (each its column of that exact name, the last when repeated; an empty cell is none) by property in each table that holds either, sorted: what they hold differently, what only one holds and, with `all`, what they hold alike, and the tables only one is in; an entity no table holds is refused by name, either side (AZ-145). `azurkit entity diff A B [--all] [--json]` prints them by table (`~`, `-`, `+`, `=`) or as JSON, from the config.xbr AzurKit ships. Its JSON rows equal Modkit f482ea041's `entity diff --json --all` on five pairs (garret4/air_elemental, catalisk/catalisk_baby, golem/lord_golem, norm_1/norm_2, garret4/garret4), with AzurKit's words for the types (number, text) and `table` for Modkit's `section`. Modkit's `--config` and `--iso` aren't kept: AzurKit reads only the vanilla config.xbr, both releases' file. Test: `test_entity_diff_sets_two_entities_cells_side_by_side_table_by_table_and_names_one_no_table_holds` (Modkit's five vanilla tests and its CLI test, merged).
- **`azurkit mod-template`: a mods file of config.xbr's vanilla values to edit** (Step 4.6i, P22; Modkit's `mod-template`): `sdk.mod_template({"tables", "entity"?, "name"?})` (`tools.data.template`) writes Modkit's mod file of the vanilla value of every number cell of the named tables, or of one entity there, by config.xbr's own names as `_keyed_patches` (a repeated property its first row's, a repeated entity its last column's, the one its name edits; an entity with none left out), from the config.xbr AzurKit ships, so it needs no image; `azurkit mod-template TABLE... [--entity NAME] [--name NAME] [--out FILE]` writes or prints it, a table without the entity left out with a line saying so, and an entity none has or a table config.xbr hasn't refused by name with nothing written. It reads back (`build --mods`, Load, `azurkit diff`) as edits that change nothing until a value is edited. Against Modkit f482ea041's on nine tables (5,937 cells, the duplicate-column tables among them) and on `--entity catalisk` over damage and settings_foo, `_keyed_patches` is the same JSON, key order too; only the description names AzurKit's verbs. Test: `test_a_mod_template_holds_each_number_cells_vanilla_value_and_reads_back_changing_nothing`.
- **AzurKit reads Modkit's mod files** (Step 4.6i, P19b; D944, D1168): `edits/modkit.py` makes a Modkit mod JSON (its TOOLS.md "Mod files": `patches`, grouped `sections`, `_keyed_patches`, `xbr_edits`, metadata and Level Studio's `source_hashes`) a preset of one `data` layer, its edits as `xbr` operations in Modkit's order and no packs layer (the packs on by default apply, as Modkit's `patch` did; a host keeps its ticks), so `azurkit build --mods` and both hosts' Load take it (the page keeps its ticks and every setting, and its line says so, `web/app.mjs`'s `load`; it said "0 packs" and put the settings back to their defaults) (`sdk._read`, under `read_mods` and `mods_file`; `preset.parse` split out of `preset.read`). Each config cell edit is checked as a build makes it of the vanilla config.xbr as it is read (`tools.data.preview` over `edits.xbr.writes`, split out of `xbr.plan`), so a bad one is refused at Load, never later in the Data tab. Modkit's `xbr_edits` ops map onto `set` (`set_keyed_double`, `set_keyed_string`, `point_keyed_string`) and `replace_bytes` (`replace_bytes`, and `replace_string_at` NUL-padded to its old slot); a config.xbr byte edit with no original takes the shipped file's bytes, one whose span already holds its value is dropped (Modkit's "already applied"), and a level file's needs its original (Q225: AzurKit reads no level before a build); each byte edit is checked, as Modkit's were, against what the mod's earlier edits left (its original may be an earlier edit's value, a cell it set or another byte edit), its operation expecting the vanilla bytes, and one that overwrites part of an earlier edit covers both, so the layer, which drops an overwritten operation whole (D28), leaves Modkit's bytes (`edits.modkit._Chain`). `patches` and `sections` refuse Modkit's 1,851 retired registry.json names (AZ-123's 171, AZ-167's 1,680), naming the cell each wrote, from `edits/modkit_retired.json`, generated once from Modkit's frozen table; `level_patches` is refused by name (dropped, D95), as are any other key, a byte edit of no bytes, a mod with no edit shape (AZ-215), a value that isn't a finite number, and a `source_hashes` file no release's vanilla file is (AZ-214; only `gamedata/index/index.xbr` differs between the releases, a known flaw: a one-release pin is held only by the operations' own originals). A null or empty `_keyed_patches`, `xbr_edits`, `level_patches` or `source_hashes` is none, as Modkit read it. Its text may nest past a link's 8-deep cap, to 64 (`preset.MAX_MOD_DEPTH`: `sections` nests groups freely), and give a key twice, the last kept, as Modkit's `json` read it (`preset.parse`'s `modkit`); a preset's text may not. Checked against Modkit f482ea041's writer on vanilla config.xbr: the 8,826 respelled double cells through each of the three cell shapes, three `xbr_edits` ops and a duplicate column's give the same file (sha256); AzurKit's keyed ops also take D51's spellings, which Modkit's `xbr_edits` refused. A `set_keyed_string` to text another cell of its table holds points the cell at that string (AzurKit's `set`), where Modkit rewrote the cell's own slot and refused a shared one: the bytes differ, every cell reads the same. Test: `test_a_modkit_mod_file_reads_as_its_xbr_operations_in_modkits_order_and_each_refusal_names_its_edit`.
- **What a mods file changes, before a build: the Data tab's Changes and `azurkit diff`** (Step 4.6i, P23; Modkit's `diff`): the Data tab's seventh view, Changes (`sdk.data`'s view `changes`, `tools.data._changes`), lists each cell the page's edits change (a loaded mods file's too), table by table in the tabs' order, its vanilla value beside the cell itself (editable, marked ● and ⚠ as elsewhere), then the byte edits a mods file brought, each its file, offset and size; with no edits it says so. `sdk.mods_diff({"text"})` gives each edit of a mods file (a preset, a build's mods file, a link or Modkit's mod file) as a build makes it of the vanilla config.xbr (`tools.data.preview`): a cell by the file's own names with its vanilla and new values, one that changes nothing, a byte edit of a level left to the build, or why a build refuses it, beside the rest, with a Modkit mod's name and description; `azurkit diff MODS...` prints them numbered (`~`, `=`, `?`, `ERROR`) with the counts and exits 1 when one would fail the build. On Modkit's own diff input its lines match Modkit f482ea041's `diff` edit for edit (same cells, values, refusal and exit), and AzurKit lists the `xbr_edits` byte edit too. 1,000 changed cells take about 15 ms in CPython; `tests/_perf.py`'s A94 and S80 time the view after A46's and S39's load. Tests: `test_a_mods_files_edits_preview_against_vanilla_on_the_command_line_and_in_the_data_tabs_changes`, `test_a_modkit_mod_file_loads_in_the_tools_tab_and_the_data_tabs_changes_list_what_it_changes`.
- **Build a given plan file** (Step 4.6j, P31; Modkit's `randomize-full --plan`): `azurkit build --plan FILE` builds that plan file (`azurkit plan`'s) as it is, in the seed's place, and a build recipe's `randomizer` takes it as `plan`, so a plan build's mods file and link build it again. It carries its own seed and settings (a `--seed`, `--settings` or `--share` beside it is refused); one edited, from another database, typed otherwise or not beatable under its settings (`layer.checked_plan`: the fill's own verify, since anyone can make a plan's sha256 again) is refused by name before the image is read; built at movement sliders other than its own, each problem its placement has at the tier they pick leaves the logic unverified (Modkit's flag, `layer.plan_reasons`), never refused. `sdk.randomizer_settings` reads one for the tab.
- **Open a plan… on the Randomizer tab** (Step 4.6j, P31): the site and the app take a plan file in the Settings view; the engine reads it back and checks it beatable under its own settings (`sdk.randomizer_settings`' `plan`), and the tab shows its seed and settings, locked but the obsidian cost, with a line naming the file and Close plan. The page's recipe (Build's, Copy link's, a build ahead's) is then the plan whole, a refused one is named by its file, Track this seed tracks it, and a plan build's recipe (a link, Load its recipe, Track this build) opens it again. The perf gate times it as A97 and S83 (`app-plan`, `site-plan`).
- **The app's Play, the macOS admin attach, the tray's seed and kept build logs** (Step 4.6k, D256, D257, D261, D274; Modkit's `live_launch`, `live_helper`, its app's Play and its Build page's logs, ported): Play (the footer, beside Save) finds the player's xemu (a running one, the standard places, Spotlight, `PATH` and Flatpak, the one picked last), launches it with the saved build in its tray and a private QMP socket (the fork's shared RAM too) and tracks that build's seed with Live on, Live trying those private endpoints first (`azurkit.app.xemu`, `azurkit.app.play`, `api/play`); with the player's xemu already running it offers to attach, which on macOS reads it through the read-only root helper behind one admin prompt (`azurkit.app.live_helper`, run from a root-owned copy on macOS's own Python; `api/attach`, Attach… in the Live bar). Ticking Live first tracks the seed of the randomized AzurKit build in a running xemu's tray, passing over another disc for the next xemu's (`api/tray`). Each Build writes a log in the user's log folder (`azurkit.app.logs`: the recipe, the report without its plan or why it failed, each save), which the Tools tab opens (`api/logs`) and Storage clears; the frozen app carries the helper as a file.
- **The build checks** (Step 4.6l; D950, Modkit's `verify-patches` and `inspect-physics`, P26 and P27): `python -m devkit builds verify-patches` reads any build's `default.xbe` (an image or a raw XBE, AzurKit's, Modkit's or another's, either release) and reports each pack's sites at the release's own address against its vanilla XBE: fixed patches applied, original or a mismatch (by digests), sliders' values, trampolines landing in the shim room, a `custom_apply` pack's places written, the frame-rate guard (fps_unlock's places at the rate the build runs, its step cap among them, Modkit's safety-critical guard), the swap's sites and every byte changed outside them all and the shim room, the header held to the state they derive; `--strict` fails on such a byte. `builds inspect-physics` shows what each player_physics slider site holds (the carved floats, the hand shims' k, the speed toggle, walk's runSpeed). Both are devkit's (the survey's rows don't ship them; `azurkit open` still reads AzurKit builds). An AzurKit build of the packs and a character passes `--strict` under both this and Modkit's verb. `devkit/builds/README.md` is the reference, `tests/test_devkit_builds.py` the tests (Modkit's verify-patches and inspect-physics tests, merged into two, on both releases).
- **The movie and audio tools** (Step 4.6l; D950, Modkit's `azurik-mod movies` and `audio` verbs, P65 and P66): `python -m devkit movies info` reads a Bink movie's header (a folder's as a table), `movies frames` its per-frame offset table (`--info`, the track layout detected) or its frames as PNGs into a new folder by ffmpeg, naming ffmpeg when it is missing or fails; `python -m devkit audio dump` writes every wave entry of an XBR (fx.xbr, a voice bank), a streamed one's samples from its sdsr, with a WAV for each the game decodes and a manifest naming each from the archive's own table. The manifest gives each entry's first 32 bytes as a sha256[:12], where Modkit's held them in hex. On the USA image both verbs give Modkit's output: the 14 movies' table and AdreniumLogo's frame table, and fx.xbr's 700 and english.xbr's 234 waves file for file. `devkit/movies/README.md` and `devkit/audio/README.md` are the reference, `tests/test_devkit_media.py` the tests (Modkit's movie and audio tests, merged into five).
- **The XBR file tools** (Step 4.6l; D950, Modkit's `azurik-mod xbr` verbs, P62 and P63): `python -m devkit xbr` holds `inspect` (a TOC entry's first records, each 4-byte column labelled by the first reading that fits, the stride detected; a column's bytes only with `--hex`), `diff` (each tag's size change, the strings each tag gained or lost and each entry changed in place; exit 1 when the files differ), `edit` (strings, bytes and keyed cells into a new file, whole or not at all, never over its input or a vanilla image), `xref` (the pointer fields AzurKit's parse models: config.xbr's 18 keyed tables, 10,340, with the damage and magic tables Modkit's model left out, and the unmodeled sections) and `verify` (the file parses whole; `--cross-check-schema` lists a pack's config.xbr cell `schema.json` doesn't document), on `core.xbr`. Modkit's refusing-only `--add-row`, `--remove-row` and `--grow-pool` aren't ported. `devkit/xbr/README.md` is the reference, `tests/test_devkit_xbr.py` the tests (Modkit's xbr CLI and schema-lint tests, merged into five).
- **Contributors: the RE data** (Step 4.11d1; D990): `devkit/re/data/` holds the reverse-engineering knowledge that had no home in AzurKit, from Modkit's last cut: the Ghidra project's snapshot (`ghidra_snapshot.json`: 545 functions, 1,103 labels, 10 structures), the shim header's 83 VA anchors (`anchors.toml`: each one's USA address, section, what the vanilla XBE holds there and a note), its 10 structures and 2 enums with their offset asserts, the player entity's field offsets and the vanilla step (`structs.h`, which no shim includes) and a note for each of the facts' 354 functions (`function_notes.toml`, the vanilla-symbol registry's docs). `python -m devkit re xbe` names a listing's addresses from them as Modkit's `xbe` did: the facts' functions and kernel slots first, then Ghidra's functions, the anchors, Ghidra's data labels and the packs' sites, Europe's through its address map. `tests/test_re_xbe.py` gains Modkit's anchor and header guards (test_va_audit): each anchor holds what `anchors.toml` says in both releases' XBEs, each anchor has one check, `structs.h` defines no anchor and compiles with the shims' flags, and the notes name exactly the facts' functions.
- **Contributors: the Ghidra side** (Step 4.11d2; D990): `python -m devkit re` gains Modkit's Ghidra verbs on the stock GhydraMCP plugin, through one client on 127.0.0.1: `ghidra-serve` serves a read-only copy of a Ghidra project headlessly (copies in the user cache folder, or the main checkout's `out/` where that is hidden, as on Linux, since Ghidra refuses a hidden folder; agents' ports 8212-8229), `ghidra-query` decompiles, disassembles (never bytes) and lists references from a serve, `ghidra-sync` renames and annotates from the USA facts' functions, the RE data's anchors and the packs (writing only the program `--target` names, keeping names and plate comments someone wrote, `--push-structs` from `structs.h`, `--emit-plan` for `ghidra-apply`), `ghidra-snapshot` is the only writer of `devkit/re/data/ghidra_snapshot.json`, `struct-diff` checks `structs.h` against Ghidra, and `ghidra-apply`/`ghidra-backup` write a reviewed plan into the closed project with a backup, a verify and a byte-identical restore. `uv run bootstrap.py --with re` installs Ghidra 12.1.2, Temurin 25, ghidra-xbe and GhydraMCP 3.0.0-rc.1 into `.tools/` from `devkit/re/tools.toml`'s sha256 pins and prints the bridge's `claude mcp add` line: the bridge runs in `devkit/re/ghidra_bridge.py`'s locked script environment (mcp 1.30.0, as Modkit ran it), since its own header's mcp 1.6.0 breaks on a current pydantic. The skill `azurik-ghidra-mcp` comes along, rewritten. `tests/test_ghidra.py` holds Modkit's five Ghidra test files lean, on `tests/mock_ghidra.py`; its three `ghidra` tests run the scripts in `.tools`' Ghidra and fail, naming bootstrap, without it.
- **Contributors: shim authoring** (Step 4.11e; D990): `python -m devkit re` gains Modkit's shim authoring verbs. `plan-trampoline` sizes a hook site to an instruction boundary (Modkit's decoder, unchanged; the code's bytes print only with `--bytes`); `shim-inspect` lists a shim object's sections, symbols and relocations and the game functions it calls at a release's addresses (unchanged, on `core/coff.py`); `new-shim` is rewritten for AzurKit's one-file pack: it writes `packs/<name>.py` and `shims/<name>.c`, takes the entry's ABI from a live Ghidra or, offline, from the facts' function a hooked `CALL` calls, and prints the hand edits left (the registry, the facts site, shimkit, the goldens, Europe); `test-for-va` picks the tests that spell a VA or a pack and those the facts, the goldens and fullsuite's import graph tie to it; `region-map` checks Europe's address map in the facts against both images by each entry's role and finds a USA address's Europe twin. `pin_va` isn't ported (the facts' sha256s pin each site). `tests/test_re_shims.py` holds them leanly, with Modkit's Europe guards: every address a Europe pack names or writes is mapped and declared, its shims fix no address of the image, and the live kit's place and level hop write Europe's own buffers.
- **The `azurik` MCP server** (Step 4.11f; D64, D950, D990): `devkit/mcp`, Modkit's 8 game-data tools under their names on mcp 2.3.0 and AzurKit's engine and sdk, launched by `.mcp.json` (`uv run --locked python -m devkit.mcp`), dev only. `entity_list` and `entity_get` read config.xbr (a file or an image's) by its own names with D51's spellings; `xbr_edit` writes a `.xbr` with the mods file's `set` and `replace_bytes`; `pack_list` is `sdk.catalog`'s packs with each config.xbr slider's cell; `pack_build_iso` and `rando_build_iso` build through `sdk.build` with the recipe `azurkit build` makes (the default packs, `enable`, `disable`, `params`, a mods file; a seed's settings, share and obsidian cost, a character, a spoiler file); `iso_extract` writes an image's files into a new folder whole or not at all, refusing AZ-216's names; `iso_repack` packs a folder's edits into the vanilla image with `filelist.txt` derived. Each answers in the `{success, result | error}` envelope, clamps nothing (D23), writes nothing when it fails (D35) and never over its input or a vanilla image (D36); the server keeps AZ-154's move of fd 1, so a tool's print never reaches the wire. On the same config.xbr its entity reads and `xbr_edit`'s bytes equal Modkit's tools'. `docs/MCP.md` is its reference; `tests/test_mcp.py` (3 tests) replaces Modkit's 4 files and 30 tests. mcp joins the `devkit` group, `scripts/freeze.py` leaves its cryptography out of the app, and `tests/test_contracts.py` refuses an import of mcp from `src/azurkit`. The `xemu` and `ghidra` pytest markers are registered for devkit's harness and Ghidra tests, and CI deselects `ghidra` on every leg and `xemu` on Linux and Windows (`tests.yml`, `scripts/ci_shards.py`'s log reader).
- The simulator comes to devkit (Step 4.13a): `devkit/sim` gains Modkit's L0-L3 (`static`, `l1`, `l2`, `l3`), L3's search and trick explorer (`l3_search`, `l3_explore`, `l3_options`, `tricks_detect`), `douse`, `faces`, `corpus`, `overlay`, `l3_hold` (the verb `python -m devkit sim l3-hold`) and the envelope's L2 flights (`envelope`), ported at Modkit `f482ea041`: each is Modkit's code with its imports pointed at AzurKit's engine through `devkit/sim/ports.py`, but for L0's XBR check (AzurKit's `Document` parse; Modkit's pointer graph has no home), the overlay's and L2's packs (planned and laid by `tools.mods`), the kernel's exports (`devkit/sim/data/kernel.toml`, from Modkit's tables), L3's engine files and the hold's proofs path. `devkit/sim/data/sim_blind_spots.toml`, `tests/test_simulator.py` (32 tests: 31 of Modkit's and its build-validity L0 and L1 check over every pack at its slider ends), `tests/test_roll.py` (4) and `docs/SIMULATOR.md` come with it; the pool, FastL3 and the scripts' verbs come with the reach engine's and certification's steps.
- The randomizer database's tools and benches come to devkit (Step 4.13b): `python -m devkit rando db-extract` (Modkit's `levels.extract` and `write_generated`: the 23 generated files, byte for byte the shipped ones from either release's image), `check` (Modkit's `rando check`: the same summary on the shipped database as at Modkit's freeze), `bench`, `impact` and `soak` (Modkit's `rando_bench.py`, `rando_impact.py` and `rando_soak.py`), ported at Modkit `f482ea041` with their imports pointed at AzurKit's engine through `devkit/rando/ports.py` (the image's reader and walk, the identity gate, D36's output check, config.xbr's keyed tables), and `tests/rando`'s 108 fixture files in `devkit/rando/data`. The rewrites: each verb's `main(argv)` returns its exit code; the soak's profiles and drawn settings hold `dark_fire` 1 (Modkit's strict had 2, which AzurKit refuses since dark areas always need Fire 1) and its builds run `azurkit build --seed --share` (no `--plan`), checking the build planned the soak's plan; the impact ranking names its places from `devkit/rando/draft/authoring` (the authoring's step brings it); and the four plans that pinned the dark-area rule at Fire 2 with Fire 1 held pin it at Fire 1 with no fire held. `tests/test_rando_db.py` (7 cases): the extract on both images, its refusals, the check's counts and errors, the 68-plan solver corpus, the impact ranking, and the bench's and soak's inputs passing the planner's checks; `devkit/rando/README.md` has each verb.
- The reach model's derive and the realm authoring come to devkit (Step 4.13c): `devkit/rando/draft` gains Modkit's `reach`, `flights`, `gates`, `moves`, `sites`, `route`, `l3_goal`, `coverage`, `value`, `svg`, `export` and `author` with the 22 authoring specs and `blocker_families.toml`, and `devkit/rando` the verbs `derive`, `author`, `check` and `restack` (Modkit's `rando derive | author | check` and `scripts/restack.py` in AzurKit's form: re-derive, re-author, check, then `record_goldens.py`'s randomizer goldens), ported at Modkit `f482ea041`: each module is Modkit's code on the shipped draft's envelope, floors, geometry and wiring, but for the derived and level files' data folder (the shipped one), the stamp's draft tables (`tiers.toml` in `src`, `blocker_families.toml` in devkit, by their old names) and the routers' key files; `devkit/rando/physics.py` restores the walk-off carry, the flights' certificates and `PICK_STAND` that AzurKit's physics dropped, and `derive.router` is the route pilot's harness-free half. The authoring specs' evidence cites name what they rest on in words, but w3's barrier reason, which `db_digest` hashes (its level file's own). Re-derived: the 17 derived files whose stamps the scrub of `tricks.toml`'s skip notes had made stale (their stamps alone change; no plan does). `tests/test_reach_model.py` (43 of Modkit's 70), `tests/test_movement_envelope.py` (5 of 7), the trick explorer's census test in `tests/test_simulator.py` and `docs/RANDOMIZER_DB.md` come with it; the certification's and the xemu chain's tests come with their steps.
- The reach engine comes to devkit (Step 4.13d1): `devkit/reach` is Modkit's `reach_engine` (its nodes, launches, runner, cache, graph, verdicts, aims, pair stage and rigs), `reach_native` (the native kernel, its C controllers and the launch backend), FastL3 with its C step and `unicorn/unicorn_fastl3.patch` (GPL-2, its own `NOTICE.md`), the L3 pool and the engine's scripts as verbs (`python -m devkit reach reach-engine | reach-truth | rig-triggers | native-trace | native-check | native-census | build-unicorn`, and `python -m devkit sim explore | conformance`), ported at Modkit `f482ea041`, frozen (D1143). Each module is Modkit's code with its imports renamed, but for the cache's and the kernel's key files (their devkit paths), FastL3's patch path, the truth set's folder (`reach-truth/` in the user data folder, read in place; the retarget stage's frame too), the reach models (Modkit's pickles in the L3 store's `x7/reach/`, read in place by `devkit/reach/models.py` through a fixed module table that refuses any other class), the two shadow pins whose functions' imports moved, the explorer bench's own processes (`-m devkit.sim.explore`) and the verbs' arguments; the area imports the randomizer's art first, for the shipped draft's import cycle. `devkit/rando/physics.py` restores the envelope's flap schedule the aims fly, and `devkit/sim/ports.vanilla_iso` keeps the image it passed in the user cache, as Modkit's did: an L3 pool's workers each hashed the whole image inside their first step, past L3's watchdog. `tests/test_reach_engine.py` (Modkit's 38 and the reader's), the 9 pool, FastL3 and verb cases of `tests/test_simulator.py` with the image stamp's, `docs/REACH.md` and `devkit/reach/README.md` come with it.
- The reach engine's certification and xemu checks come to devkit (Step 4.13d2): `python -m devkit reach l3-certify | l3-contact | l3-gate` and `python -m devkit xemu chain`, with the route pilot (`devkit/xemu/route.py`) and the minigame solvers (`devkit/xemu/minigames.py`), are Modkit's `scripts/l3_certify.py`, `l3_contact.py`, `l3_gate.py`, `xemu_chain.py`, `xemu_route.py` and `xemu_minigames.py` at `f482ea041`, frozen (D1143). Each module is Modkit's code with its imports renamed, but for the level's reach model (`devkit/reach/models.reach_path`, Step 4.13d1's), the route graph's builder (`devkit/rando/derive.router`, Step 4.13c's), `contact.json`'s path and the scripts' `sys.path` lines; `l3_goal.load_router` keeps this checkout's routers in `routers-azurkit/` beside the L3 store's reach models, so it never deletes Modkit's own keys in `routers/`. The sitting kit's selftest gets back the chain's frame-rate check and the route pilot's pipe check, and its exec namespace's `xr` is the route pilot itself. The 27 `tests/test_reach_model.py` and 7 `tests/test_simulator.py` cases 4.13a and 4.13c left, the douse test's certification half and the verb references in `devkit/reach/README.md` and `devkit/xemu/README.md` come with it.
- **The xemu harness** (Step 4.14a2; D108, D990): `devkit/xemu/oracle.py`, Modkit's `scripts/xemu_oracle.py` ported line for line (`python -m devkit xemu oracle CMD`, `devkit/xemu/README.md` its reference): sandboxed clones of the owner's xemu fork on the oracle's fixtures, now found by `AZURIK_XEMU_FIXTURES` or the user data folder's `xemu-fixtures` link, admitted by the slots both harnesses share; its admission waits for AzurKit's suite lock as well as Modkit's. Its game-data reads run on AzurKit's core and facts, `replay` loads the simulator's L3 only when it runs, and the hold's reaper runs the module. `devkit/xemu/shim_ranges.py` reads from a build the data its shims write while the game runs (the player-model shims' and qol_alloc_race's copy gate's), which `boot-build`'s code check leaves out; on the same builds its copy-gate ranges equal Modkit's. `tests/test_xemu_harness.py` runs the harness's offline selftest (132 checks, marked `xemu`) and pins the copy gate's range on both releases; the demo recorder's 12 checks wait for its port.
- **The xemu sitting kit and the owner demos** (Step 4.14a3; D108, D603, D604, D990): Modkit's `scripts/xemu_sitting.py`, `scripts/sittings/` and `scripts/xemu_demo.py` ported line for line into `devkit/xemu/sitting.py`, `devkit/xemu/sittings/` (`model_kit.py`, the model-swap rows with the frame's cost and real-time rate, and the three example specs) and `devkit/xemu/demo.py` (`python -m devkit xemu sitting` and `demo`; `devkit/xemu/README.md` gains their references). A spec's and add-body's builds are AzurKit's own (`sdk.build`, through the MCP server's `pack_build_iso`, or `azurkit build`'s argv); a pack build's XBE equals Modkit's. The model rows read the swap's shim words, attack sets and jiggle rows from a build through AzurKit's ports, and a level's spots and spawns through its image reader and node graph, as Modkit's do on the same images. The harness's selftest gets back the demo recorder's 12 checks (145 checks), and `tests/test_xemu_harness.py` runs the kit's two selftests (25 and 15 checks, marked `xemu`); the chain's and the route pilot's checks come with the reach engine's xemu checks (Step 4.13d2).
- **The `azurik-xemu-oracle` skill** (Step 4.14a3; D951, D990): Modkit's sitting card and its seven reference files in `.claude/skills/azurik-xemu-oracle/`, their § and hazard numbers unchanged, pointed at devkit's commands (`python -m devkit xemu oracle`, `sitting`, `demo`, the model rows' module), AzurKit's paths and environment names (`$FX`, `$XEMU_FORK`, `$EV`) and each evidence cite put in words; its history (the fork-build adoptions' measurements, its old description) is `docs/archive/xemu-oracle-history.md`. AGENTS.md's table names it; only a seat that sits loads it.
- **The model verbs' first leafs** (Step 4.15a1; D950, D1168): `python -m devkit models model` holds Modkit's `azurik-mod model dress`, `inspect` and `export` (`--characters` too), the ones ModelKit's adapter calls first. `dress` dresses a variant, with `--recipe`'s fields laid over it, as a build does, through the swap's own dress, `tools.characters.dressed`, now taken out of the character plan (which calls it; no byte changes, G3's goldens unchanged), on the default packs' layers, and writes its `characters.xbr` and `index.xbr` only once the dress passed (D35), outside the checkout and the images' folders (D36, `devkit/models/image.py`). The shipped glTF export no longer imports names its port left out (Modkit's checkout guard and `REGIONS`), so it runs again; `devkit/models/README.md` is the reference, `tests/test_model_verbs.py` the tests.
- **The model verbs' body leafs** (Step 4.15a2; D950, D1168): `python -m devkit models model` gains Modkit's `check` (a build's checks on an edited glTF of a body, and the recipe row its `mesh` takes), `import` (an edited glTF with any topology, or its painted weights, through the writer; `--out` the edited `characters.xbr`, written whole), `clips` (a glTF's animations as a body's clips; `--out` the edited `characters.xbr` and `index.xbr`, written whole), `chains` (a body's hair and cloth chains and the spring rows a recipe needs) and `export --variant` (a variant's textures as its build recolours them, a PNG a material row, through the shipped `colour_preview`), each giving Modkit's output on the same inputs. `tests/test_model_authoring.py` holds Modkit's authoring engine tests (glTF export and clips, mesh edits and imports, every mesh op, the writer, LODs, paint and colours), ported and merged, and pins the leafs beside the engine they run; `tests/test_model_verbs.py` the chains test.
- **The model verbs' spring and rest leafs** (Step 4.15a3; D950, D1168): `python -m devkit models model` gains Modkit's `jiggle` (a dressed variant's real-time springs, X41's twin, over the physics scenarios and its clips, the acceptance targets judged, `--set` to try a spring value), `pose` (every bone's world each tick of its clips with those springs, as an `.npz` written whole) and `rest` (a variant's rest positions for other values of its mesh ops on its frozen mesh, a shape slider's ends for ModelKit), each giving Modkit's output byte for byte on the same inputs. `tests/test_model_verbs.py` holds Modkit's three tests of them, ported.
- **Headless Blender for the model verbs** (Step 4.15a4; D950, D1168): `python -m devkit models model preview` is Modkit's `azurik-mod model preview` (a picture a view of a body, posed or bound, its LOD K in LOD 0's frame), `devkit/models/blender/batch.py` its Blender batch (many frames of one body from one Blender, the body cached by its content, at most `$AZURIK_BLENDER_SLOTS` Blenders on the machine, the slot folder ModelKit shares) and `python -m devkit models blender-check` its check against the real Blender; the scripts that run inside Blender are Modkit's unchanged. An MP4 or preview inside the checkout or an image's folder is refused by devkit's output guard (D36). `devkit/models/README.md`'s Blender section is the reference, `tests/test_blender.py` the two stand-in Blender tests.
- **The model verbs' `edit`** (Step 4.15a5; D950, D1168): `python -m devkit models model edit` is Modkit's `azurik-mod model edit`, one verb from a body's export through Blender to a built image: each Blender save is read back in a pass (the mesh imported as the recipe's `mesh.gltf` with its ops after it, the actions as clips, `inspect`'s budgets on the body as its build leaves its LODs, `preview`'s pictures, a `log.jsonl` row and the work folder's `recipe.json`), headless with `--script` for agents and tests, the interactive mode refused under them; `--build` builds the last passing recipe with the sdk, its variant as the character's `variant_file`. `tests/test_blender.py` holds Modkit's two tests of it, the edit pass and the LOD preview with a pass's LOD rows, on a stand-in Blender.
- **The fx verb** (Step 4.15a6; D950, D1168): `python -m devkit models fx` holds Modkit's `azurik-mod fx` whole, its seven leafs `list`, `show`, `closure`, `texture`, `templates`, `plan` and `clone`, on the shipped engine's fx modules; each prints what Modkit's printed for the same image and inputs, and `texture`'s PNG and `clone`'s files are Modkit's byte for byte. What `texture` and `clone` write is kept outside the checkout and the images' folders (D36, `devkit/models/image.py`); `devkit/models/README.md` is the reference, `tests/test_model_verbs.py` the test.
- **The arm fit and the authors' docs** (Step 4.15a7; D950, D1168): `python -m devkit models weapon-fit` is Modkit's `scripts/weapon_fit.py`: per body or built-in variant swinging Garret's staff moves, the arm turn a clip that keeps the Axion staff and the hoe out of its chest, written into `weapons.json`'s `fits` with `--write`; garret4's own reference now holds the hoe on its carriers as his build does (Modkit's script stopped there), and townspeople/male_6 gives the shipped row. `devkit/models/README.md` gains Modkit's model how-to (the `model edit` session and its passes, an action as an attack, hair chains, how big a body may be at 64, 128 and 256 MiB, LODs, `weapon-fit`, the sculpt rounds), and `docs/VARIANTS.md` a field reference of every recipe field as AzurKit's loader checks it; AGENTS.md's Characters section points to both.
- **Modkit's last four skills, rewritten for AzurKit** (Step 4.16a2; D951, D1083, D1168): `.claude/skills/` gains `azurik-orchestration` (the four roles, the step shapes, the gate and the window seats share, prompts, traps, seat practice with the reach seats on `python -m devkit reach` and `sim l3-hold`), `azurik-change-control` (the change classes, the gate and green under D1170's one rerun and perf debt, landing with `scripts/land.py`, CI, a release as a tag on a commit on main, the protected paths and their update commands), `azurik-testing` (which tests a change runs, the markers pure, vanilla, europe, web, xemu and ghidra, the empty allow-list, one behavior-named test a fix, determinism, goldens and facts, the simulator's levels and blind spots, CI's tiers) and `azurik-dev-environment` (uv and the dependency groups, `bootstrap.py` and a lane's setup, where the images and user data live, the environment variables, devkit's areas, the MCP servers, the owner's environment rules), each from Modkit's with what only Modkit had (the Tk GUI, its ledger's paths, its CI, its suite rules) dropped. Modkit's `azurik-patching-and-shims` becomes AGENTS.md's "Patching and shims: what binds" under Packs (the Pentium III rule, the callee's contract, a config cell's consumer, a patch done when its behavior is seen, the HDD cache), pointing at `docs/SHIMS.md`. AGENTS.md's table names all seven skills; the builder role loads `azurik-xemu-oracle` here. `tests/test_agent_files.py`'s loader guard now also holds each skill's frontmatter to its folder's name and a description (`test_every_skill_loads_its_frontmatter_naming_its_folder_and_no_text_the_loader_runs_as_a_shell_command`).
- **An "In game" line after each build names the controls that build adds** (Step 4.18a; D1153): `sdk._ingame` reads the build's own layers into the report's `ingame`, in the modern pad's names, each only where the build has it: qol_save_warp's "LT + A, B, X, Y on a save platform: town and back", the speed toggle's "L3" where its tick's landing is among player_physics' changes (a toggle on a slider left at vanilla installs nothing and adds none), the roll's "RB" with the distance or speed the player moved (the roll is the game's own, so only then), and qol_photo_mode's "click both sticks". The page shows them in one line under the status (`#ingame`), hidden with none and whenever the build is forgotten or a new one starts, on the site and in the app alike. - **The randomizer's logic counts the save-point warp while its pack is on** (Step 4.18b; D1154, D1155; RANDOMIZER_PLAN R2.14): `Settings.save_warp`, off by default (a settings file's `save_warp = true`, share version 5's options byte, a plan's settings), gives the solver an edge needing nothing to town's save platform's node (`Temple`, `town_central`) from each of the shim's 41 rows that has one (36 rows on 34 nodes; d2's D2_1 and D2_2, f2's F1_2, f3's F3_3 and w1's w1_1 have none and get no edge), in the logic and physical graphs, so a node whose escape reaches a save platform is no trap once town gets back to the start. The return gets no edge: it lands on the platform its own ritual stored, which that closure already holds. The warp changes no flag forced on entry and no region. A build applying `qol_save_warp` plans with it counted, settings counting it in a build without the pack are refused by name, the spoiler says when it is counted and its traps reflect it, and the tracker and the verify read the plan's settings. Unticked, every plan is byte for byte as before (the goldens; the eighty plans' test). And the suite's own guards: no test passes whatever the code it checks does (a skip on a constant, an assert on `inspect.getsource` text, a test asserting nothing, only under an `if` or only a non-zero exit) and none is named after the pass that wrote it (`tests/test_contracts.py`); a test marked Europe alone stops the run, every test that starts a Ghidra server shares the `ghidra_sockets` worker (AZ-312; the interrupted-start test joins it), no test finds an image but through the fixtures, the shim tests write only their own temp folders (a read-only copy, an empty home) and a test's git ignores an exported GIT_DIR (`tests/test_tiers.py`); `devkit rando restack` runs at nice 15 or lower, only in a lane (`tests/test_dev_tools.py`).
- **What the simulator, the collision meshes and the movement checks did in Modkit, pinned** (Step 4.28c; D1168, D1171): Modkit's tests of lanes C, L1 and C4 of the test census, whose behaviour no AzurKit test, golden or facts entry pinned (a pack's goldens hold its bytes, not what its code does), ported lean: in `tests/test_simulator.py` L0's empty XBR pointer, the level graphs' world matrices against the game's own transform code, the memory packs' run placement above 64 and 128 MiB, the stream threads' fixes (the pump's retry, the node race, the copy thread's buffer and out-of-memory eviction), photo mode's two hooks and the speed toggle's click, each under L2; in `tests/test_art.py` the collision-mesh reader's layout faults, each mesh's one owner and a platform's placed faces; in `tests/test_rando_db.py` the prefetch list's cut level f7; in `tests/test_characters.py` a worn enemy's own size and borrowed timing under enemy_scaling; in `tests/test_movement_envelope.py` the walk slider on flat ground and a slope, and air control after a charged or zipline takeoff, under L2.
- **Modkit's last page, app and Live tests** (Step 4.28d; D1168, D1171): the test census's unpinned rows of the page and the app and of Live's links and follower, ported lean into blocks at the end of their files. `tests/test_hosts.py`: every Live link reads the same guest memory through shared RAM and QMP and never writes it; the fork's shared RAM waits through a boot, finds its page directory anywhere and follows a new xemu under its name; the endpoints' probe order, the automated xemus skipped, the gdbstub refused and the one fix named; a level's spots read only once its load has settled, another seed named; the follower's marks, reloads and training-room timelines (AZ-481, R1.41); a Live feed whose update fails ends saying why and closes its link; the app's engine process ends once the app that started it is killed (AZ-801). `tests/test_randomizer.py`: what the maps say each way needs, coloured by key or by the barrier its spell breaks, dashed one way, and an exit with no place keeping its need. `tests/test_site.py`: the Data tab's search picks by name in any case, its grid sorts numbers, then text, empty cells last both ways, and Reset table is one Undo step; New draws another seed, a batch of skips partly on clicks all on, all off and back, and an exit with no place sits at its map's right edge on every floor, named with its need.
- **The randomizer database's last test pins** (Step 4.28e; D1168, D1171): Modkit's planner tests whose behaviour no AzurKit test, golden or facts entry pinned, ported lean. `tests/test_rando_db.py`: the shipped database's sixteen owner-, xemu- and L3-confirmed facts as one table of reach queries (e5's earth-1 skip, f6's terraces, d1's elevator bounce, f3's keylock pit, a5's ring of fire walls, the gear rooms of e5 and e6, f4's lava river, d1's floor 6 and beam walkway, town's sewer and e6's lake, w3's centre top, stored smash and ice climb, the fire guardian's seal, e2's minecart, each progression minigame's requirement), the loader's refusals table on the fixture databases, an L3 kit mark loading only where the logic never collects the spot holding less (AZ-543), the vanilla placement beatable and its route replayed in order in every realm, R_phys's edges and skips, and a route mark a skip outdoes (AZ-725); `tests/test_reach_model.py`: the tour of every sphere in sphere order with its search hints, a max tour never stranded on frag_fire_1's ledge (AZ-789), and a ride against a rig's one-way mover kept out of logic (AZ-589). No code or data changed, so no seed moves.
- **The developer log's release join** (Step 4.28f; Modkit's Step 2.212): `scripts/devlog.py --release TITLE --remove` writes the `docs/devlog/` entries into `docs/DEVLOG.md` as one `## [TITLE]` section above the newest release (Keep a Changelog's sections in order, each one's files by name with numbers compared as numbers) and deletes their files; with no option it prints the join. Its `problems` (the lint set, `tests/test_contracts.py`) refuses a malformed entry file, an entry held twice (in two files, or already released) and a bullet under `[Unreleased]` the old way, and the join refuses on them too, nothing written. Modkit's code and tests, ported; on today's 82 entries Modkit's script and this one write the same log byte for byte.
- **Modkit's last unpinned randomizer rules, ported as tests** (Step 4.28f; D144, D275, D249, AZ-432, AZ-606, AZ-280, D35, D81, AZ-393, D356): `tests/test_randomizer.py` holds the settings' refusal of a toggle that turns on a held or manual skip and a skip batch standing for its variants (on devkit's toy database), the tracker table's sums by kind and level with nothing said of an unseen item without the spoiler (the golden plan's 203 items, as in Modkit), a need naming each way a spot is reached by (`needs.reaching`, the words Modkit's spot tips said), a run under garret4's 7 m/s planning the tier its sliders play as, never refused (AZ-606 under D1066), the writer touching no exit or spot string and refusing a placement it can't write, all at once, and a fractional obsidian cost taken; `tests/test_characters.py` a graded swapped body leaving the logic verified and a short one flagging its touch.
- **Modkit's retired-names guard and packaging pin, ported** (Step 4.28f): `tests/test_agent_files.py` keeps the names Modkit retired (xdvdfs, compile.sh, the old Ghidra env vars and verbs, the deleted docs, the pool flags and packs, the old game-data repo paths...) out of every tracked Markdown file but the archive and the developer log, outside a `## History` section, as AzurKit keeps porting docs and skills from Modkit; its first run found `docs/RE_DATA.md` linking Modkit's archived loading-zone audit, which AzurKit has no copy of (now said in words). `tests/test_wheel.py` pins the running Python among pyproject's classifiers.
- **Modkit's fx clone and texture tests, ported** (Step 4.28g; the test census's C3 rows, Modkit's test_xbr_clone and test_textures): each of the 9 effects templates grafted into the airship boss's fx clone reads as its donor records (barrier, stream and bolts, which no golden wears, among them); a graft leaving out what its set names, a parent after its child and an anchor on a non-transform are refused by name, and a recolour repoints its renderer's +0x18 alone; a `graph` edit points an effect at another key in place; every square texture (1,467) and animated list (77) of characters.xbr and fx.xbr rebuilds from its numbers. Each is its Modkit test with the imports renamed, the image read through `iso.Reader` and the damage table's columns through `Document`. Tests: the four at the end of `tests/test_characters.py`.
- **`azurkit save inspect` reads one .sav file** (Step 4.28g; Modkit's `save inspect` did): its kind, size, a text or binary save's version, a text save's lines and a signature's digest, as a slot's file shows; `save edit` on one refuses it by name, as the signature covers the whole slot.
- **`devkit models edit --from booblord` is held to leave her staff and notes out, as New from this is** (Step 4.30, Modkit's Step 2.209 review): the last half of Modkit's own-copy test, now that the edit verb is ported, joins `tests/test_character_weapons.py`'s.
- **The tracker's spots say what reaching them needs** (Step 4.30, Modkit's map tips, AZ-432): hovering a spot on a level's map shows, as its tip's second line, what reaching it needs under the default options (the reach from the level's arrivals with each way named, its own requirement and its darkness, then an obsidian lock, barrier, key lock, seal, keylord or guardian on it), the same for every seed. The maps read it from `src/azurkit/tools/randomizer/spot_needs.json`, which `scripts/spot_needs.py [--check]` writes from `needs.spot_needs` (a reach of every spot takes seconds); all 1,422 spots read as Modkit's tips did.

### Changed
- **Check with game data keeps its answer by content** (Step 4.6c round 2, D1015, D999): a Check of the edit it checked last, unchanged (`sdk._check_key`: the image, the edit and each file its recipe names, by their bytes, a glTF's with the buffers it reads, and the content installed), answers at once, and a dress worked out before is taken as kept: `characters.cache_key` now keys a recipe file's (`variant_file`) by what it dresses (`library.file_stamp`: its recipes but their title and notes, which no dress reads, and the files they name), never its path, so the editor's Check, whose hidden recipe file is new each call, keeps its size and plan in the app's store, and the site's worker offers and keeps a Check's dress in IndexedDB as `build()` does a build's. Yours as saved (or but for a title or notes typed since) dresses by name, as its Build does, so in the app a Check and a Build of it keep one dress (the site's worker keeps each under its own call's hint). Only a first Check of new content dresses (booblord's own copy: A92 and S78, a bar op); A93 and S79 time a Check again, unchanged. `tests/test_variant_editor.py` holds it.
- **A LOD's cut is compiled** (Step 4.6c round 4, D1009, D1167): `decimate.decimate`'s collapses (its offers, the link test, the cost, the max_error's rehome and the collapse, the cheapest first, ties by point) run in `native/decimate.c` on its setup's packing, every float operation the twin's in its order, so each LOD and shadow is the same bytes; a NaN, or a skin the twin refuses, is the twin's. Booblord's LODs were about 17 s of her first Check under the site's engine (Pyodide on Node). `tests/test_native.py` holds it, natively and under Pyodide.
- **A variant's paint is compiled** (Step 4.6c round 5, D1009, D1167): a texture's DXT blocks (`textures._dxt`), each texel's triangle and weights (`paint._raster`) and the hue's `fdlibm.atan2` over every texel (`paint._hsv`) run in `native/paint.c`, every operation the twin's in its order, so each painted texture is the same bytes; a UV corner not finite, past 2**40 or -0.0 is the twin's. `tests/test_native.py` holds them over seeded levels, points and triangles, natively and under Pyodide.
- **A round's rays are compiled** (Step 4.6c round 4, D1009, D1167): `mesh_edit._ray_at`, a round side's ray through a point (its form's exit, tapered or not, a drape's hull, the blend, the gate and the fades), runs in `native/mesh.c` on the side's numbers packed once (`_ray_row`), every operation the twin's in its order, so a mesh plan, its subdivision and a jiggle bone's shares are the same bytes; a division by zero, which the twin raises, is the twin's. About a sixth of booblord's first Check went there (660 thousand rays). `tests/test_native.py` holds it.
- **A spring's path is smoothed compiled** (Step 4.6c round 5, D1009, D1167): `jiggle._smooth` (a spring's path through its integer weights, a loop's last sample its first) runs in `native/dress.c`, every operation the twin's in its order, so the springs' clips are the same bytes; a loop of under 2 samples, or weights summing to 0, is the twin's. `tests/test_native.py` holds it, natively and under Pyodide.
- **A glTF clip's thinning is compiled** (Step 4.6c round 5, D1009, D1167): the keys `clip_import._thin_track` keeps (each sample's value and the Hermite run from the last kept key against the samples between) are worked out in `native/dress.c`, every float operation the twin's in its order, so each imported clip is the same bytes; a time not finite or not after the one before is the twin's. `tests/test_native.py` thins seeded tracks both ways, natively and under Pyodide.
- **A worn body's build holds each file of the image once** (Step 4.6c round 3, D1017, D1073): a build or Check with a character read the files its dress reads (`default.xbe`, config.xbr, characters.xbr, fx.xbr, index.xbr: 103 MiB on USA) a second time for its own layers; `sdk._planning` now takes them from the dress's `Vanilla`, the same bytes. The variant body the swap keeps between its size's dress and its build's (`_KEPT_BODY`) kept a copy of the staged characters.xbr it was made on even when that was the image's own; it now keeps the image's (64 MiB less held through the rest of the dress and between calls). The site's dress grows its heap that much less; nothing is slower. `tests/test_characters.py` holds both.
- **A character's dress lets go of its spare characters.xbr copies before its LODs and shadow** (Step 4.6c round 3, D1017): the swap's last stages held the springs' clips' file, X41's read of the built body and W41's lifted file (each about 72 MiB on booblord) beside the staged one until its end, through its LODs and shadow, where a dress peaks; each is let go once the staged file is it. The bytes a build writes are the same (the variant goldens hold them).
- **A turned clip's grid asks two keys a time** (Step 4.6c round 5, D1167): `retarget.turned_over` and `_sampled` keep a grid time 2 ms from every key by its distance from the two keys either side of it (`_off_keys`), the same float as the least over every key, so booblord's arms ask a few thousand distances, not 11 million, and her clips are the same bytes.
- **A glTF body's helper bones read each clip once** (Step 4.6c round 5, D1167): `gltf._helpers` reads every clip's scale tracks once for the bones whose scale moves, not once a bone (about 14 million steps of booblord's first Check under the site's engine), the same bones, so her clips' checks and exports are the same.
- **A mesh edit's rays are asked a side at once** (Step 4.6c round 5, D1167): the compiled ray kernel answers a round side's rays over every point of a share's run, and of the rounds a round at a time over all their points, in one call (`az_rays`), each kept for the plan to take as it asks it, so the mesh is the same bytes with a few thousand calls in place of about 600 thousand (a ctypes call under the site's Pyodide costs Firefox more than the ray itself).
- **A WAV's resample works out its weights once a phase** (Step 4.6c round 3, D978): `xbox_adpcm.read_wav`'s windowed sinc took 32 `fdlibm` sines and cosines a sample, the same weights again at every sample of a rate's few phases (44.1 kHz has one), 4.4 s of booblord's dress on this Mac. `_taps` keeps each phase's weights and their sum, added in frame order, so each sample is the one worked out before bit for bit, in a fortieth of the time; a Build or Check of a body with its own voices is that much sooner. `tests/test_characters.py` holds it.
- **The Characters tab's shipped no-image answer holds the Yours group** (Step 4.6c, after Step 4.6a): `web/characters/index.json`'s view now carries `sdk.pickers`' `custom` (empty: no variant of yours ships), re-recorded by `scripts/record_goldens.py characters`, so the page's first frame and the engine's answer agree; `tests/test_characters.py`'s shipped-files test holds it.
- **A spring's chain is posed by the compiled engine model** (Step 4.6c round 4, D1009, D1167): `jiggle.keyed` posed its parent's chain, and the clear its body, one clip time at a time in Python (86 thousand times for booblord); `jiggle._posed_at` asks `retarget`'s compiled `pose` and `origins` (`native/dress.c`) for every time at once on those bones alone, the same rotations and origins bit for bit. `tests/test_native.py` holds it.
- **The perf gate's Variants scenario opens its later visit as S02's does** (Step 4.6c round 3, D1017): `tests/_perf.py`'s `site_variants` imported three recipes on a page, closed it and opened the next beside it in the same browser, so WebKit kept the closed page's process for the site's service worker (Step 4.4m) and the capped site's peak held it: 1.5 GiB of S78's 7.1 GiB. Its later visit now opens the browser again on its profile (`Driver.reopen`), the library kept, as every later visit of the gate does.
- **A Check's dress keeps its parts by content** (Step 4.6n, D1167, the Check design's L1): the stages of a variant's dress whose output is a function of what they read alone (its body's W24 mesh, W29 rig and W30 paint as three parts, each keyed on the one before; the size its walk and roll are folded with; W28's arms' and springs' restyles; the LOD and shadow cuts and their writes) are kept in the process (`tools/characters/parts.py`) under a key of those reads, never the variant's name, folder or recipe path (which reach only its sounds and its log), and taken in place of the stage, so a second copy of a variant under any name (New from this) dresses only the glue: booblord's own copy checked again as another copy took 4.6 s in the app's engine after 55 s for the first, and 15.5 s after 137 s under the site's engine (Pyodide on Node); booblord herself took her copy's body, and a copy with a texture row dropped keeps its mesh and rig (39 s, 76 s when the body was one part). Each answer is a fresh Check's byte for byte (round 5's answers for the copy and every built-in; the second name's, a texture edit's and a spring edit's equal to their fresh dresses). A part's file is kept as the pages where it differs from the stage's input and its objects pickled, within `parts.ROOM` (256 MiB); `_KEPT_BODY` (one body, its whole file) is gone. `tests/test_variant_editor.py` holds a copy's Check, and a spring edit's, to a fresh one.
- **A Check's parts are kept across visits** (Step 4.6o, D1167, the Check design's L2): each part of a variant's dress that Step 4.6n keeps in the process (its body's mesh, rig and paint, its size, its restyles, its LOD cuts and writes; and now the glTF clips a recipe reads and its kit's clips, whose in-process memos they replace) is handed to the host's store as `tools/characters/part_codec.py` keeps it (no pickle: its values and the dataclasses of the modules a part holds by their fields, runs in columns, zstd; a format, a class's fields or a class no part holds refused by name), under its key and the code's digest, and a part the process doesn't hold is taken from there before it is worked out. The app keeps them in its signed `swap` store, written behind the answer (`app.swapcache.Parts`); the site in IndexedDB's `azurkit-parts` (`web/parts.mjs`), which the page offers a Check by its recipe's content (its name, folder, labels and sounds left out) and keeps after its answer, the last 8 recipes' parts (the Tools tab's Storage lists it and Clear removes it). Booblord's own copy's parts keep in 28.8 MiB, and a copy checked under another name at a later launch took 6.3 s in the app's engine (60.1 s for the first copy), and at a later visit 18.6 s under the site's engine (Pyodide on Node, 141.3 s for the first; heap 1382 MiB at its answer), the same answer and dress byte for byte. `tests/test_variant_editor.py` holds a later visit's Check to a fresh one.
- **A built-in's Check parts ship** (Step 4.6p, D1167, the Check design's L3): every part (`tools/characters/parts.py`) a Check of each built-in variant and of its own copy (New from this) works out at the page's defaults, on both releases, is recorded by `scripts/record_shipped.py` as a `part` row of `shipped.json` (its key, which already holds what its stage reads, its `part_codec.digest` and its file) and a file of `part_codec`'s bytes at zstd 19 in the entry's `variants/<entry>/shipped/parts/` in Azurik-Custom-Content (named for their sha256, so a re-record writes only the parts that moved), listed with the entry but left by Install for the Check that asks for one (`content.later`, mirrored in `web/content.mjs`), so Install and the site's page start carry none of their 117 MB. A part neither the process nor the host's store holds is read from the content folder (a clone's), else fetched from the source its installed entry came from (`shipped.FETCH`: the sdk's on CPython, the site worker's synchronous request), no further than its size and held to its sha256, and a fetched one is kept as the host keeps a part it made (the app's store, the site's IndexedDB), so a first Check of a built-in or its own copy under any name dresses only the glue and a later visit fetches nothing. No size part ships (a built-in's size is its `size` row) nor a refused Check's. A set's items are written in one order, so a part's digest is the same in every process, and the goldens' pin builds each built-in's own copy too and holds every part row to a fresh dress's part under its key (`shipped.OFF` takes none). The recorder's size and plan jobs dress with no part taken again, so booblord's parent places row is heard as before.
- **Check with game data answers first, then draws** (Step 4.6q, the Check design's L4): its answer (the log, the load against the build's lines, the bones and effects) holds no picture now, and the page asks `variant_pictures` as it lands, which draws the variant from the dress the Check kept (its base body drawn then, once a Check), in the app's engine or the site's worker that keeps the dress. The answer's values and the picture's bytes are the old Check's; booblord's own copy checked again as another copy answers in 4.8 s under the site's engine with its picture 2.0 s after (6.8 s together), and in 3.2 s in the app's engine with its picture 1.1 s after.
- **A Check's glue is cut** (Step 4.6q, D1167, the Check design's L4): with every part of a dress kept, what was left of a Check was the glue between them, and most of it now goes. A body's bounds are read as numpy columns (`characters.mesh_bounds`: each value the walk's, a zero's sign and a NaN too; a dress asked it of every clip it imports); its bones and LOD triangle counts are read without its vertices (`read_asset(vertices=False)`: the Check's bones, W39's and W36's counts around their kept cuts); B's bakes (`retarget.bake_kept`), each sound's ADPCM (`wave_entry`, by its samples) and each WAV's read (`read_wav`, by its bytes) are parts kept by content too; and the dressed file is named by its body's parts' key and each later one by its pages that differ from it (`parts.since`), so no stage hashes a 70 MB file the chain knows. Booblord's own copy checked as another copy after the first, its picture included: 6.8 s under the site's engine (Pyodide on Node; 20.8 s before on the same busy box) and 4.3 s in the app's engine (5.5 s). Every file a dress writes is the old dress's, byte for byte (her copy, booblord and beamlord, dressed fresh), and the wasm heap after the copy is 1763 MiB (1918 before).
- **The changed-area gate reads a dev area's data as its package's** (Step 4.11b): a file under `devkit/<area>/` that isn't Python or a doc (`devkit/re/data/x.toml`) now picks the test files reaching `devkit.<area>` or a module in it, which read it by `__file__`, and those reaching a module outside it that names its file (`bootstrap.py` naming a `tools.toml`), where it made the whole suite (no module named its folder) or picked every module holding the folder's name as a string. The lint files now hold all of `tests/test_agent_files.py`, not only its scan for a home path, so a docs-only change runs the agent files' checks too (the three lint files: 2.2 s). graph() already read `devkit/`, `shimkit/` and `nativekit/` (Step 4.4dx3): one table test pins all three.
- The L3 store's reach models are this checkout's (Step 4.13f, D1172, D1175): every current level's model in `x7/reach` was rebuilt with AzurKit's code and checked field for field against Modkit's build of the same model at its freeze (equal but for the seeded and exact envelope search AzurKit dropped and the tricks' rewritten notes), so `devkit/reach/models.py` drops Modkit's module table and refuses a pickle naming any module but this checkout's; `l3_goal.load_router` keeps its route graphs in `x7/reach/routers/` again, one folder; the 21 proof records whose start snapshots are gone are `retired` in `proofs.jsonl` (history: their marks still resolve, the database's checks, `db_digest` and every plan are as they were) and `l3-certify recheck` names and passes over a retired record instead of stopping on it.
- **The roll's lock reason names RB** (Step 4.18a; D1153): `layer.LOCKS`' reason for player_physics roll_time_scale at 0 or less says "the first RB press holds the player in it", the modern pad's name for the original's BLACK. Test: `test_moved_movement_sliders_plan_the_nearest_tiers_seed_by_the_physics_table_and_no_moved_slider_is_promised` reads it.
