- **Each visit starts at the defaults unless Remember settings is ticked** (Step 4.19a; D1156, amending D915): a box in the bottom bar after Reset to defaults, off by default, its title "Keep your packs, settings, randomizer and character for your next visit". Ticked, the page keeps and restores its setup as D915 did (a link or a loaded recipe still takes its place); unticked, it keeps none and forgets the kept one at once. The choice is kept (the site's `azurkit.remember`, listed and cleared in the Tools tab's storage; the app's config `remember`, sent with each `api/setup` `{"remember", "setup"}`, `host.json` giving the setup only while it is true), and Reset to defaults leaves it as it is, naming a forgotten setup only when one was kept. A setup kept by 1.0.x, with no choice, is removed unread on the first visit (the site's load, the app's launch). The theme, the tracker's plan and marks, Content from, Show objects, installed content and the caches stay kept whatever the box says. The perf gate's A02 and A31/A32 tick the box at their first launch and A45's config holds `remember`, so each times the restore it timed. Tests: `test_a_revisit_starts_at_the_defaults_with_remember_settings_unticked`, `test_a_revisit_restores_the_setup_with_remember_settings_ticked_until_reset_and_names_what_it_drops` (the site), `test_the_apps_relaunch_starts_at_the_defaults_with_remember_settings_unticked`, `test_the_app_gives_the_next_page_its_setup_only_with_remember_settings_ticked` (the app); the tests that restored a setup across a visit tick the box first.
- **Seed difficulty decides where the key items go** (Step 4.20; D1157, amending D1031, D1037, D1039; D1038's optional lean kept): four levels, each offered at any settings: Easy, Normal (today's seeds and the default, byte for byte), Hard and Expert, in place of Any/Standard/Hard/Frame-perfect's promises. Each key item takes its spot by a lean on three integer measures: its round (the progression reach's, dense-ranked), the rungs of the settings' ladder that miss it at that point (so tricks follow from placement) and its detour on its level's node graph (side rooms, dead ends, far ends); Easy the lowest, Hard weighted, Expert the highest, strict for attempts 0-9, weighted for 10-19 and a level's 20 more drawing as today (a strict lean repeats a placement the verify may refuse); the obsidians lean by detour. Easy keeps the lowest score of 3 passing attempts and of Normal's own plan for the seed (`fill._Run.normal_plan`, last, so it is kept only where it scores below all 3 and no Easy plan scores above Normal's), Expert the highest of 6. The compiled kernel (`native/fill.c`, `FILL_VERSION` 3: `A_LEAN`, `A_PAST`, `L_DETOUR`) and its twin agree bit for bit; `SEED_DIFFICULTY_FILL` 2. Every plan's spoiler and tracker show a measured line, e.g. "Expert: 19 key-item steps, 20 off the path, needs Power storage twice, Roll jumps once and Air flutter once" (the tracker's `head.difficulty`, the tricks named with Spoiler on); settings files name the level; v1.0.0's levels load at the nearest one (Standard as Hard, Hard and Frame-perfect as Expert) and the tab says so. Over seeds 0-29 at six Movement x Skips cells, the medians of key-item steps and key items off the path rise Easy < Normal < Hard <= Expert at every cell (Hard/Hard: 12/17/18/20 and 11/15/17/19), a Hard trick is needed in 0/4/16/30 seeds, no fill fails and every Normal plan is main's. With the elemental powers kept vanilla, Easy's early fragments opened a realm before the kept power that gets the player back out, and the trap gate's repair had no key item after them to swap, so Easy planned as Normal; the repair now moves the realm's opener to a free spot from the kept power's sphere on (`P_REPAIR_PAST`), and in an Easy attempt (D1160: Easy easier in every seed) takes no key item beside the kept power for a way out and, where one way-in item alone opens the traps, the earliest and closest spot first: none a rung misses, in a sphere that already collects a key item (else it adds a key-item step), then by sphere + detour (`fill._earliest`, `P_REPAIR_EARLIEST`; drawn at random among every later free spot, Easy's chain came out deeper than Normal's in 148 of 1,400 seeds). Over seeds 0-99 at 14 cells (Safe, Standard and Hard movement, skips up to Frame-perfect, nothing, the powers or the powers and upgrades kept), Easy's score is at or below Normal's in every seed (below in 1,386; Normal's own plan kept in 7, where its draw put the key items in fewer spheres or fewer off the path than any of Easy's 3; every other Easy plan from its lean) and its key-item steps in 1,372 (before the repair: 1,252); with the powers kept, median key-item steps, off the path and score 21-23/4-5.5/27 against Normal's 23-25/8/32-33. Making Normal's plan too costs Easy's fill 15-41 % more kernel reaches (median by cell), about 0.08 s a seed. Tests: `test_easy_with_the_elemental_powers_kept_plans_from_its_lean_no_deeper_than_normal_and_below_its_score_with_no_trick`, `test_easy_keeps_normals_own_plan_only_where_it_scores_below_each_of_easys_so_no_easy_plan_scores_above_normals`, `test_seed_difficulty_levels_lean_chains_off_path_and_tricks_in_order`, `test_seed_difficulty_rides_strings_and_files_and_v1_levels_load_at_the_nearest`, the site's `test_seed_difficulty_offers_every_level_at_any_settings_loads_v1_levels_at_the_nearest_and_the_tracker_shows_the_line`.
- **The Models view's groups are drop-downs** (Step 4.21, D1169, D1061; the owner: "can you make the custom content models be under a drop down?", then "I want yours to have a drop down too"): Species, Community (its Install and Update buttons, Content from and its note) and Yours each fold under a `<details>` of the page's own style (`.fold`, which the Randomizer tab's Specific skips now share), in the same order; Species is open and the other two shut at every visit, the state never kept (D1156). Each summary counts its models (how many of all while the search narrows them) and names the chosen one when it is in that group, and the group opens when its model becomes the chosen one (a pick, a link, a recipe, the remembered setup), once: shut again, it stays shut. A failing source opens Community to show its line. The search now reaches every group (an entry by its title, a variant by its name or title, an entry with no match hidden) and opens a shut group it finds models in; cleared, it shuts those again, but the chosen model's. The species legend drops its colour words to fit the summary (the marks say each grade). `tests/test_site.py` holds it on the shared page.
- **Nothing small blocks the workflow** (Step 4.22, D1170): every pytest run, the gate's, its flake stage's and CI's, reruns a failed test once at once (`tests/_rerun.py`, loaded by `tests/conftest.py`; failed fixtures are set up again). A test that passes its rerun is flaky: the run passes, the summary names it with its first try's error, `scripts/fullsuite.py` keeps it in the receipt's `flakes` and appends it to `flakes.jsonl` beside the receipts, and each CI job lists it in its summary (`AZURKIT_FLAKES`, `python -m tests._rerun`); a test that fails twice fails the run as before, and a rerun is recorded, never a skip. The perf gate's timings are advisory: an op over its budget or ceiling, past 1 s with no progress, or whose page froze in a gap (now the sample's `froze` mark, not an error) is a perf-debt row (the block's `debt`, printed and appended to `perf-debt.jsonl`; a Save's row carries the free disk), and the block fails only on an op whose median is over its budget and over main's last passing full run's (`fullsuite.main_reference`, `perf.py --reference`; main's own full run takes none, so each that passes re-baselines) by more than 25 % and 0.5 s (`perf.REGRESSION`, `REGRESSION_S`), re-run three more times first, and on memory peaks, errors, native failures and a growing list as before; only such a candidate takes more samples. `scripts/land.py` drops `--release`: landing never waits for a release, a tag on a commit that is on main.
- **No tracked text points into Modkit** (Step 4.24d; D1171, the census's E31): the RE docs, `docs/SHIMS.md`, the game-reference skill, devkit's RE data and docstrings and Live's link name AzurKit's paths, `python -m devkit` verbs and docs where they named Modkit's `azurik_mod/` paths, `azurik-mod` verbs or Modkit-only docs (TOOLS, PATCHES, SCRIPTS, RANDOMIZER, Finished/), each RE doc's header saying what a Modkit name left as provenance means; `docs/SHIMS.md` is rewritten for AzurKit's engine (prebuilt objects, the facts' functions and kernel, the carve allocator's claims, `new-shim` to xemu). `tests/test_agent_files.py` holds every relative link of a tracked Markdown file to a tracked file and refuses text that names Modkit's checkout, links into its package or imports it.
- **AGENTS.md says the Modkit port is done** (Step 4.24d; D1168, D1171, D1030, the census's D43): its knowledge table's Modkit row names the private archived repository that holds Modkit's history instead of a checkout to read, and § Porting from Modkit drops the hand-port procedure (its git commands run in Modkit's checkout) for the record of each cut, Modkit's module map and where its history is.
- **A build never writes beside a vanilla image named `usa.iso` or `europe.iso` or found, nor in an `iso` folder holding one** (Step 4.28a; Modkit's D36 folder rules, §6a.5, MCP-1 bug 4, AZ-178, X.50): `sdk.build` (before anything is read) and `sdk.save`, the command line's and the app's, refused only the input and a found vanilla image itself; they now refuse, by name, an output directly in the folder of a vanilla image found (`AZURKIT_ISO`, the user data folder's) or of an input named `usa.iso` or `europe.iso` (any case), or in the folder either's link leads to, and anything inside such a folder named `iso`, its subfolders and an output link in it too, however the path is spelled (`gate.require_new_output`, Modkit's `require_new_output`, which `devkit/rando/ports.py` now re-exports in place of its copy). Extract keeps its own rule (never over the image or a vanilla image). `tests/test_edits.py` pins it (Modkit's four test_iso_identity tests, merged).
- **A retired pack name says what replaced it** (Step 4.28a; Modkit's D43 table): `qol_skip_save_signature` (`azurkit save edit` signs an edited save again) and the randomizer's old `rand_major`, `rand_keys`, `rand_gems`, `rand_barriers` and `rand_connections` (its settings choose what is shuffled) are refused by name with what replaced them, by `mods.normalize` (every host's check of a recipe, a mods file's or a link's packs) and `azurkit build --pack` (`mods.RETIRED`, `mods.unknown`); an unknown name is refused as before. `tests/test_packs.py` pins it (Modkit's test_legacy_pack_migration).
- **The perf gate judges against the last release, and an op it never measured only owes debt** (Step 4.29, D1170): `scripts/fullsuite.py`'s `release_reference` hands `perf.py --reference` the last release's run (the newest receipt with verdicts on the newest `v*` tag's commit the stack holds, a run of every op first, else the release's before) in place of main's last passing full run, for every perf stage, main's full runs too; an op that run never measured that errs is a perf-debt row with its error as its reason, and only a regression against that run (an op slower than there, one it measured that errs, or an error row an older `perf.py` wrote with no reference), a memory peak, a native failure or a growing list blocks (`perf.blocking`), which the receipt's status, its summary and `scripts/land.py` read from the block's verdicts.

### Removed
- **Seed difficulty's forcing table** (Step 4.20, D1157): `data/forcing.json`, `scripts/seed_forcing.py` and what only they used (`difficulty.asked`, `Ask`, `Shape`, the promises and their grey lines, `fill._Pin` and `fill.c`'s pin), and the refusal of a level above the settings' top: a level now leans placement, no promise to rate. What pinned their tests' behaviour: the levels' order and plans (`test_seed_difficulty_levels_lean_chains_off_path_and_tricks_in_order`), every level valid at any settings (the strings-and-files test and the site's), the kernel's lean against its twin (`tests/test_native.py`'s spread).
- **Functions nothing named are gone, and a guard keeps it so** (Step 4.30, D10, Modkit's AZ-105): `tests/test_contracts.py` holds every module-level function of `src/`, `devkit/`, `shimkit/` and `nativekit/` to a name somewhere in the tracked tree (a Python name, attribute, import or string, or a call in a Markdown file); on the port's whole stack it found the gallery picker's `draw_previews` with its picture writers (both drawing copies' `body_preview`, `thumbnail`, `rimmed`), the colour preview's `portrait`, `layer.build_sliders` (the generator's `sliders` replaced it), the Tk tracker's Live hover lines (`setup_lines`, `automation_line`, `without_gdb`) and the build re-check's `physics.tier_gap` (with its `Margin` protocol, folded into `Margins`, and `travel_plain`), all deleted. The simulator's `boot_tick` keeps its Modkit test (the logo state, and `qol_skip_logo` starting prophecy.bik in its place) in `tests/test_simulator.py`.
- **The unreachable drop-in WAV folder for a variant's sounds is gone** (Step 4.30, D1184): the swap's `sound_files` and `_player_variants.drop_ins`/`variant_sounds`, which no host passed, are deleted; a variant's own WAVs are its recipe's `wavs` (the Variants editor's Add WAV), read by `recipe_wavs`. Two refusals that named the old `--player-variant-sounds` flag now name the recipe's voice, sfx and wavs.

### Fixed
- **An app engine that ends mid-call says how it ended** (Step 4.5o): once in the capped perf run (4 cores, 8 GiB) the reshaped booblord's build (A31) ended "AzurKit's engine stopped (out of memory?): try again" (`src/azurkit/app/engine.py`: its process ended before it answered, and no cancel had killed it), with no crash report and normal memory pressure; the engine's reader recorded neither its exit code nor its signal, so nothing told an out-of-memory kill from a crash, an exit or another program's kill. It was the last: another program on the machine ran `pkill -f "multiprocessing.spawn"` to clean up its own pool, which sends SIGTERM to every Python multiprocessing child, the engine and its pool's included, as that program's own log names their ids (the engine's tree peaks at about 4.7 GiB there, of the 6 GiB the capped profile allows; a build of that recipe under lldb ends cleanly). Now the reader waits up to 5 s for the process's exit status before telling its calls, the call's error says it ("AzurKit's engine stopped by signal 15 (SIGTERM): try again"; a SIGKILL, how an OS out of memory ends a process, adds "out of memory?"; else "exited with code N"), and the server's stderr names the call and the process (`azurkit: image: the engine's process (N) stopped by signal 15 (SIGTERM)`). Ten capped passes of A31 and A32 ran clean; an eleventh whose engine and pool got SIGTERM mid-A31 failed with that message. `test_an_engine_that_ends_mid_call_says_how_it_ended` kills a spawned engine mid-plan from outside.
- **The service worker's site test gives each load of the site's page the bound the site's tests give it** (Step 4.5w, tests only): on GitHub's Windows runner `test_the_sites_page_keeps_its_release_behind_a_first_visit_and_later_pages_run_isolated_on_it_offline_too[firefox]` failed its first visit's load in `_page.ACT` (30 s; run 42494981621), the page's state saying its engine had started and its one request unanswered `GET /worker.mjs`, heard 0 s before the failure. Not the test's host (`tests/_static.py`): Playwright's Firefox says a request as Firefox is about to send it (Juggler's request event comes from the channel's interception check, before the connection; here the event came 0-19 ms before the host heard the request, and on time with its answer held 3 s), so Firefox began the engine's first import only as the failure was made, about 37 s after the page's process started, and the failure's reads of the page waited with it, as at 4.5l's Windows stall (`GET /`, its page's process also 37 s old), which 4.5l's no-proxy prefs, in this run, didn't cover. Why Windows' Firefox held them is not shown; here the test passed 20 of 20 in Firefox, its loads 0.04-0.29 s. `tests/test_service_worker.py`'s `visited()` loads each of the test's three site pages in `VISIT`, `_page.STALL`, as `test_site.Site.open` loads the same page (a page that starts its engine), then waits for its engine as before; the test's claims are unchanged. `AGENTS.md` says the request event's timing beside 4.5l's proxy bullet.
- **A Save the browser refuses to keep says so** (Step 4.6c round 2): the site's Variants view no longer drops IndexedDB's refusal (a quota, a Clear meanwhile) when it keeps your variant after a Save, Save as, New from this or Import: the status line says it was saved but this browser didn't keep it, and to Export it to keep a copy (a Delete likewise, that it may be listed again next visit). `tests/test_site.py`'s Variants test saves after the Tools tab's Clear.
- **A shape's quick preview follows a Check made under another name** (Step 4.6d): `sdk.variant_preview` took the kept Check only for an edit of the same name and folder, so a built-in checked by its name (booblord) and then dragged, an edit of it under the working name, asked for the Check again; the kept Check now stands for any edit over the same image and packs whose mesh is the same but for its shapes (`sdk._place`). `tests/test_variant_editor.py`'s quick-preview test asks it under another name.
- **The Shape tab's held pictures are of the recipe as it is** (Step 4.6d; Modkit's `_base_key`): they were kept by shape values alone, so after a JSON or Body edit a drag back to a checked value showed the old Check's pictures "as it builds", or a let-go quick-previewed the old dress; each is now kept with the recipe but its shapes' values (title and notes left out), and a let-go checks again unless the last Check dressed the recipe but its shapes' values and colours. `tests/test_hosts.py`'s Shape tab test holds it.
- **The Variants view's first open opens a variant when the community list comes in while it lists** (Step 4.6d): a list a newer one overtook returned before either was drawn, so the first open found no list and opened nothing (a second page waited on its saved rows); it now ends with the newest list. `tests/test_site.py`'s Variants save test holds it.
- **A plan file whose settings aren't a table or whose db_digest isn't a string is refused by name** (Step 4.6j): `planner.plan.load_plan` checked a plan's keys but not their types, so one with its sha256 made again (anyone can) and `settings` a list or `db_digest` a number crashed `azurkit build --plan`, the Randomizer tab's Open a plan…, `azurkit spoiler` and the tracker with an AttributeError or TypeError. It now refuses one whose share, db_digest, settings or placement is typed otherwise ("not a plan file: …") before reading any of them; `layer.checked_plan`'s own share and placement guard moved into it. Test: two rows in `test_a_plan_reads_back_into_its_spoiler_and_what_the_randomizer_cant_take_is_refused_by_name`.
- **Check with game data takes a variant whose dress leaves a file as the disc has it** (Step 4.6m): a New on flicken stopped with `KeyError: 'gamedata/characters.xbr'`, as `characters.worn_by` read only the files a layer writes (`compose`'s); a file no layer writes is now the image's own, as Modkit's Check reads it. `tests/test_variant_editor.py` checks a New on flicken.
- **New from this on booblord_high Checks and builds** (Step 4.6r): its own copy refused ("idles.glb: animation 'bust_bounce': bone glute_right at t=0.000 sits 4.2e-03 off the file's pose"), since a copy has no `from`, so its springs were placed on its own finer mesh rather than where the built-in's build puts them (booblord's), and her idles' keys no longer met their rest. The clip check's tolerance (1e-4, Modkit's) was never the cause, and Modkit's own copy drops `from` the same way. An own copy of a recipe whose build places its springs by the one it is from now keeps that one's mesh and jiggle as a recipe field, `places` (`{"of", "mesh", "jiggle"}`, `_player_variants.placing`), so its springs sit where the built-in's do, its dress takes the built-in's body parts and shipped places under one key, and the goldens' pin builds every built-in's own copy with no refusal excused. A `places` on a recipe with no jiggle is refused by name.
- **A reshape's shipped Check parts are taken** (Step 4.6r): booblord_high's and booblord_high_human's body parts (W29's springs and all that stands on them) were keyed on where their parent's places came from, so the parts `scripts/record_shipped.py` made fresh never matched a Check's, which takes the shipped places row, and their first Check worked those stages out again (213 s native for booblord_high with every part shipped). The key now holds the parent's specs alone (the row is a fresh dress's places, which the goldens' pin holds), the shipped answers are re-recorded with booblord_high's own copy's parts beside them, and booblord's content entry takes them at version 7.
- **The Randomizer tab's Obsidians row says what shuffling them keeps** (Step 4.19b): "All 100 stay collectable, so every temple lock can open", in place of "Each lock stays reachable", which read as if obsidians were keys.
- **With the elemental powers kept, a seed whose every attempt met the trap gate plans** (Step 4.20; D1160: every seed plans): at Hard movement and skips with the elemental powers kept vanilla, Normal found no plan (D35) for 5 seeds of "0".."499" (52, 137, 216, 335, 350) and for 9 with the upgrades kept too: each of its 20 attempts opened a realm (life or air) before the kept power that gets the player back out, and the trap gate's repair, a swap with a key item collected just before the new traps free, had none to swap (the way out is the kept power). Normal now has 20 more attempts whose repair, as each level's attempts', moves the realm's opener to a free spot from the kept power's sphere on (`fill._Run.past_at`; `native/fill.c`'s call word `A_PAST`, `FILL_VERSION` 3): over "0".."499" at six cells (Hard/Hard with nothing, the fragments, the powers or the powers and upgrades kept, Standard/no skips and Frame-perfect/Frame-perfect with the powers kept) every level plans every seed (12,000 plans, each passing the verify; before, those 14 refused), the 14 from attempts 20 and 21, and Normal's plans of the 2,986 seeds today's attempts planned are main's byte for byte. Test: `test_with_the_elemental_powers_kept_each_level_plans_a_seed_whose_attempts_of_today_all_meet_the_trap_gate`.
- **Remember settings keeps no Randomizer tab for the save warp alone** (Step 4.25; D1155, D1156): with the qol_save_warp pack ticked and the tab otherwise at its defaults, the remembered setup stored the tab, and its drawn seed, as changed, since the settings' `save_warp` follows the pack's tick; it is the pack's, which the setup's ticked packs keep. `tests/test_hosts.py`'s frozen-app test, which ticks every pack, holds the setup to its packs again.
- **About's credits name the Unicorn patch** (Step 4.25): NOTICE.md's entry for the reach engine's patched Unicorn (Step 4.13d1), a developer tool that never ships, is in the About tab's credits too, as `tests/test_site.py` holds every NOTICE.md entry to be.
- **Booblord's spins keep their sittings' grades** (Step 4.27d): the seven grades of the Booblord spin pick (on town_male_6, garret4, the catalisk and her four looks) were keyed by her recipe before the vase fix, so each read "not seen in game" in the Weapon box and a build's status line; they are keyed by her recipe now, as the vase fix carried her looks' own grades.
- **A golden is written whole, then renamed** (Step 4.28a; Modkit's Step X.81e): `scripts/record_goldens.py` wrote each golden in place, so a re-record that stopped partway (a full disk) left half a file; it now writes a part file beside it and renames it into place (`record_goldens.write`), leaving the old golden and no part file when it stops. `tests/test_packs.py` pins it (Modkit's test_goldens).
- **A mods file that isn't JSON is named, with its line** (Step 4.28a; Modkit's AZ-030): `sdk.read_mods` said where a mods file's or a link's JSON breaks only as a character offset (`not JSON (... at 12)`); it now says the line and column, and `azurkit build --mods` names the file in each refusal of it (`... (in mod.json)`), before the image is read, writing nothing. `tests/test_edits.py` pins it (Modkit's test_edit_layers).
- **A shim that can't read its parameters fails its site** (Step 4.28a; Modkit's AZ-172): a trampoline declaring `floats` or `tables` on a shim whose `.text` has no relocations (its code reads no slot, whatever its other sections hold, as qol_skip_logo's) landed with the parameters written nowhere they are read; the engine now fails the site, naming why, so no layer is made. `tests/test_shims.py` pins it with the short site and the XBE with no `.text` (Modkit's test_float_param_injection and test_trampoline_patch).
- **`azurkit` takes a flag only as spelled** (Step 4.28a; Modkit's Q100): argparse took any unique prefix of a flag, so `azurkit build --no-def` was read as `--no-defaults`; the command and each verb, an action's too (`entity diff`), now refuse a flag typed short, exit 2 naming it, and nothing starts. `tests/test_packs.py` pins it (Modkit's test_cli_dispatch).
- **`azurkit` and `python -m devkit` no longer fail on a console that has no character for what they print** (Step 4.28b; Modkit's AZ-190): Windows writes a pipe or a file in its ANSI code page (cp1252), so `azurkit save inspect` of a slot whose text holds a byte that page has no character for (0x81, read as Latin-1), or `devkit audio dump` (its `→`), ended in a codec error, exit 1; both print `?` in its place and go on. Test: `test_the_commands_output_replaces_what_the_consoles_code_page_cant_encode`.
- **The audio dump scores a constant blob's entropy 0.0, as Modkit's did** (Step 4.28b): its port negated the sum, so a blob of one byte value had entropy -0.0, which `manifest.json` printed. Test: `test_the_dump_scores_entropy_refuses_an_implausible_header_and_previews_each_raw_blob_once`.
- **Every text read and write in the code names its encoding** (Step 4.28b; Modkit's AZ-191): the app's memory-peak read on Linux and the gate's and the web tools' lock files opened text in the locale's encoding (cp1252 on Windows); each names UTF-8, and `test_every_text_read_and_write_names_its_encoding` holds the package, devkit, shimkit, nativekit, the scripts, the icon's renderer and bootstrap to it.
- **An empty seed is refused by name** (Step 4.28d; Modkit's N9, Q74): `azurkit plan` and `azurkit build --seed`, and the API's plan and build, took a seed of no text, or only spaces, and planned it as a seed of its own; they refuse it, naming the seed and how to give one, before any image is read, as the page's Build and Track this seed already did. A share link or mods file holding one still loads, as v1.0.0's did; its build refuses the seed. `tests/test_randomizer.py` holds it.
- **A jump or flap height past a double's range notes its float32's infinity** (Step 4.28f; AZ-171, Modkit's L14): `player_physics` squares the jump's and the flap's scale (g x k x k), so a scale of 1e200 makes a product that is already infinite before it is packed, and `engine.saturated` noted only a finite value: the build wrote float32's +inf with no Q110 note. It notes any value but NaN that becomes an infinity (jump 1e20, swim 1e39, jump and flap 1e200: `tests/test_packs.py`, Modkit's test ported).
- **The MCP server's `pack_build_iso` refuses a build with nothing in it** (Step 4.28f; Modkit's Step MCP-1 bug 14, D528): with every pack on by default disabled, none enabled and no `config_mod`, it wrote an unchanged image; it fails, naming why, with nothing written, as Modkit's did, while a `config_mod` alone builds (config.xbr and filelist.txt). `tests/test_mcp.py` ports Modkit's last three unpinned MCP tests: that one, `pack_list`'s advisory ranges and cells on every slider, and `xbr_edit` taking the mods file's operations and writing nothing for an unmet `expected` or a bool value.
- **A save edit never writes the new slot inside the one it read** (Step 4.28g; Modkit's AZ-141): `azurkit save edit SLOT --out SLOT/out`, or the app's Save into a folder inside the slot it opened, wrote the new slot there, a folder the game's signature then covered, so the original no longer loaded. The Saves session keeps the folder each slot was opened from and refuses an output inside it by name, and the CLI opens a slot's folder through it. Modkit's other save edges are pinned with it: a non-ASCII SaveMeta name, a text part ending in loc.sav's binary tail with no newline (AZ-339) and 8-bit text (H9) round-trip, a .sav under 8 B is a file, a signature.sav not of 20 B no signature, a key not of 16 B is refused, a folder named like a save stays a folder (L25), a slot signed with no edit is signed as it is (Step 2.84), and each bad `save edit` input is one line, exit 1, nothing written. Tests: `test_a_save_slot_decodes_edits_and_re_signs`, `test_save_reads_a_lone_save_file_signs_a_slot_with_no_edit_and_refuses_bad_input_in_one_line_writing_nothing`.
- **devkit's XBR editor refuses a string offset outside the file** (Step 4.28g; Modkit's L34): `XbrEditor.replace_string_at` took a negative offset as one from the file's end and grew the file; it now refuses any offset outside it by name, as `replace_bytes` did. Modkit's other XBR edges are pinned with it (the test census's C2 rows): `xbr diff` of a missing file and `xbr edit --replace-bytes` at a negative offset are one line, exit 1; the string scan's NUL, length and letter rules; the levels' TOC invariants and known strings (a1, w1, town, hourglass, fx) and town's scan under 2 s of CPU; a shrunk config string taking its old text back with the next one untouched (L33); and a Modkit mod's unknown op, a file no release has, an offset, value or column of the wrong type (AZ-180) refused by name, its `filename` key read as `file`. Tests: `test_devkit_xbr.py`, `test_a_cell_edit_writes_only_its_cell_and_refuses_by_name`, `test_a_modkit_mod_file_reads_as_its_xbr_operations_in_modkits_order_and_each_refusal_names_its_edit`.
- **A Shape slider dragged while the Check still draws its pictures shows the nearest shape** (Step 4.29): the Check's pictures landing after a drag had moved the shapes' values were drawn as built (unmarked, "as it builds") and the drag's Preview was lost until the slider was let go; now the Shape tab holds them and shows the nearest shape again, marked Preview. The perf gate's drag rows (A96, S82) waited 600 s on it and stopped their Variants passes (A87, A95, S73, S81 with them).
- **A shim's kernel call whose resolver lies past a rel32's reach is refused by name** (Step 4.30, Modkit's D1-extend): the resolving stub's call of the shared resolver is refused naming the stub and the resolver, where it raised Python's bare packing error.
- **The perf gate's rerun of a slower op is judged on all its samples** (Step 4.31): a gate run of `scripts/perf.py` (no `--keep`) forgot an op's first samples when an op slower than the release's ran again, so it took its whole count anew (6 of a short op, not 3 more) and judged only the new ones; an op past 30 s was judged on its rerun's one sample alone (stack 2's A31 and A60, `n 1`). Each run's samples now stay in the run, so the rerun takes only its R more (one past 30 s) and the verdict reads them all.

## [1.0.1 (November 7, 2026)]

### Changed
- **A seed's fill runs each attempt in the compiled kernel** (Step 4.4ci57; D1141): `native/fill.c` runs a whole attempt of the fill, its twin and reference `fill._Slots.attempt` line for line (the progression items with their own spots, trades and the seed difficulty's pin over the ladder's rungs, the counted items by threshold band, the trap gate's repairs with their spheres and swaps, the useful items, the filler and the verify, on CPython's `random.Random` streams, MT19937 seeded as CPython seeds it), and a shaped candidate's need and play (`difficulty.need`, `play`: what `Shape.why_not` and `score` read, through `Shape.kernel`), on the reach tables and a blob `fill._Kernel` packs once a fill, its reaches reach.c's on the call's own workspace. The call leaves the GIL, so the app's attempts beside each other (`fill.WIDTH`, 4) run on their own cores, and the site's worker runs them in the side module. The notes the twin tells are logged and told after each call in its order (the bar's every percent; an attempt's cancel point is its end). Tables only the reach's twin reads, no library or `AZURKIT_NATIVE=0` run the twin. The same plans (A71's sha256[:12] 8879c01e633e, A80's e22785ac6108, at widths 1 and 4). In-process on this Mac at a normal-priority load of about 22, width 4: A71's plan 1.07-1.12 -> 0.24 s (1.63 -> 0.75 CPU-s), A80's 0.53-0.56 -> 0.13 s. Tests: `test_the_fills_attempt_and_acceptance_kernels_give_their_twins_answers_on_the_randomizers_own_data` (every path of the kernel, placed and stuck, against the twin), and the default plan under Pyodide counts the side module's attempts.
- **Every test run keeps only its failed tests' temporary folders** (Step 4.5v2, tests only): pytest's defaults kept every test's `tmp_path` for its last 3 sessions, so the gate's site tests' downloads (built images) piled up, one 8-worker gate's 44 GB. `pyproject.toml` now sets `tmp_path_retention_policy = "failed"` and `tmp_path_retention_count = 1`: a passing test's folder goes as it ends, a passing session's whole folder at its end, and of the failed sessions' (their failed tests' and their module- and session-wide folders) only the newest's stays, where the defaults kept three. CI's vanilla tier drops its own `-o tmp_path_retention_policy=failed`, now the default; `tests/test_contracts.py` pins both.

### Fixed
- **A shaped candidate's play in the compiled kernel has room for its every stage** (Step 4.4ci57): `fill._Kernel._accept` gave az_accept's answer `Q_HEAD + L + 1` words, one short of play's most stages, L + 2 (L rounds of the plan's options each collecting one spot, a victory round with nothing new, the last), so it would write a word past its end on a database whose every spot comes one a round; the shipped database can't (its forfeitable spots are never collected), so no plan changes. Test: `test_the_acceptance_kernels_play_keeps_every_stage_when_the_options_collect_one_spot_a_round` (a three-spot chain).
- **The app's page server keeps a page's connections and holds a burst of connects** (Step 4.5b): the desktop app's server (`src/azurkit/app/__init__.py`) answered each request over a connection of its own (HTTP/1.0, Nagle on) from socketserver's listen queue of 5, the two things the site's test server (`tests/_static.py`) dropped for GitHub's Windows runner, which refuses a connect past a full queue (4.4ci15's 29 ECONNREFUSED; 4.4ci42's install of 551 files a connection each). A queue of 5 is one unproven cause of the Windows frozen app's first load that never finished (`[firefox@frozen_app]`'s 30 s `Page.goto`, 4.4ci30). Its listen queue is now the system's longest (`socket.SOMAXCONN`) and each connection is kept for the page's next request (HTTP/1.1, `TCP_NODELAY`); every answer has its length but a long call's (its headers, a space each `KEEP` s, then its JSON), which says `Connection: close` and ends at it; a refusal closes its connection, a request over a connection kept past the app's close is refused, and a browser that drops a kept connection prints no traceback. `test_the_apps_server_holds_a_burst_of_connects_and_answers_a_pages_files_over_one_kept_connection`: 32 connects made before the app accepts any are each answered, and one kept connection serves the page, a file and a quick call; the long-call test reads HTTP/1.1.
- **A site or app test whose WebKit page lost its process fails at once, naming it** (Step 4.5f, tests only): on GitHub's Intel Mac runner WebKit ended a live page's process at its network process's request (`reason=RequestedByNetworkProcess`, run 42198913490) and told Playwright nothing, so the read-back's evaluate waited out `_page.STALL` (120 s) and its failure said only that the page's state went unread. A WebKit page's call on macOS (`_page.within`: every evaluate, load, pick and close the tests bound) left `_page.LOOK` (15) s unanswered now reads macOS's log in a thread of its own, from a second before the call began, again each 15 s and at the call's bound, taking the call's answer as it comes until its bound, never after (a read still running past it only names what ended, so `_page.ACT` and `_page.STALL` are no wider than they were); a line of its own browser's UI process on a page whose process ended (`scripts/ci_resources.py`'s `webkit_said`, which takes the browsers' process ids, and `ENDED`: a crash, a memory or CPU kill, the network process's request) fails the call at once with that line. A call answered sooner reads no log. The page's state a stall prints (`_page.HANG`) also holds its last 5 refusals of its security policy (`azurkit.violations`, what was refused and from where), which the status line names only when they are the page's own: Intel's WebKit's unexplained "Refused to apply a stylesheet" (Step 4.4u4) names its source next time. Tests: `test_a_call_whose_page_webkit_ended_fails_at_once_naming_the_ending` (a stand-in ended page, its log a stand-in's, another browser's ending and the network process's refusal left out), `test_a_webkit_call_answered_past_its_bound_fails_though_its_log_is_still_read` and `test_a_call_a_page_leaves_unanswered_fails_by_name_with_its_state_within_its_bound` (the refusals in the state).
- **A changed file that isn't Python picks the tests that read it, not the whole suite** (Step 4.4dx11): `scripts/fullsuite.py`'s `plan()` sent every path no rule covered that isn't Python to the whole suite (11-15 min a lane gate; `.gitattributes` for 4.4dx2, `scripts/ci_retry.sh` for 4.4ci46). Such a file now picks the test files reaching a module that may read it by its path (`namers`): one whose strings, its docstrings aside (`graph_of` keeps each module's, `Graph.texts`), hold the file's name as a word, and in a folder below the top level one naming that folder or sitting in it (a package reads its data by `__file__`); a deleted file's users still name it. One no module names, or one a module every session runs names (`tests/ci_durations.json`: `scripts/ci_shards.py`), stays the whole suite. On this checkout 217 of the 233 such tracked files now pick their readers: `scripts/ci_retry.sh` the smoke set (its `test_contracts` reads it), `.gitattributes` `test_dev_tools` beside it, a package's data 26-29 of the 31 test files; 16 stay whole (the images' facts and manifests, the randomizer's top-level data, the Data tool's `config.xbr` and schema, `tests/_pyodide.mjs`, `tests/ci_durations.json`). Replayed on the 39 lane receipts since 11-03, each on its head's files: 4.4dx2's gate, whole in 754 s, picks 5 files; the other 5 whole gates stay whole (what every session runs); no test file the old plan picked is lost. `tests/test_dev_tools.py` holds the cases.
- **The flake stage runs a test that starts nothing once a session five times in one session** (Step 4.4dx12): the changed-area gate's flake stage started a pytest session for each of its 5 runs, so a stack whose added or edited tests are plain ones paid 5 starts (the collection, xdist's 3 workers, the session's no-window check) for runs of a few seconds. Now a test that reaches no module- or session-scoped fixture of the suite's own (a conftest's or a test file's: `browser`, `pyodide`, `frozen_app`, `site`, `shipped_database` and the like; pytest's own, such as `tmp_path`'s, and `usa_iso` and `europe_iso`, which only hand it a path, aside) has its 5 runs in the first run's session, each a case of its own, `<id>[<its parameters>-flake-run-K]`, through `scripts/flake_repeat.py`, a pytest plugin the stage loads from the worktree (`-p scripts.flake_repeat --flake-runs=5`, `fullsuite.REPEAT`); a case's runs share one xdist group, so one worker runs them one after another as its sessions did, never two at once. A test that reaches one, through a test-scoped fixture too, keeps a session a run (what such a session sets up once is part of its run: five runs on one `site` would be one), and the later sessions run those alone. A failing repeat is named with its own run (`fullsuite.split`), and the receipt's `flake` block names the tests repeated (`repeated`). `case_id` also drops a group from `-rfE`'s summary, which reads a grouped case's `@group` as its parameters' (`t.py::t[x]@frozen_app]`, a frozen app test's failure too). `tests/test_dev_tools.py` holds stand-in module- and session-scoped fixtures' tests, one through a test-scoped fixture, five sessions each, beside plain ones (one taking the images' paths and `tmp_path`) run five times in the first session on one worker each, and a plain test failing its third repeat.
- **The flake stage's test counts its five runs on Windows too** (Step 4.5c, tests only): its stand-in tests, run by the stage on two xdist workers, appended a line a run to one file, and on Windows, whose C runtime appends by a seek to the end and then a write, one worker's line overwrote the other's: GitHub's Windows runner read 4 runs of 4 cases (31 lines of 35; `test_a_flake_stages_test_that_starts_nothing_once_a_session_has_its_five_runs_in_one_session`). Each worker of a session now logs to a file of its own, each line stamped with its time, read back in that order, and a run's session is xdist's run id (`testrunuid`), not the worker's parent process, which on Windows is the venv's launcher. The stage is unchanged; the test still pins five runs.
- **An app test's home gets no write from the test before it** (Step 4.5d, tests only): on GitHub's Windows Firefox (the 8a3128b candidate) `test_a_collected_spot_leaves_the_map_and_a_live_pickup_flashes_and_says_what_was_got` found the spot it had just marked collected still on the map (`['Water power: collected']`, what the map shows with Show collected on). The page redraws the map in the same task its marks change, and a collected spot stays drawn only with Show collected on or in a Live pickup's flash, so its page had opened with Show collected on, and the one way found to that is a late write: socketserver waits for none of the app server's daemon threads at `Server.close`, and the app finds its config folder at each write, so a request a closed server was still answering wrote where the folders were next: the next test's home, or the user's own config once its test's `monkeypatch` had put the folders back. The other floors' test, before it in the module, ends with Show collected on, its page posting its marks every 0.7 s to its close. Each test in `tests/test_hosts.py` now ends with a wait (`_answered`, in an autouse fixture, `_closed_servers_answered`, that takes `monkeypatch`, so it ends before the folders are put back), as `_home` begins with one, for up to `ANSWERED_S` (30 s: a browser's 6 kept connections' config writes on one lock, each a read and a replace past Windows' refusals, 19 s at most), until no closed server is answering a request (`_answering`: a thread in a closed server's `_Handler.do_GET` or `do_POST`). Here, the two tests in that order in Firefox with each config replace refused 4 times (as Windows refuses one while its scanner holds the file) failed 10 of 10 as on CI, 2 or 3 `api/tracked` writes of the closed server still running at the second test's `_home`, and pass 5 of 5 with the wait at the first test's end (1.7-2.1 s, 3 writes), the run's own config left with no tracker; unrefused they pass 10 of 10 either way, and the collected spot's test alone passes 11 of 11, and 10 of 10 slowed (`taskpolicy -b`, nice 19). Test: `test_a_closed_servers_late_write_lands_in_its_own_tests_home_not_the_next_ones` (two tests in a run of their own: a write held past the first test's end lands in its config, and the run's own and the next test's have none).
- **CI's vanilla tier fetches the game data a part at a time, each with retries of its own** (Step 4.5e): the vanilla job fetched each release's folder (USA's 30 parts, Europe's 25, 90 MiB each) in one lazy fetch of a sparse clone, about 2.5 GiB in one SSH session. Twice a macOS runner (run 42313664434's Intel job over port 22, run 42387860921's Apple Silicon job over `ssh.github.com:443` with keepalives) lost its whole network for minutes 2.5 min into the Europe folder's fetch: that session died mid-pack, harden-runner's agent's own uploads timed out at the same minutes, a later try's connect timed out or its DNS lookup went unanswered, and each of the 3 tries, 1.5 min of waits apart, started the whole fetch over inside the outage. Of 50 timed fetches in recent runs, 2 of 31 on macOS failed so and none of 19 on Windows and Linux; the disks held 42-106 GiB free. Whether the runner's virtual network or harden-runner's network extension drops the traffic isn't shown (no macOS fetch ran without the agent, which logged no drop of these flows). Now the clone holds the commit, its trees and the root's three small files (`--filter=blob:none --no-checkout`), and each file of the folder comes in a checkout of its own, its blob in one small fetch, through its own `retry --tries=6` (`scripts/ci_retry.sh`'s new `--tries=N`: N tries, each wait 30 s longer than the last, 7.5 min of waits for 6), so a cut costs one part and the next try starts at it; the log names the count of files each image came from. `tests/test_contracts.py` pins the clone's and the checkouts' form, the per-file loop and its retry, and the helper's `--tries` form.
- **CI's vanilla tier gets the game data on Windows again, each part a fetch of its own** (Step 4.5h): 4.5e's form had each file's blob lazily fetched inside its own `git checkout`, and Git for Windows' FSCache, which `checkout` turns on across the files it writes and which serves a relative path's folder listings from its cache, kept the object folder's listing from before that lazy fetch, so the checkout never saw the pack the fetch had just written: the bdcf682 candidate's two Windows jobs failed the root's checkout ("unable to read sha1 file of .gitattributes", `manifest.json`, `reassemble.py`; checkout's own exit 255, not ssh's) on all 6 tries, its lazy fetches' ssh starting and printing nothing, while Linux and macOS, with no such cache, passed. Each file's blob now comes in a `git fetch` of its own process (the command git's lazy fetch runs, with no background maintenance left holding the folder the step deletes), and its checkout then runs with lazy fetching off (`GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1`), so it reads a blob already on disk on every OS, and still each part is a retried fetch of its own. Here, on a blobless shallow clone of a stand-in repository, 4.5e's checkout with lazy fetching off fails with the Windows jobs' messages and exit 255, and the step's new functions, run as written, rebuild both folders with no lazy fetch (trace2's promisor count 0). `tests/test_contracts.py` pins the fetch's and the checkout's form.
- **The Data tab's test presses Clear edits before the engine can answer the edit it clears** (Step 4.5g, tests only): `test_the_data_tab_edits_reverts_and_undoes_before_an_image` made its last edit, waited a timer and pressed Clear edits, counting on three grids asked first to keep the worker busy past the timer; once on GitHub's arm64 runner in Firefox it waited out its 600 s on the undo stack and the edits (the page idle, no error), the state the edit answered before the Clear leaves (the edit kept, then cleared: the edits empty, which the wait read as not yet). Here the timer came first in 60 runs of 60 in Firefox (plain, slowed and with the page's main thread slowed 30 ms a message) and no path in the page's code that loses an edit was found, so the cause is not sure. The test now presses Clear edits a microtask after the edit: the edit's ask is out with the edits before it, and its answer, a task, comes after the Clear in any browser at any pace (the three grids go); it then waits for the undo stack's depth and asserts the edits, so a wrong order fails at once by name. The page's code is unchanged.
- **The flake stage's end of a run waits on Windows for every process under it** (Step 4.5i, dev tools only): `test_a_flake_run_or_collection_past_the_stages_cap_is_ended_with_its_processes_and_fails_the_stage_by_name` failed on GitHub's Windows runner (the bdcf682 candidate) as its `late` stage deleted the output of the run the cap had ended: WinError 32, the file still held. `scripts/fullsuite.py`'s `end()` ran `taskkill /F /T` and waited for the run's own process alone, which on Windows is the venv's `python.exe` launcher; `taskkill` only starts each kill, and the interpreter under the launcher, still ending, held its inherited handle on the output (likely the cause, not seen directly). `end()` now lists the run's tree first (`descendants()` reads Windows' process table through `scripts/ci_resources.py`'s `table` and `tree`, which skips a child older than its parent), holds each process, ends any `taskkill`'s walk missed and waits for each before it returns. The test drops its two bounds above the stage's wall (a run's wall under the cap, the stage's under the cap plus 5 s), which a starved box broke (15.3 s at load 69): it now pins the run's wall within the stage's, the stage's at least the cap, and that each stage deleted its run's output. The macOS and Linux paths and the site and app are unchanged.
- **The site tests' file server sends an answer whole while macOS is short of buffers** (Step 4.5j, tests only): on GitHub's arm64 runner a first visit's install in Firefox failed (its registration gone, the host asked for all 551 files), because the server's `sendall` hit ENOBUFS ("may succeed when buffers become available", send(2)) mid-answer and the connection closed on a cut file; one bad file fails an install by design. `tests/_static.py`'s `Sender` now sends 64 KiB pieces and sends a piece refused with ENOBUFS or ENOMEM again after a pause (up to 30 s with none sent); on macOS each piece is no longer than the socket's low-water mark, raised to 64 KiB, so XNU's `sosend` copies a piece in one chain and a refused one sent none of its bytes. Why the kernel was short (the 7 GiB runner, three browsers) isn't shown. `web/sw.js` is unchanged: a failed install leaves the page running and the next visit installs again from what it kept.
- **A WebKit engine's numpy load no longer waits on an instantiate WebKit may never settle** (Step 4.5k): on GitHub's Linux runner a WebKit engine twice sat in numpy's load (Pyodide's `loadPackage`, which the Characters tab's calls and a link's decode wait on) to its test's end: the restarted engine of `test_a_tabs_ask_whose_engine_traps_starts_the_engine_again_and_is_answered` 596 s and the third page's engine of `test_the_sites_patches_tab_installs_a_community_pack_from_its_own_address_and_builds_it` 120 s (the pool's state: `at` numpy, its last message that long ago), where here it takes 0.1-0.2 s (the first test's flow 60 times of 60 in WebKit, each worker's messages timed). The load instantiates numpy's 13 shared libraries each with `WebAssembly.instantiate` (Emscripten's async `dlopen`), whose promise WebKit's own worker bug 208368 (GTK) leaves unsettled now and then: its completion timer fires on another run loop and is skipped (closed in 2026 unfixed, when its test's flaky expectation went). In WebKit the engine's worker (`web/worker.mjs`) now compiles and instantiates at once, the promise settled by itself (a worker may; JSC's sync compile needs no timer): numpy's libraries, Pyodide's small module's instantiate at its start and md5.wasm's load; Chromium and Firefox keep their own. Not shown: that bug in this WebKit (2370) on Linux, which this Mac can't run (an IndexedDB write cut by a worker's end, the other suspect, didn't wedge the next worker's read here in 200 tries: WebKit 80, Chromium and Firefox 60 each); that the load sat in an instantiate and not in its wheel's fetch; why both engines had started from the memory snapshot. Test: `test_an_engines_numpy_load_in_webkit_waits_on_no_async_wasm_instantiate` (in WebKit the browser's instantiate never settles and the Characters tab's list is answered; elsewhere the browser's own instantiates numpy's libraries).
- **Each Firefox the tests launch takes no proxy, so a page's load of its test host never waits on the system's proxy settings** (Step 4.5l, tests only): on GitHub's Windows runner `test_a_page_of_the_next_release_never_runs_the_last_ones_engine[firefox]` got no answer in 30 s to its first load of its 127.0.0.1 host, the load's one request (`GET /`) heard only 1 s before the failure and the host printing no error, so the request was held before it started, not cut on its way back as 4.5j's macOS send was (that one printed its `ENOBUFS`). Firefox asks for a request's proxy before it starts it (`nsHttpChannel`: the proxy, then `http-on-modify-request`, Playwright's request event); Playwright's `network.proxy.allow_hijacking_localhost` keeps 127.0.0.1 in that lookup and Juggler's channel filter keeps every request off Firefox's direct fast path, so with Firefox's default proxy setting, the system's, each request waits on the system's answer, on Windows WinINet's, which Firefox's bug 1869403 found taking 4-7 s a request and 40 s before a page drew anything, with no proxy set (its cache since is dropped at each network or registry change). The app's page in Firefox there once went past its 30 s at its first load too, a different server, naming no file (run 42122369507). `tests/conftest.py`'s launch guard now gives each Firefox launch `network.proxy.type` 0 (`DIRECT`) under the launch's own prefs. That WinINet held this load is likely, not shown: the runner's Firefox logged nothing. Test: `test_each_firefox_a_test_launches_takes_no_proxy_under_its_own_prefs`.
- **The pack golden builds as many pages at once as its worker's share of the cores holds a page's two engines** (Step 4.5m, tests only): `_at_once` gave the golden a page a core of all the machine's cores (at least 2), so on GitHub's 4-core Intel Mac runner, whose vanilla tier runs two xdist workers, both releases' WebKit goldens ran up to 4 pages each at once, and each pick starts its page's two engines (F checks the image, then B builds the page's recipe ahead, D1007): a load of 42-54 there, the runner's own process listing unanswered 13 s, and a pick's Playwright round trips past `_page.ACT` (30 s) while its page had already checked the image and was building ahead (run 42406769117, as on b339758). It now takes the cores over xdist's workers, halved for a page's two engines, at least 1, and still no more than the free memory holds: 1 page at a time on that runner and on the 3-core Apple Silicon one, 6 at `-n 2` on a 24-core Mac. Both WebKit goldens at once on two workers here, under `taskpolicy -b` and nice 19 beside 8 busy loops: 4 pages a group picked in up to 8.3 s, the input's change event 1-3 s into the call and its answer 3.9-5.4 s after it; 1 page, 42 picks in at most 2.0 s, each golden 350-414 s against 255-300 s.
- **A site page's call that fails as Playwright's "Execution context was destroyed" names whether its page reloaded** (Step 4.5m, tests only): on GitHub's Intel Mac runner a WebKit page's evaluate in `test_a_weapon_a_body_lifts_off_the_floor_builds_on_the_site_as_cpython_builds_it` failed so with nothing more (run 42406769117). Its closing period is Playwright's WebKit driver's rewrite of an error WebKit answered the call with (its frame's own word for a navigation has none); the tier's WebKit log named no ending of a page's process, and the site's test pages have no navigation of their own (their service worker is blocked; the engine's restart restarts a worker, not the page). Not reproduced: the test 10 times in WebKit here, slowed, all passed. `_page.within` now fails such a call naming it and its error, how long after or before the call the page's document began (its `performance.timeOrigin`, the start of its navigation: after the call, the page reloaded or navigated; before it, the gap tells the page's own load from a navigation begun before the call that committed during it), WebKit's log's endings since the call, the page's state and its driver's processes; any other error is raised as it came. The weapon test clicks Build through `_page.click`, so its click is one of those calls.
- **A freeze in a gap of the perf gate says how much CPU the browser ran in it** (Step 4.5n, tests only): the full run's capped app failed `A32` on "the page froze 593 ms in the head start before A31", a stall the page's own code didn't make: in that head start it asks nothing after its change's answers and runs no task of 20 ms or more, and stopping WebKit's GPU, network or UI process, the app's server or its engine there for 0.7 s delays none of its timers (21 head starts on the app and the capped app, none of them a tick 50 ms late). The freeze probe's error now names the CPU the browser's processes used across the frozen gap (`tests/_perf.py`'s `Memory.ran`, from the run's 0.2 s sampler and one sample as the gap ends), so the next one tells a busy page from one its machine kept from running; no verdict changes.
- **The memory snapshot's site test runs one engine at a time** (Step 4.5p, tests only): on GitHub's Apple Silicon runner `test_a_later_worker_starts_from_the_first_ones_snapshot_also_grown_and_builds_as_cpython[webkit]` failed as "Target page, context or browser has been closed" (run 42435914930), the one WebKit page process that ended in the tier: WebKit's log says `reason=Crash`, and macOS's report an `EXC_BREAKPOINT` on an engine's worker thread in JavaScriptCore's `operationWasmTriggerOSREntryNow` (a hot wasm loop's entry into its optimized code, which stopped on one of JSC's own checks), the process at 1.24 GiB (its highest 1.41) with 2.6 GiB of the runner's 7 free. So WebKit's own wasm tier faulted, as in 4.4ci47's crash, not a memory kill (WebKit logs those as `ExceededMemoryLimit`, and a jetsam kill leaves no crashed thread); the browser ran on, and the test closed nothing. Playwright 1.64.1, the newest, still ships WebKit 2370. The test held up to four pages at once, each running two engines (F, and B, which the tests' 4 cores start), more wasm tiering up beside each other than its claim needs. Each page now says 2 cores (`TWO_CORES`: no B) and closes before the next opens, so one engine runs at a time and each start the test checks is its page's one engine's (each page's `starts` is now the one start it names). Here, two at a time in WebKit: the old form passed 40 of 40 (no crash: this Mac's WebKit 2370 is the macOS 26 build, the runner's the macOS 15 one), up to 8 page processes at once, each at most 1.62 GiB (median 0.92); the new form 30 of 30 in as long (a median 19.3 s against 20.0 s), 2 at once, each at most 1.19 GiB (median 0.47); Chromium and Firefox 2 of 2 each. The page's code is unchanged: memory wasn't short, and the fault is in the browser's engine.
- **Firefox's Save opens about twice as soon after a build, its copy at a quarter of the CPU** (Step 4.5q): the 8ccc5c0 candidate's full run failed `R24/firefox` (the engine ready again and Save open after a seed's build) at 4.15 s, cpu 5.9-7.6 s, where the full runs of bdcf682, 8a3128b and 08b6b19 took 1.48-2.36 s. Not a change of the candidate's: bdcf682 and 8ccc5c0 measured back to back in one window (at nice 15) took alike (medians 4.20 and 3.79 s), and 8ccc5c0 took 1.5-2.1 s once the box went quieter minutes later. In Firefox R24 is the copy that keeps a build's Blob few parts (Step 4.3r4ff): the engine is ready 0.05-0.96 s after the build, and Save opens when the 835 MB between a seed's three longest copies (of 2.75 GB) have gone into one Blob, a `Response.blob()` in Firefox's temporary file, 1.4 s and 4.1 CPU-s on a quiet box: CPU enough that on a busy one (the full run's nice 10 beside other projects' load, a game here using 2 cores) it took 4-7 s, its time and its counted CPU both up, the same work slower (likely on the slow cores: not shown). The perf tests' changes since bdcf682 don't reach it (perf.py serves the site and launches Firefox itself). The bytes now go into a build file of their own (`web/flat.mjs`: a worker reads each 16 MiB run and writes it through the file's sync access handle, its share after each for the bar), held and removed with its build as a character's file is (`outputs.mjs`'s `written`): the same 835 MB in 0.83-0.96 s and 1.0-1.2 CPU-s against 1.36-1.48 s and 4.0-4.3. A/B/A/B in one window at nice 10 (a load of 6-22): R24/firefox medians 1.93 and 2.67 s before, 1.19 and 1.04 s after (cpu from the build's click 4.4-6.9 s, then 2.5-3.8 s); Firefox's tree peak 3.48-3.54 GiB, then 3.20-3.29 (still read by reference); S50 unchanged. A private window, which refuses the file system, keeps the plain Blob. Tests: the Save-greyed test holds the file's hold, finds the file while its build is kept and gone once a change forgets it (no file in WebKit or Chromium), and the private window's makes no build file.
- **The stubbed engines' site tests count only the engines in Firefox** (Step 4.5r, tests only): the 3de6f8c candidate's Linux Firefox CI (job 128671222516) and its full run here failed `test_a_sites_reshape_dress_runs_in_the_worker_the_page_grew_its_small_asks_beside_it[firefox]` on its last claim, the page's workers 2 and none stopped: they were 3, one stopped. Step 4.5q's build file writer is a Worker Firefox's build starts and ends, and the tests' `STUB_ENGINES` took every Worker for an engine: the writer took the stub's `ready` for a failed write, so its build fell back to the plain Blob and the writer was stopped. The stub now makes a Worker whose first page module on the stack is `outputs.mjs` (its `written`) a real one, kept out of `made`; the claims are unchanged. Here at `-n 2` the test failed 5 of 5 before, passed 5 of 5 after, and the four tests that use the stub passed in all three browsers. The page's code is unchanged.
- **The replaced-file site test saves once Save opens, and Firefox's Save opens whatever its build file's writer meets** (Step 4.5u): the 911040d candidate's Linux CI (job 128708306015) and a full run here failed `test_a_build_with_a_replaced_file_is_the_apps_image[firefox]` waiting 600 s for a download. The test clicked Save as soon as the page had the build, and Firefox greys Save until its Blob is made (Step 4.3r4ff), now by Step 4.5q's worker, so on a busy box the click met the greyed button and did nothing; every other Save test waits for it to open (`_page.built`), and now this one does. Measured in Firefox here on the 911040d page, 10 builds of each kind: the writer took 34-75 ms after a replaced file's build (3.2 MB), 21-43 ms after a default one, 0.42-0.98 s after a character's and 0.99-5.3 s after a seed's (822-837 MB), so a click at the test's moment (45-287 ms after the build) met the greyed Save 0 of 20 times at idle and 20 of 20 for the two long kinds, and 10 of 10 after a replaced file's build with the writer's start 500 ms late, as on a busy box. Once Save opened, all 40 Saves held, the replaced file's image CPython's by its sha256 10 of 10. The writer never hung: a refused sync handle, a write refused as a full disk's and a `flat.mjs` that 404s each reached the page, 10 of 10, which kept the plain Blob and held (Firefox's own quota set to 1 or 20 MB never refused the 1.9 MB file, 20 runs). Now a failed writer's bytes go into a `Response.blob()` as before Step 4.5q (30 of 30 held), its failure too leaving the plain Blob, and every way its worker can end (refused at its start, a message the page can't read, a close that throws in its failure) reaches the page, so Save opens. The fixed test passed 10 of 10 in Firefox here. Tests: the replaced-file test waits for Save; the private-window test also runs with a build file that takes no byte, the bytes copied as before, the file removed and the Save holding.
- **The build file's site test runs again once when WebKit's network process ends its page** (Step 4.5s, tests only): on GitHub's Intel Mac runner `test_a_characters_pieces_go_from_the_engines_heap_into_a_build_file_held_until_its_build_is_gone_and_a_crash_leaves_none[webkit]` failed at its change's tick (run 42461371001), 4.5f's watch naming WebKit's end of the page's process, `reason=RequestedByNetworkProcess`: the network process had refused a message from it, `AudioSessionRoutingArbitratorProxy_BeginRoutingArbitrationWithCategory`, and asked for it to be ended. That name is the first of WebKit's message table (`IPC::MessageName` 0, as a header of zeros reads), the network process has no receiver of it, a page's process sends it only to its browser's own process for a page's sound, and the site plays none: WebKit's own fault, as run 42198913490's unnamed one, not the test's (its "crash" is a closed page) nor the site's. A player's Safari reloads such a page, whose load removes its build files. `_page.within` now raises `_page.Ended` with WebKit's lines, and `_page.past_refusal` runs a test again once, with a warning, when they say `RequestedByNetworkProcess`; any other failure (a crash's end too) and a second such end fail as before. The test's every call to a page is now bounded (`_page.answer`, `_page.close`), so such an end in any of them runs it again rather than waiting. Not reproduced here: the test 20 times in WebKit at `-n 2`, slowed as the flake stage slows (the utility clamp beside 12 loops), passed in 36-91 s a call (CI's failing one took 73 s), WebKit's log naming no end of a page's process; once each in Chromium and Firefox, passed. The page's code is unchanged.
- **CI keeps Pyodide and the browsers in its cache and rides out an outage of minutes** (Step 4.5t, CI only): the 911040d candidate's run 42467070736 failed 9 jobs at "Pyodide and the browsers": GitHub's release downloads answered Pyodide's core 504 for minutes (AzurKit's own v1.0.0 files too, while GitHub's status said operational), and `web_tools.download`'s 3 asks spanned 15 s. `tests.yml` now keeps what `scripts/web_tools.py` fetches (Pyodide's core and wheel, Playwright's three browsers) in Actions' cache (`actions/cache` v6.1.0's restore and save), keyed by the exact versions and the OS (`web_tools.py --cache`: Pyodide's, its packages, the locked playwright's, the macOS release or Linux distribution and the CPU) and a short digest of what is kept (Pyodide's files, each engine's name and its install, Chromium's headless shell alone), so an edit to what is kept moves the key, restored before the fetch (on a miss its folders go first, so a restore cut short in its extraction leaves no part file to keep or save) and saved after it on a miss, before any key or image is on the runner, so a run fetches none of it once a run on its OS kept it; nothing of the game's is in it. A fetch that still runs asks 6 times, 15 s to 2 min apart (5 min 45 s of waits), and no wait starts that would end past 10 min from the first ask; each failed ask says when the next comes. `scripts/ci_retry.sh`'s retry (uv sync, apt under install-deps, the content clone) tries 6 times by default too, 7.5 min of waits, where 3 tries spanned 1.5 min. Tests: the download's stand-in server test holds the new schedule and the cap, the contracts pin the cache's steps in both jobs and the retry's 6.

## [1.0.0 (November 7, 2026)]

### Added
- The tracker's Track shows its steps on the bottom bar in the app and on the web page: the randomizer's logic read, the seed's fill, its placements, then the seed's logic and the world map of its first view (Step 4.3 train).
- The Characters tab hides the 76 objects (the pickups, keys and statues: at most two bones and no clip but an idle or a sit, the engine's rule, so never the boulder) until Show objects, off by default and remembered; the count line says how many are hidden, and a model worn stays listed and worn (Step 4.3 characters tab).
- The perf gate (Step 4.3j, D980-D985): `scripts/perf.py` times every player-facing operation (`tests/_perf.py`'s 55 ids, the app's A01-A30 and the site's S01-S25) by the page's clock in the app's serve mode and on the site, headless, against 3 s (9 s capped); `scripts/fullsuite.py` runs it after pytest inside the lock for the ops a change reaches, and main's full run times them all in four profiles; `tests/perf_ceilings.toml` lists main's over-budget ops with their ceilings and its progress debts, and only shrinks; `scripts/land.py` lands a `src/` or `web/` stack only with a passing perf block, no stack whose list grows main's, and `--release` only with an empty list.
- Community packs' engine (Step 4.3k, D988, D997): `tools/mods/community.py` checks a community pack (an Azurik-Custom-Content `pack` entry's `packs/<id>/pack.json`: a release-free mods file of AzurKit's own packs and `gamedata/*.xbr` edits, self-contained), gives the content folder's Install check of a pack entry (`refusal`, for `content.verify`'s hook), resolves a recipe's `community` pins (installed, untouched since its install, at its pack.json's sha12, else named with what to Install or Update), merges its built-in packs with the recipe's own (a pack set two ways refused by name, equal copies one) and plans its edits as their own `community <id>` layer; `edits.preset.from_manifest` carries a build's community layers back as the mods file's `community` layer (id and sha12). `web/content.mjs` is the page's one Content from, list and Install/Update path for both community tabs (the requires walk each entry once with a done set, at most 256 MiB an Install, no reply past its listed size, "k of n files", nothing kept unless the engine passes every file, an Update dropping what its new version doesn't hold but not a file another installed entry holds now). The hub half (Step 4.3r4q): the Patches tab's Community group on both hosts, on the one Install path `web/characters.mjs` now uses too, a recipe's `community` in a build, a link, a mods file and the remembered setup, `azurkit build --community ID`; no pack ships.
- The zoom detail maps (Step 4.3l, D988): a coarse level's floors ship again at 2 px a metre (Modkit's detail pictures, `art.detail_factor` and `art.detail`, 18 levels, 102 pictures in `web/art/<ART_VERSION>/detail/`, `index.json`'s `detail`), and the Tracker's map (`web/mapview.mjs`, `web/art.mjs`; the app's `api/art`) reads the shown floor's once its zoom comes within a notch of its own pixels and draws it past them, one held at a time, on the site and in the app; perf ops S30, S31, A37 and A38.
- The tracker's maps drop a spot once it is collected (Show collected, remembered, shows them dimmed), and a Live pickup flashes its spot, its rows and the Got count and says "Got: <item> · <level>" for a few seconds (a plain highlight with reduced motion) (Step 4.3 live tracker).
- Each floor button on a tracker map carries a badge of the spots not collected on that floor (Step 4.3 live tracker).
- The tracker's Follow switches the level map to the floor you stand on as you move between floors, holding the floor you were on across a seam; a floor you pick by hand stays until your floor changes, and with Follow off the map keeps the floor shown (Step 4.3 live tracker).
- The Tools tab's Open a build… offers Track this build for a randomized build: the tracker follows that build's own plan, from its seed and share string, with nothing retyped (Step 4.3 live tracker).
- On a phone or a tablet the page shows one plain line saying AzurKit is for computers (D1000: `(hover: none) and (pointer: coarse)`, no layout of its own), and README says where it runs (Step 4.3m).
- The progress bar counts a dress's work, item by item, on both hosts: the app's engine and the site's worker set `pool.TICK`, each item done counted on the stage its call reported last, and the app's pictures and G4 match pass their progress to the engine (Step 4.3m).
- The perf gate times D999's custom shape: A31, booblord with one slider moved, built the first time (a bar op), and A32, the same reshape at the next launch (strict), driven by `tests/_perf.py`'s `app_reshape` (Step 4.3m).
- A seed's planning and the tracker's first reading of the maps' exits show on the progress bar: `sdk.image` and `sdk.tracker` tell the planner's notes (`generator.NOTE`'s "placing the items", `needs.NOTE`'s "the maps' exits, k of n") to the calling thread's call, each folded into the stage it reported last, the exits counted inside the tracker's world-map step (Step 4.3m).
- Firefox in the web tier and the perf gate (Step 4.3n, D994): `scripts/web_tools.py` (and `uv run bootstrap.py`) installs Playwright's Firefox into `.tools/`, the `browser` fixture drives the page in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox (a whole run each; a changed-area run Chromium alone), `tests/conftest.py` stops a run that selects a Firefox test without it, and a browser main's `scripts/perf.py` doesn't time yet gets one first record: `--update-ceilings` lists its over-budget ops and debts as measured, and `perf.py` and `scripts/land.py` accept them against main's list once (`perf.new_browsers`); Firefox joins `perf.py`'s browsers with its record, in the stack after this one, since the landing queue lands with main's `land.py`.
- **The perf gate names a starved sample** (Step 4.3r4a): each sample timed from a click keeps the CPU seconds its tree used, read in the run's memory sampler, each process's highest figure staying when it exits, so a child gone mid-op doesn't read low. A sample past 1 s whose wall passes 1.3x that waited for a core: it is taken once more, then stands with its `starved` tag, and the case names it; no verdict reads the tag. One past 30 s isn't taken again; the lag run, whose reads wait by design, keeps no CPU figure.
- The sparse seed build's engine half: a build's base may hold `iso.View`s of the levels (`generator.sections`, each level's TOC and node sections), `compose` carries what the layers change of them as `iso.Patched` patches, `iso.read` writes only those, each changed file is hashed whole by reading past them, and `generator.read_back`'s `sparse` reads the image's sections against the plan (D35); `generator.heads` and `generator.pages` name a host's reads ahead. The image and manifest are the whole build's, byte for byte. No host passes a view yet, so every build reads as it did (Step 4.3r4d; X4; D1017).
- `edits.staged` is `edits.image` as a generator for a host that hashes a build's changed files and the root files' blobs itself, with `Manifest.to_json` and `root_files` taking its sha256s by `shas`; the bytes are `edits.image`'s, and no host calls it yet (Step 4.3r4d, R3-0 (f)).
- **The app's work ahead, wired** (Step 4.3r4g; AM-HUB-A; D1006, D1007, D1014): the page's settled setup is built, its typed seed planned and that plan's first tracker view read before their clicks (`api/speculate`, `api/prefetch`, at the page's change number, on the app only); Build, Track and the tracker's first ask take them, running or done, the plain run's answer. A change stops a job at its next record. Cancel answers at once and keeps the engine, its Logic and the fill: the build's job runs on through a seed's fill for the next Build of those settings. `AZURKIT_RAM` caps the memory the host reads (the capped profile's 8 GiB) and a heavy job ahead answers to memory alone. New ops A56, A57 (the median seed's twins of A23, A27) and A58 (a stale job's drop). A preview or a G4 match stops a build ahead at its next record, so they never stack past 6 GiB. A change that leaves nothing to build (no image, Randomize with no seed, a refused body) drops the job too. A job ahead's pool parts, its threads, its build's hash and the sdk's hash pools run at its low priority too.
- The perf gate times the site in Firefox too (Step 4.3r4h, D994): `scripts/perf.py --browsers firefox`, and main's full run (`--all`) in every browser, against Firefox's first record in `tests/perf_ceilings.toml`; its run logs Firefox's tree peak (`tree_peaks`), which no verdict reads.
- The Characters tab's "Chain on any press" checkbox, beside Gameplay and off by default (D1045): ticked, the tab's character gets `"chain": "any"`, so a build, a link and the remembered setup carry it; picking another model unticks it (Step 4.3r4hub).
- The tracker's level map draws its other floors' tracked spots faintly on the floor shown (alpha 0.45 divided by the floors between, under everything else, with no tooltip and no click; collected ones only with Show collected); the floor buttons' counts stay (Step 4.3r4hub, D1044).
- A reshaped variant's pictures are drawn at last (D1011): `sdk.preview` with `shapes` draws what a build of those shapes writes, and the Characters tab keys its drawing on the name and shapes, showing the nearest shape it holds marked Preview at once, the exact ones replacing it once the slider has rested and no Build runs; a build never reads a picture. The site dresses in a worker of its own (`web/dress.mjs`), so the page's asks don't wait for it, stopped at once by a new shape, a Build or a G4. `tests/_perf.py`: A59 and S51 the preview (strict), A60 and S52 the exact first (a bar), A61 and S53 again (strict) (Step 4.3r4hub, AM-HUB-G).
- The release job (Step 4.3r4j; D1022, D885; built, not yet run): `.github/workflows/release.yml`, on a `v*` tag from `main` alone, freezes the four apps on their own OSes, builds the wheel and sdist, writes `SHA256SUMS` and the build attestations and holds them in a draft release; publishing the draft uploads its wheel and sdist to PyPI by trusted publishing and then deploys the site to Pages. README's Download and check it (SEC-7).
- The site's second worker (Step 4.3r4l, AM-HUB-D): `web/workers.mjs` wired into the page: F answers, B works ahead on the typed seed's plan and first view and the settled recipe's build, a change drops it by `terminate()`, a click takes its answer or follows its records. One heap: a worker a job grew stays for the next heavy job up to `GROWN`. The Blob is joined in the worker, off the page's main thread. A character's pieces go into an OPFS build file (`web/outputs.mjs`, P7) under a Web Lock per file, cleared at a page's load and counted by Storage. The perf gate's new ops: S59 (B dropped), S60, S61 (median seeds), and S15 is timed; its site passes clean the origin's OPFS (X2). An image's whole check runs in a worker that ends (`web/hashworker.mjs`), and a build's pieces and hashes are views of the engine's heap.
- **The site registers its service worker** (Step 4.3r4p; D1013): `web/app.mjs` registers `web/sw.js` on the site once the engine is first ready, so a first visit runs as before while the release is kept in the background, and every later page starts from that cache, cross-origin isolated, offline too. A controlled page starts its workers from their own URLs, shows "An update is ready: Reload" when the next release waits and checks every half hour; the Tools tab's Storage counts the release and Clear deletes it. No service worker (a private window) or a refusal runs the page as before. The perf gate fails a return visit (S02) that isn't isolated, and its lag model's `sw.js` lists the files it serves late.
