- **The site keeps a character's dress** (Step 4.3r4t, D1067, D1015): the worker's `tools.characters.CACHE` (`Kept`) takes a build's kept answers from IndexedDB's `azurkit-dress` by the build's hint and keeps a miss's after the answer (32 hints, the least recently built out, a hit the newest again; Storage and Clear list it), so the same character built again, at a later visit too, isn't dressed again; the perf gate times a character's first build on the site as a bar op (`S14`) and the same build at a later visit as a strict one (`S64`: 0.003 s, 0.11 s under Safari's read lag).
- The store (Step 4.3r, D1015): `src/azurkit/store.py` keeps what AzurKit works out between calls and launches in one folder a kind (`store.KINDS`: plans, layers, dresses, pictures, memos, swap), JSON or AZKP1 and never a pickle (`swap` keeps the bytes `characters._kept` and `engine._kept` still pickle, until they move to `dresses` and `memos`/`pictures`), each entry sealed whole (its kind, key, length and body: a sha256, the app's an HMAC under `cache.key`) so a torn, altered or moved entry is a miss, never decoded; one budget of 5 GiB across the kinds, the least recently used out first whatever its kind, and only files of its own names in its own folders ever removed (a player's settings, content installs and key never). It starts no thread, so the page's worker can run it. The app's `swapcache` sits on it (`Kept`; the swap's own 2 GiB is now the one budget): every put is written behind the answer by one daemon writer thread a process (a get meanwhile answers from what it holds; a quit never waits on it, and a write it cuts short leaves only a part file), and 4.3i's `swap/*.pickle` files are never read and are removed by the next write.
- **A Preview button by the Weapon box** (Step 4.3rv-chars, D1061): a small popup that stays open and follows the box, on both hosts and before an image, with no engine call: the staff's and hoe's own pictures (`picker.weapon_preview`, 4 PNGs, 15 KB, the `characters` family's) and, for the three with no object, the model they come from, each with a line (`weapons.json`'s `previews`, `index.json`'s `weapons`).
- Shipped answers for the swap (Step 4.3s, D995, round 2's R2.3): `tools/characters/shipped.py` and its table `shipped.json` hold each species' and built-in variant's size at its defaults on both releases, and booblord's whole plan at the page's defaults, one `AZKP1` file a release in her content entry (`variants/booblord/shipped/<release>-<sha12>.azkp`, each whole file zstd against its vanilla file, no pickle; about 6.6 MB a release for her 117 MB of changes). `characters.size` and `characters.plan` take a row in place of a dress where it stands on what that dress would read (the release, the recipe as given, the fold, the claims before it, the installed content, the sha256 of each staged file it read); a plan's file is read only once its size and sha256 are its row's and inflated to at most the sizes its head lists (D992's SEC-5 rule), and a shipped answer is never kept again in the app's store. `content.SUFFIXES` lets `variants/` hold `.azkp`, and `web_tools.CONTENT_KINDS` publishes it as data. `scripts/record_shipped.py` records the rows from fresh dresses (`--check` re-derives them for the release check); the goldens build with `shipped.OFF` and `tests/test_characters.py` holds each shipped answer a golden build stands on to it, booblord's plan on both releases, so a change to the swap's bytes is red until it re-records. The site's booblord at the defaults, once her entry ships the files, is the app's bytes (it wrote other bytes before: the products' platform bits, AM-D3's).
- Seed difficulty's engine (Step 4.3sd-a, D1031, D1037-D1039): `Settings.seed_difficulty` Any (the default, today's plans byte for byte), Standard, Hard or Frame-perfect; `planner/difficulty.py` (rungs, need and play, the promises, the shipped rate table `data/forcing.json` and `scripts/seed_forcing.py`, the profile and openers), the fill's pin and its lazy acceptance with a pure attempt a host may run in parallel, share version 4 with its literal category mask and fill version, the settings file's key, the tab's `difficulty`, the spoiler's lines and the tracker's `head.requires`, the page holding a loaded level; perf ops A70-A80. The page's select is the next step (SD-b). In the app's engine a shaped fill's attempts past its first run on its threads (`fill.WIDTH` on the app's `procpool.Beside`, the planner's `fill.BESIDE` hook, so the engine itself holds no thread; the same plan at any width), each as soon as one is free and stopped once a plan is decided. A spoiler's openers read one switchable option set a tier (`Logic.reach`'s `off`), about 0.3 s where they compiled some 70 sets in 4-5 s, and the newest shaped plan's go on ahead past any build or other plan, counted in the View's bar; the tracker's Spoiler asks the engine for them, so the server's process never works them out too. A reach's Python in the compiled path costs about half what it did (each fill's sets decoded and named once), so A71's fill, 6.37 s at width 1, is 4.64 s there and 2.28 s at width 4.
- Seed difficulty's page (Step 4.3sd-b, D1031, D1037-D1039): the Randomizer tab's select after Skips, Any by default, its levels greyed from the engine's `difficulty` with the chosen level's line and why the first greyed one is under the row; an edit that takes a level off offer moves it down, saying so; a share string's level sets the select, a loaded one that greys kept and said; the tracker's line under the head (`head.requires.text`). The site's ops and its parallel attempts wait for AM-HUB-D.
- **The native kit and the compiled reach** (Step 4.3t; D1009): `native/reach.c`, the randomizer solver's least fixpoint (`Logic._solve` with its escapes, the physical graph, the traps, the tracker's collected mode and each round's log) over integer tables, built by the new dev-only `python -m nativekit [--check]` with the pinned zig into six libraries committed in `src/azurkit/core/_native/` (macOS arm64 and x86_64, Linux x86_64 and aarch64 at glibc 2.17, Windows x86_64, and a wasm32 side module Pyodide's ctypes loads), with `-ffp-contract=off -fno-fast-math` and baseline CPUs, rebuilt to the same bytes from any path; `azurkit.core.native` loads this platform's and keeps the Python twin, the reference and the fallback (`AZURKIT_NATIVE=0`, no library, a number past int64's room), equal bit for bit over seeded random tables natively and under Pyodide (`tests/test_native.py`). Not wired into the planner yet.
- NOTICE.md and the About tab's credits name what the compiled kernels' libraries carry: zig's compiler_rt (MIT, Zig contributors) in each, and the mingw-w64 runtime's startup code (public domain and ZPL 2.1) in the Windows one (Step 4.3 train B3, the publish audit's F3).
- **The seed's fill counts its spots, and a second ask for it waits on the first** (Step 4.3u): the fill tells `core.progress.note("placing the items, k of n")` each percent of its spots and at each reach, and `core.progress.noting` (the per-thread fold `sdk._noting` makes of the build's notes, in the core where the planner reaches it) folds it into the call's stage, so Track's bar counts inside "Filling the seed" and each note is a point a cancel or a changed seed stops the fill at; a fill another thread is making for the same Logic, seed and settings (a host's plan ahead) is waited on, the waiter telling its count every 0.1 s so it is cancellable too, and copied when it ends; a cancelled or refused one is made again by its waiter. The planner's unused `fill.NOTE` hook is gone. The build's plan stage takes the count once the sdk folds with `core.progress.noting` (the hub's turn).
- **A build's layout and read-back report each file** (Step 4.3v; R2.9's checkpoints, the amortize plan's AM-B1, critic C16): `edits.image(progress=)` hears each file composed and each changed file hashed (on the host's threads), and `generator.read_back(progress=)` (`writer.read_back`'s) each level file it reads back, done of total; an exception a record raises stops the build there, so a cancel or a dropped job waits for one file's work at most. The hosts pass them at the hub turn (`sdk.image`'s layout and judge stages).
- The app's work ahead, its engine half (Step 4.3w, D1006-D1008, D1014): `azurkit.app.ahead` keeps one slot per kind (a seed's plan, its first tracker view, a build), each job keyed by the recipe's canonical JSON, what else its answer comes from (the sliders' share string, a plan's sha256), the image's stamp, the content folder's `installed.json` and AzurKit's version (none for a recipe file's character, whose contents it can't read); the page's change number drops an older offer, keeps a job of the same key and stops one of another key at its next record (the next starting once it has ended; none while a click follows the slot's job); a click takes a running or finished job of its key, or runs its own in the slot; Cancel answers at once and demotes the job, which runs on through a seed's fill (kept for the next Build of the same settings) and stops at its first record past it or at any change, a quiet one handed to the host's kill after 2 s; work ahead runs at background priority and, past a seed's fill (which a click may wait on), waits at its records while a click or another call is in flight, and a recipe that last peaked over 2 GiB isn't worked ahead on a machine under 16 GiB or of 4 cores or fewer. `azurkit.core.host` lowers a thread or the process and puts it back (macOS's QoS class and PRIO_DARWIN_BG, Windows' below-normal priority, POSIX's nice 10, one way only) and reads the machine's memory. The engine's, the server's and the page's wiring is the hub's turn.
- Kept answers, their engine halves (Step 4.3x, D1008, D1015): `azurkit.app.memo` (the app) and `web/memo.mjs` (the site) keep the tracker's view, the setup's restore, the Randomizer tab's answer and a seed's plan (the plan store) as JSON under the sha256 of the code key (the package's files and version, a frozen app's executable; the site: the wheel's integrity), the randomizer database's key, the call, its canonical arguments (the tracker's marks as the tracker reads them) and installed.json's digest, so a kept answer is the engine's own bytes and any changed input misses; nothing is kept with Live on, for a click that marks or resets, or for a refusal; `confirm` works an answer out again and logs a difference (by digests only) as a bug, keeping the engine's; a torn or another key's entry is a miss. The hub turn wires them (api/tracker, api/restore, api/randomizer, api/plan; the page's paint before the worker is up).
- The warm-up, its engine halves (Step 4.3x, D1008, D1007): `azurkit.app.warm` (the app) and `web/warm.mjs` (the site) hold one queue that works out ahead, one item at a time, what the page's next calls read: phase A at ready on intent only (Randomize on in the remembered setup, a tracked plan remembered, or the Randomizer tab opened: the randomizer's logic, the world map, the tracked plan's view, the tab's answer and, on the app, the remembered seed's plan (the site's is the plan ahead's), kept by the kept answers), phase B after the first pick (the image's check and pickers; on the app the remembered character dressed at the current packs). No item starts while a call is in flight and the running one waits at its next record (a pooled dress runs on); a change drops a queued item and stops a running one at its next record; a call that needs an item has it run first, its line and records on the call's bar; a tab moves its items first; `status()` is the bottom bar's row. The hub turn wires it (the engine's warm-ups, the server's launch, the page's row).
- **The site's second worker, its engine half** (Step 4.3y; D1006, D1007): `web/workers.mjs` keeps two engine workers on `worker.mjs`'s protocol, F answering the page's asks and B working ahead on the page's list (the typed seed's plan, the settled recipe's build), dropped by `terminate()` when a key leaves the list. A click whose key is B's finished job takes its answer at once; one B is still working on follows B's records and F never starts it. B takes F's place (after a build past KEEP, a Cancel, a failure) only while idle under KEEP, and is retired after any job past it, its answer kept; one heavy job runs at a time and B's work ahead never beside F's build (F's build drops B's job ahead, B starts nothing beside it); B neither works nor stays past the page's 5 GiB tally, nor under 4 cores; Cancel answers at once and demotes a build whose key is still listed to work ahead, which Build again follows. Keys are canonical JSON (a File or NaN refused by name). Nothing wires it yet: `app.mjs` and `worker.mjs` take it at the hub's turn. `tests/test_site.py` drives it on stub workers and once on two real engine workers (one seed's plan alike on both, B's next plan dropped mid-way).
- **The site's service worker, its engine half** (Step 4.3z; D1013): `web/sw.js` keeps the release `scripts/web_tools.py`'s `site()` now lists in it (each file but `sw.js`, `.nojekyll` and `content-source/`, with its sha256 and size, and the page's CSP) whole in one cache per release and scope, each file checked against its sha256 (an update copies the files an older release's cache holds with the same bytes and downloads the rest; a half-done deploy fails the install, keeping the release before), serves the page, its modules and its engine from it, so a page never runs another release's engine and needs no network once kept, adds COOP, COEP, CORP and the page's CSP to what it serves, holds an update until the page takes it, and deletes the older releases' caches when the next activates. The page registers it at the hub's turn; until then nothing does. `tests/test_service_worker.py` holds it in Chromium, WebKit and Firefox.
- `release.yml` has a dry run (D1068): a dispatch from `main` builds the four apps, the wheel, the sdist and the site as a tag's run does, writes `SHA256SUMS` over the apps, the wheel and the sdist and checks it, and leaves them all as the run's own artifacts, so the 4.4 tag doesn't find the job's bugs. It skips the tag's name check (there is no tag) and never reaches the attestations (Sigstore's log is public), the draft, PyPI or Pages; no job it runs holds an id-token or a write. `SHA256SUMS` moved from the attest job into a job of its own, `sums`, which both runs share; a tag's push still runs everything to the draft and the published draft's run is unchanged (Step 4.4ci8).
- CI on GitHub Actions (Step 4.4ci, D1022-D1024): `.github/workflows/tests.yml` runs the pure tier on every push to main and every pull request on macOS (Apple Silicon on macos-15, and Intel), Windows x64 and Linux x64, and the vanilla and europe tiers on a push to main, each image rebuilt from the private game-data repo's parts with the `gamedata` environment's deploy key, on Linux behind harden-runner's egress block with the tests in a network namespace, and on every OS only the pytest log's tail and summary printed; `checks.yml` runs the leak guard over the checkout and the whole history, and `nativekit --check` on three OSes once `src/azurkit/core/_native/` exists; `apps.yml` freezes the four apps on a push to main, each a 14-day workflow artifact with its sha256. Nothing deploys, publishes or releases.
- **The notices of what AzurKit ships** (Step 4.4pub2): NOTICE.md holds, each with its holder and its licence text as upstream states it, Python 3.14 (the app's interpreter and the site's Pyodide build), Pyodide 314.0.7 (MPL-2.0, with its source's link) and Emscripten 5.0.3, NumPy (2.5.4 in the app, 2.4.6 on the site), PyInstaller 6.22.3 (its bootloader's exception and its runtime's Apache-2.0), platformdirs, and the app's window: pywebview with Bottle, proxy_tools and typing_extensions, PyObjC on macOS, and on Windows Python.NET, clr-loader, CFFI, pycparser and the WebView2 SDK's DLLs. The About tab credits each and links NOTICE.md for the texts.
- About shows the commit AzurKit was built from beside its version (D992's release check, item 7; Step 4.4rel-a): the wheel (`web_tools.wheel`, so the site's and PyPI's) and the frozen app (`scripts/freeze.py`) carry the commit they were built from (`azurkit.STAMP`), a git checkout names its HEAD, and a build or checkout without git says it can't name one; nothing is fetched (`azurkit.commit`, the app's `host.json` and the site worker's ready `commit`).
- The Data tab's engine (Step 4.7a, D986): `azurkit.tools.data` reads config.xbr's 18 keyed tables from the vanilla file it ships (both releases' file, byte for byte) and gives the tab's index (the tables by Modkit's categories; the critter, pickup, armor and staff pickers with their info rows), the Player, Critter, Item, Armor and Weapon views of Modkit's Game Data editor (all 201 match Modkit's, cell for cell, and so do its pickers), a table as a grid, and `check`, which turns a cell's edit into the recipe's `xbr` `set` operation or says why the cell can't hold it, with its type and an example; each cell carries its note, unit, range hint and editor from `schema.json` (Modkit's 110 checked entries, by config.xbr's own names, and the 22 damage records of critters_damage it left out). A recipe's `set` takes `col` (a column of an entity named twice) and `fill` (an empty cell), and refuses a number an int32 cell can't hold once the game rounds or truncates it (D45), critters_damage's cell for each of the 57 damage records among them.
- The Data tab on both hosts (Step 4.7a): `sdk.data` and `sdk.data_edit` (the app's `api/data` and `api/data_edit`, the site worker's `data` and `data_edit` messages), `azurkit data [TABLE [ENTITY]]`, the tab between Characters and Tools; its edits are the page's recipe `xbr` (built after the packs, remembered, linked, saved in a mods file), ⚠ marks the cells a ticked pack's slider writes (each config.xbr slider's spans, as a build plans them), and its line says the edits wait for the image to build (D979). The Characters tab's species list is the shared picker now. The four tools' independence contract holds `tools.data`.
- The Data tab's page (Step 4.7a): `web/data.mjs` draws the six views from the engine's answers (sections of mini-tables; All tables a grid, an entity a row, sorted by a column's header, Changed only), edits a cell on a click, Enter or F2 (a dropdown or a checkbox where the cell's value set says), shows a refusal as one line under the cell, marks a changed cell ● and a cell a ticked pack writes ⚠, and keeps the edits with Revert, Reset table, Undo, Redo and Clear edits; `web/picker.mjs` is the shared gallery picker (a search box over grouped entries, a picture where one ships).
- The gate keeps what it checked (Step 4.7c, the Tools tab's engine): a stamp is whole when every file of the image was checked, also when only a build's check was asked of it (`gate.vouched` prefers the store's whole stamp), and `gate.verify(kept=)` takes a host's stamp of the image as it is now, so Verify every file after the image was picked hashes only the files the build's check left (USA: 1,151 of 1,195 files, 1.75 of 2.74 GB; one thread 1.43 s to 0.90 s), its progress counts only those bytes, and a check after it, of every file or a build's, hashes nothing.
- The Tools tab (Step 4.7c, D986, D919's list), on the site and in the app: your image's release, size and kind (`sdk.image_info`: vanilla, an AzurKit build, or modded with its first file that differs), Verify every file with its bar (`sdk.verify_all`: only what the image's kept check left, hashed on the app's threads or in the site's WebCrypto, then whole), its file list with a filter and Extract (`sdk.files`, `sdk.extract`; `azurkit files IMAGE [--extract PATH --out FILE]`), Open a build…, Load and Save a mods file (`sdk.mods_file`), a plan file's spoiler and the last build's report (View, Save), and AzurKit's storage by name with Clear (the site's named keys and databases, the app's named entries, never an image or its folder, SEC-10); greyed until an image with "Choose your image first (1.)" (D979). Perf rows A47-A55 and S40-S48; a plan file's spoiler (A54, S47) is timed cold, asked as the tab opens, a bar op (the logic's first read in a launch, D1008): its bar counts the randomizer logic's checks while a checkout reads it (`loader.NOTE`, the app's engine reading ahead with `sdk.randomizer_logic`), then the spoiler's stage, never going back.
- Replace files, the engine half (`edits/files.py`, Step 4.7d): a mods file's `files` entries (`{path, sha256, size}`, never the bytes) plan the files the player picked into one whole-file layer (`files put`) that goes after every other; an earlier group it shadows is dropped whole and named (D28), and a file another layer writes whole is refused by name. A build's manifest names each replaced file by its sha256 only, never its bytes (the image holds them), so replaced files of any size make a build AzurKit opens. default.xbe, filelist.txt, /azurkit.json, /azurkit.bin and a path the image hasn't are refused by name, as is a size past an image's file's 4 GiB, and a rebuild whose file isn't picked again this session or is another file (their sizes, never a sha256). A recipe holds no computer path, and `files.source` refuses a network share, a device path and /net/ before a host reads one (SEC-11). The hosts' wiring (the recipe's `files`, the Tools section, the site's picked File) is Step 4.7d's hub half. The hub half (Step 4.3r4q): the Tools tab's Replace files on both hosts (`web/replace.mjs`), `azurkit build --file PATH=FILE`, a recipe's `files` in a build, a mods file and a build's recipe, a link's refusal by name, and no vanilla read of a file only Replace files writes.
- Saves' engine (Step 4.7e, D919's save editor; the Saves section and `azurkit save` come with its hub half): `azurkit.tools.saves` reads a slot from its folder or every Azurik slot on an xemu HDD image (a qcow2, versions 2 and 3, or a raw disk; its E: drive's FATX, read-only), decodes each file (the container's fields, a text save's lines, a binary save's version and named word, the signature), changes a text save's lines all or none, signs the slot again with the console's key, derived in memory from the player's own flash image and MCPX boot ROM over the game XBE's title key (AzurKit holds only the certificate key's fingerprint), and writes it as a new folder or a zip whose bytes are the same on every call. The reader keeps SEC-12's checks: a visited set and a chain capped at the volume's clusters, a folder read once and at most 4,096 records, the header's fields checked against the file's size, a compressed cluster, a backing file and an encrypted image refused by name, and every name a save holds checked once (no separator, `..`, NUL, control or device name), so a zip's entries are the slot's own names. On the D98 oracle drive it reads the one slot in 7 block reads, and the game's own signature checks under the derived key. The hub half (Step 4.3r4q): `sdk.saves` (`tools/saves/session.py`), the Tools tab's Saves on both hosts (`web/saves.mjs`, `api/saves`), `azurkit save inspect|edit`; the independence contract holds five tools.
- **The developer side's home** (Step 4.11a; D950, D990): `devkit/`, beside `shimkit/`, where Modkit's developer side is ported area by area (the xemu oracle, the simulator, the RE and Ghidra tools, the MCP server, the randomizer's database tools, the reach engine, the model verbs). `uv run --locked python -m devkit AREA VERB` runs a verb; `devkit/README.md` says why none of it ships and where what stays out of the repository lives. The `devkit` dependency group (capstone 5.0.9, Unicorn 2.1.4) is installed by default and reaches neither the wheel nor the app; `tests/test_contracts.py` refuses an import of `devkit` from `src/azurkit`.
- **The XBE verbs and AZ-108's guard** (Step 4.11c; D950): `python -m devkit re xbe` reads a `default.xbe` offline, as Modkit's `azurik-mod xbe` did: `addr`, `sections`, `read`, `vtable`, `dis`, `fn`, `calls`, `insns`, `sse2`, `find-refs`, `find-floats`, `strings` and `hexdump` (a human's), on `core.xbe`, `core.iso` and capstone. It reads `--iso` or `--xbe`, else the image of `--region`'s release, and names the release on stderr; a listing names the addresses the release's facts know (its functions and kernel slots) until the Ghidra side brings the project's names. `devkit/re/README.md` is the reference, `tests/test_re_xbe.py` the tests. `tests/test_shims.py` gains AZ-108's guard: every RET a facts function reaches pops what its declared ABI makes its caller push, on both releases.
- **Contributors: the reverse-engineering notes** (Step 4.11g; D990): `docs/RE_XBE.md` (the XBE: sections, notable functions, kernel imports, calling conventions, the cheat UI, memory and threads), `docs/RE_MOVEMENT.md` (player movement), `docs/RE_DATA.md` (the game's data files) and `docs/SHIMS.md` (C shims and kernel imports) come over from Modkit's `docs/`, dev-only like the rest of the developer side. Their findings are unchanged; each pointer to a private evidence file is dropped or replaced by the step or sitting it names, and each doc's head says that a path under `azurik_mod/`, `scripts/`, `tests/` or `shims/`, or a link to a doc not yet here, names Modkit's tree, and so do its commands, environment variables and "this repo" claims, AGENTS.md winning where they differ (SHIMS.md's toolchain is Modkit's and isn't ported; RE_XBE's Ghidra setup arrives with Step 4.11d). Their function addresses, calling conventions, section bounds and Europe addresses were checked against `src/azurkit/core/facts.usa.json` and `facts.europe.json` wherever both name the same address, and agree (RE_XBE's `.rdata` now ends at its virtual size, 0x1A298F, not the byte before `.data`).
- **The xemu oracle's core** (Step 4.14a1; D108, D990): `devkit/xemu/queue.py` (the sittings' first-come tickets, the batch token that gives way to them, the owner-demo lease) and `devkit/xemu/warm.py` (a sitting's warm build states) come over from Modkit as they were; `devkit/sim/snapshot.py` is the Unicorn-free part of Modkit's `sim/l3.py` the harness uses (the guest RAM snapshot's file and its private store, the results writer, the addresses it halts at, the pad and the row), beside `obs.py` and `inputs.py`, reading the game's RAM layout and page tables from `azurkit.tools.randomizer.live`. Both harnesses share the slots' folder, the lease and the store. `tests/test_xemu_harness.py` holds Modkit's four queue and warm tests and three new pins on synthetic memory (the store and the snapshot formats, the observation, the pad); none launches xemu.
- `content.verify` and `content.install` take a host's `refusal` (an entry and its checked files in, why it can't be installed out), read from what landed before anything is written; with none, a built-in variant's recipe is read as before. A manifest entry may carry a `description`, one line of plain text of at most 200 characters, kept in the entry's state for its row before Install (Step S1, for Step 4.3k's community packs: DESIGN-community-packs D8 and its trust rules).
- **Lanes' developer-log bullets merge without conflicts** (Step 4.4dx1): `.gitattributes` gives `docs/DEVLOG.md` git's built-in union driver, so lanes that each add a bullet at a section's end rebase onto each other with no conflict, each bullet once, where every stack hit this file's conflicts and resolved them by hand. Union keeps both sides of a hunk, so a lane whose later commit edits its own bullet, rebased onto a main that moved, keeps the old line beside the new (twice this week): `scripts/devlog.py`'s `stale` names a release's two bullets with one bold title, or of one step with 60 % or more of their words alike (today's most alike two of a step share a third), and the lint set (`tests/test_contracts.py`, every gate's preflight and smoke set) and `scripts/land.py` refuse it. `tests/test_dev_tools.py` rebases lanes in a scratch repository on today's log. AzurKit has no `CHANGELOG.md`; a change to `.gitattributes` still runs the whole suite.
- **The changed-area gate runs the tests a stack added or edited again and again at CI's pace** (Step 4.4dx2): most CI rounds lost this week were tests a lane had just added or changed that failed only on CI's slow runners. Once a changed-area run's tests pass, `scripts/fullsuite.py`'s flake stage runs each test function the stack adds or edits against its base (`edited_tests`: a `def test*` whose source, decorators included, isn't the base's word for word; a helper's or fixture's change, a moved test and a deleted file add none) `FLAKE_RUNS` (5) times under `taskpolicy -b` and nice 19 (nice 19 off macOS) beside 4 busy loops at nice 10 (Step 4.4ci27's pace: the pure tier so took 39 min here, CI's 36), at `-n 2`, in every browser its parameters name. A failing run fails the receipt with each test it failed, the run and its output's last 40 lines, and no perf stage runs after it; the receipt's `flake` block keeps the tests, runs asked and done, and wall time. No run starts once the stage's time plus its longest run passes 600 s (changed runs took 190-506 s here), a stack with more than 20 such tests runs each once, and the block says so; the busy loops end themselves if the runner dies. A whole run has no flake stage; `scripts/land.py` lands a changed receipt that has one only once it passed (`fullsuite.flake_passed`).
- **The tracker's maps open any level before a seed is tracked** (Step 4.4u3): with no plan open, the Tracker view's Map picker (the world map, then each level by realm as the Table groups them, life, town and the airship their own rows) or a click on the world map opens a level's map as a plan's (floors, zoom, pan, Fit, exits, barriers and features with what they need), each spot ringed plain with the icon of the item the vanilla game puts there and its tip naming it; a click on a spot marks nothing. The spots come from the engine's tracker with no plan (`sdk.tracker({})`, `tracking.vanilla`: the same database and `Logic.vanilla` a plan's tracker reads, `tracker.spot_classes` and `tracker.realms` shared with it), so no image is read and the view isn't greyed; the page asks it once as the view first shows, and the app answers it outside the slot a typed seed's first view is read ahead in. A plan opened then shows the level browsed with the plan's own spots. `tests/test_randomizer.py`, `tests/test_site.py` and `tests/test_hosts.py` hold it, and the perf gate times Map's readiness and a level picked on it on both hosts (S70, S71, A85, A86).
- **PyPI's page links the site, the source, Issues and the developer log** (Step 4.4ci51): a release's page there never changes after its upload, and `pyproject.toml` had no `[project.urls]`; it now names the site on GitHub Pages (Homepage, the app's `SITE`), the repository (Source), its Issues and `docs/DEVLOG.md` on `main` (Changelog). `tests/test_wheel.py` reads them back from the built wheel's metadata, with README as its description.

### Changed
- **A worn body's combo chains only on a hit by default, and `"chain": "any"` chains on any press** (Step 4.3 chain option, D1045): a character's new `chain` field, `"hit"` (the default; a link, mods file or setup without it reads so) or `"any"` (refused by name otherwise), carried by share links, mods files and the setup. By default every body, every mode and every variant keeps the game's stunned-target gate on `attacks_transitions` rows 28-31, so its second to fourth attacks follow only a hit, as Garret's; `"any"` clears it on any body, Garret's own and Skin only too (Step 2.153's D432 rule, which every swapped body had before). X18 (`light_hit.c`) now also stuns the hit of an attack set's first three attacks, read from X36's state, and installs after X36: 21 of the 183 bodies (air_elemental, beamlord, the shards, the water tentacles, spideyzar, the six male farmers, the four booblords) had sets whose attacks never stunned, so their combos could not have gone on. X37's drain's hit, a platform's at the game's level 2, cleared the stun its swing had just given (beamlord's set 2 restarted at his drain): X37 now names its target to X18 while it sends it, X18 gives it the level the player's own hit in that column gets, and X37 then holds off that critter for the rest of the window, so the drain still hits it once a window.
- **The randomizer reaches by its compiled kernel** (Step 4.3 native reach; D1009): every reach the fill, the spoiler and the tracker ask runs `native/reach.c` through `core.native` (an option set's tables built once from its rules, about 0.02 s; a trace's spheres replayed from the kernel's rounds), on the app's CPython and by the side module from the wheel in the site's worker; the Python twin runs under `AZURKIT_NATIVE=0` or with no library. Every plan is the same (`rando_plans.json`, RS1's 80 plans, `tracker.json`, G3's seed cases); seed 2's fill on this Mac 1.66 s to 0.26 s, one reach of its plan 80 ms to 11 ms. R2.1's resumable escapes are gone (the twin is the fallback). The perf gate fails a run whose engine answers without the library its worktree ships (`sdk.catalog`'s `native`).
- The perf gate times the site's Save in two parts: the page's part, the click to the browser's download starting, which must be within 1 s or show its own progress, and the browser's write of the file, which the browser's own download indicator shows; the whole Save still answers to 3 s (Step 4.3 train B2).
- The Characters tab lists the community models under the species, beside Content from, their Install and Update buttons as they were (Step 4.3 characters tab).
- A seed's build leaves its spoiler out (`sdk.image(spoiler=False)`), so it shows 1-2 s sooner: the site's worker sends the spoiler after the build (and replaces a worker the page doesn't keep only then), and the app's engine makes it from the plan it kept at the first View or Save (`api/spoiler` `{}` gives its text, `{"out"}` writes it) (Step 4.3m).
- **The suite runs on 8 workers** (Step 4.3o, D991): pytest-xdist 3.8.0 in the dev group, and `scripts/fullsuite.py` runs every gate with `--numprocesses=8 --dist=loadgroup`, the frozen app's tests in one group (one freeze a run), its cap 1,800 s (from 6,000); D26's stop holds under `-n` (a worker hands the controller its message, exit 4) and the controller still prints `vanilla: N run`, `europe: M run` and `web: K run`. The same 250 tests pass: the whole suite took 564 s on 8 workers against the serial gate's 4,465 s. G3's body and KIT cases build in 4 processes, and `no_content` sets `AZURKIT_CONTENT`, so those processes read its empty folder, not the user's content.
- The perf gate times the click's wait (D1006, D1014; Step 4.3q): a Track or Build after a change the player makes (a typed seed, the Randomize tick, an image pick, a model's pick, a slider) is clicked 3 s after it, the clock starting at the click (A12, A15, A20, A23, A26, A27, A31, S05, S14, S18, S23, S24); a freeze probe in the page fails a sample whose page froze over 250 ms in a gap between actions (a head start, the save dialog's wait, the warm-up's) and logs the lateness inside each action's own wait (`late_ms`, `gap_ms`); A29 cancels a build that really runs (seed "2", at its first stage record) before its timed Build; S18 and S20 race Track's click's done-states, and S19 times the Tracker view's world map from the view's own click (a tab's open, so no head start), so a slow first draw shows in it; the site's samples each take a persistent browser context on a new folder (WebKit's ephemeral one has a 1 GiB quota and no OPFS); `tests/_perf.py`'s `Driver.state`, `warmed` and `change` are the helpers later lanes' warm twins use.
- **An image's check holds only the runs it is hashing** (Step 4.3r4-train5): the site's checks (the pick's, Verify every file, a Save's) free each run's buffer as soon as its digests are taken instead of leaving it to a GC, so WebKit no longer keeps every run already read resident; a keylord Save's peak on the site (capped-site with S49) fell from about 6.0 GiB to about 4.8 GiB.
- **A build's bar moves through its last stages** (Step 4.3r4a): `sdk.image` hands `edits.image` and the randomizer's read-back its counters, so laying out the image (each file composed and hashed) and reading it back (each level file) count to their whole on both hosts, each a cancel point, instead of sitting on one line after the plan. No byte changes.
- **G4 match shows its dress moving** (Step 4.3r4a): `sdk.match` takes `progress=`, the dress tells its five serial steps ("the dress, k of 5", `core.progress.note`: a cancel point each) and the app's engine counts the swap's items inside each step, so its bar never goes back. A build's dress runs outside `noting`, so a build's records and bytes don't change.
- On the site, a build's written pieces become Blobs in the worker as soon as their bytes reach JavaScript, so the worker holds one piece's bytes at a time and the page none: a seed's build peaks about 0.5-0.8 GiB lower in the browser's tree [one sample], with the same bytes saved (Step 4.3r4b, L2).
- On the site, a new engine's first read of your image reads its folders' tables ahead, a depth at a time, in awaited reads (Safari answers each read of a File about 200 ms late, so the engine's 34 one-at-a-time reads took 6.9 s); the pages stay in the read cache where the engine's own walk finds them, and a kept image's page list comes with its stamp, so a reload's pick takes one round (Step 4.3r4b, R2.5). A hint only: the engine still reads and checks every byte, and a failed read or a damaged table is left to it.
- The site ships each release's folder-table pages (`web/tables.json`, recorded by `scripts/web_tables.py`), so a first walk of a release's image, and of an AzurKit build on a release's root, reads them in one round after the descriptor's, not one round a depth: 2 rounds, not 7, under Safari's slow reads (Step 4.3r4b, L3). Offsets only, a hint: a key that misses falls back to the walk, and the engine still reads and checks every byte.
- **A dress keeps no level it only scanned** (Step 4.3r4c, round 4's capped-app lever): `model_inspect.placed_species` is worked out once a release (`Vanilla.release_key`; files handed over as a mapping, which nothing checked, answer for themselves) and reads each level through `Vanilla.peek`, so the 850 MiB of levels stay out of `Vanilla`'s cache through the dress. No byte changes: every file of a `Vanilla` from an image is that release's (D27), so the answer is the release's.
- **`$AZURKIT_CORES` caps the pool's children across the process** (Step 4.3r4c, round 3's L7): a `later`'s call shares the cores with the call beside it (`procpool._BUSY`), and a finished child is idle at once for it, where a dress ran 6 of them at 4 cores and spawned 7. A call that starts while others hold cores splits over what is left and never waits: with none free it takes one child past the cap, so G4 beside a dress at 4 cores answers in 3.27 s, not 18.75 s. So it is the capped profile's honesty and up to 0.3 GiB at a dress's peak, not a time saving. No byte changes: a call's results are each item's whichever child ran it.
- **A pool child gives its heap back** (Step 4.3r4c, round 4's capped-app lever): `app/procpool.py`'s child holds none of a part's inputs or results while it waits, and one whose peak resident size has reached `RETIRE` (256 MiB) sends its results and leaves, as only an exit returns that heap (a collection or the allocator's trim returned at most 2 MB a child); the next call takes a fresh child, about 0.3 s for 4. The peak is `getrusage`'s on macOS and `VmHWM` on Linux, whose `getrusage` starts a spawned child at its parent's peak. Windows has no peak to read and keeps its children: a named flaw. No byte changes: a call's results are each item's whichever child ran it.
- **A whole file's check over the ranges it shadows runs compiled** (Step 4.3r4d4): `edits.layer.compose` counts the bytes of earlier range writes a whole file doesn't hold by `core.native.runs` (keylord's build compared about 3.6 million bytes one by one), its first byte and count as before.
- **A dress's clip and skeleton math is compiled** (Step 4.3r4d4; D1009, AM-D4): `native/dress.c` runs `retarget`'s engine model (each bone's world rotation and origin over a clip's times, one call for many), `bake_clip`'s rows on its linear and sampled paths, the damage boxes, the crossfade's pose and heights, the jiggle's skinning and skin distances (a reshape's) and the character layer's byte runs, through `core.native` on both hosts; their twins answer without the library, bit for bit (every NaN a NaN). In one window against main: keylord's build 3.23 to 1.76 s serial on CPython and 7.01 to 4.30 s in Pyodide on Node (its dress about half), S14 at the click 7.09 to 3.11 s in WebKit, 5.63 to 3.38 Chromium, 8.58 to 4.88 Firefox, 8.30 to 6.23 under lag; a reshape 101 to 88 s pooled, 183 to 131 s serial; every byte as before.
- The perf gate times the site's engine ready again after a keylord build and a seed's (R14, R24): the wait the next op sees, on the same worker or its replacement (Step 4.3r4f).
- On the site, every engine after the first starts from a memory snapshot of the one before it (Pyodide's, once the engine's modules are imported; no numpy, no image), kept in the browser by Pyodide's version and the wheel's integrity: a return visit and the worker after a big build skip CPython's start, a restore of a heap past Pyodide's initial memory grows it first, the Tools tab's Storage lists it and any fault starts cold, as `ready`'s `start` says (Step 4.3r4f, R2.10).
- The perf gate's site build samples also keep the steady memory, the heap the engine reported plus the build's pieces, beside the run's resident peak, which stays the gate's line (Step 4.3r4f, D17).
- On the site, a build's changed files and the bytes its root files hold are hashed by the browser's SHA-256 (WebCrypto, about 10x wasm's) instead of the engine's, one file at a time and each handed over only for its digest, through `sdk.image_staged`; the bytes are the same (Step 4.3r4f, R2.6).
- **Firefox holds no copy of a site build, outside a private window** (Step 4.3r4ff, D1057): the 7.27 GiB it logged was not its download buffer: a blob: URL of a Blob of many parts makes its parent copy the whole image into memory at the URL, and hold about 2 GiB more over the download. The page now gives Firefox a Blob of at most 7 parts it reads by reference: the three longest copies stay the File's slices, all between them goes once into one temporary-file Blob ahead of the Save, Save greyed with its own moving bar until it opens, so a click never waits. Same bytes; the tree over keylord's Save 7.73 to 2.56 GiB, a seed's 7.51 to 2.78. A private window has no temporary file for it, so it keeps the plain Blob and its parent's copy.
- A randomized Build's tracker holds that build's exact plan (a worn body's size fold included, D1043), and marks stay with the plan they were made on: those still on their way when the new plan opens too, and a same-plan rebuild keeps its marks and the map shown (Step 4.3r4hub).
- A serial dress records each stage (Step 4.3r4i, AM-D2 with C16): the mesh, the springs, the paint, the springs' clips, the LODs, the shadow and each of `pool.each`'s items run in the caller are `core.progress` notes, each a record and a cancel point, none moving the bar, for a call that folds notes: `sdk.match`'s dress, a G4 match stopped sooner where it runs serially (the five counted steps were 4.3r4a's). A build's dress, the only one a job ahead runs, folds none (`sdk.image` holds no `noting` round it), so these drop no job ahead sooner: its largest record gap stayed 26.9 s serial and went 30.4 to 26.0 s pooled with the fold added, which also left a serial record's percent empty between ticks, so it isn't wired, and a stale first dress in that gap takes `ahead.GRACE`'s kill (a known flaw). A note in a child is nothing, as before.
- A kept dress is `AZKP1`, not a pickle (Step 4.3r4i, AM-D2, cache I5): `characters._kept` keeps a plan zstd'd against the vanilla files at level 3 on the store's writer thread, after the answer (booblord's about 7.9 MB and 0.26 s, a pickle's about 110 MB), and a size as its hex, so reading a dress runs no code. They stay in the `swap` folder until the hub's turn moves `engine._kept`'s and the Storage row with them. The store's `put` takes what makes its bytes; a get before the writer makes them itself, once.
- The pool's serial path works a later out when it's asked (Step 4.3r4i, AM-D2's SP): with no host thread (the site, the CLI) `pool.later` pickles its arguments and runs at its first `result()`, so a cut started ahead and never taken (a LOD 0 that changed) costs nothing. No byte moves; the app's later is a child, as it was.
- A reshape's parent's places ship (Step 4.3r4i, R3-CP1, X11): `shipped.json` gains a `places` row (booblord's jiggle bones at her defaults, one row: USA's and Europe's `characters.xbr` are one file), which `_variant_body` takes in place of a child's `_parent_places` where it stands on her specs, her content and the staged `characters.xbr`. The table names its recording host, and off it (D1016) the pin and `record_shipped.py --check` hold a size and places within 8 float32 steps and a plan to what it stands on, and a build takes a fresh size close to a shipped one. `shipped.py` and its recorder, no byte of her build moved (`usa-72ca94916d6d`, `europe-fbd58d2351b0` as they were; her content entry owes nothing).
- **A seed's build reads only each level's TOC and node sections, on both hosts, and the site's Save is checked** (Step 4.3r4k; the amortize plan's AM-HUB-E): `sdk._planning` hands a seed's levels to the build as `generator.sections`' views and the judge reads them back with `sparse`, and the site's worker reads what it needs in two awaited rounds (`sdk.image_staged`'s `ahead`; `PAGES` 160), then each level with its patches over it for its sha256 (WebCrypto) and md5 (wasm, `web/hasher.mjs`, `native/web/md5.c`), so the levels never enter the engine's heap. While the browser writes the Blob, the page hashes the same Blob's files against the build's (`sdk.image_checks`) with its bar moving, and in Firefox, whose reads of a changed File are silent, the File's copy of each changed file against the release's first; a mismatch fails the Save by name (D35; it closes Firefox's silent read of a File changed on disk, before the build as after it). New ops S49 and S50 time a site Save after keylord's and a seed's build, and Firefox's whole Save passes on its moving bar (D1053). The bytes don't change: goldens untouched.
- **An Install's list doesn't wait for the image's check** (Step 4.3r4m): on the site, while the image picked is unread, the Characters tab asks the engine twice, once with no image, which overtakes the check in the worker (it waits on the File and WebCrypto, the engine idle), and once with it: the list and the model's panel show now, its weapons and G4 say Reading until the image's answer, and a build still waits for that answer.
- **The perf gate sees a twin's call** (Step 4.3r4n, AM-N1w's review item 6): `core.native` counts the reach calls each path answered and `native.report` shows them (`calls`), the app's engine process handing its counts to the server and a site's worker to the page before each answer; a run whose twin answered any call beside a loaded library fails, so a per-call fallback (`Tables.twin_only`, a number past `LIMIT`) or an engine that loads apart from its server can't hide. `tests/test_native.py`, `tests/test_dev_tools.py`.
- **The site's Save stays a Blob in every browser** (Step 4.3r4p; D1049, D1053): W-SAVE measured the build's Blob streamed through the service worker (a hidden frame's download, its last bytes held for the check, a real bar) against today's Blob download, keylord's and a seed's Save three times in each browser: slower in Chromium (2.5-2.7 s against 2.1) and Firefox (5.7-6.8 s against 3.0-3.6, its processes growing to 8-26 GiB over the Saves against 3.5-4.7), within the spread in WebKit, so by D1053's rule (ship whichever is faster or lighter) the Blob stays and no stream path ships.
- **The service worker answers with each file as it keeps it** (Step 4.3r4p, review F1; D835, D985): `web/sw.js` keeps every file of the release with its isolation and CSP headers and answers with the cache's own response, never rewrapped, so the browser reads it from the cache itself: Chromium's return visit (S02) 1.279 s to 1.141 s over three interleaved pairs, level with the lane's base without the service worker (1.138 s against 1.117 s, within the pairs' noise); WebKit and Firefox unchanged. A file an older `sw.js` kept under the same release takes these headers at the next install.
- The Characters tab draws each model in the first frame of its idle, not its T-pose: a townswoman's vase on her head where her clip keeps it there and out of sight where it doesn't, garret4's staff in his hand, Booblord still without one; a variant's in its recipe's idle. The shipped pictures are recorded again (D1042, Step 4.3r4pic).
- **The site's image check says which await it is in** (Step 4.3r4t): `web/worker.mjs` posts `{type: "at", where}` as its check reaches each await (the image's id, the kept stamp, the tables' reads, the engine's layout and prime, keeping the stamp; numpy; the job's own call) and `web/hasher.mjs` says where each of its three hashers is (run k of n read at its offset in MiB, digest, done), relayed from its worker; the pool keeps it as `state()`'s `at`, so a browser test's wait that times out (`_page.until`) names it, after Firefox's characters tab waited 300 s in two gates with only "the check's last message was a progress".
- **A character's build parses config.xbr's tables once and checks each change's kind once** (Step 4.3r4t, D1067): `core.xbr.document` keeps each keyed table's parse by its payload's sha256 (a keylord build parsed 180 tables, now 25: 0.34 to 0.09 s on Pyodide) and `edits.layer.compose` checks a change against its group's first member (3.59 M comparisons gone: 0.17 to 0.015 s); keylord's build 5.02 to 4.58 s on Pyodide and 1.67 to 1.43 s on CPython, Build keylord at the click under Safari's read lag 3.58 to 3.01 s, the bytes as before.
- **A browser test's wait that times out says what the page was waiting on** (Step 4.3r4t): `tests/_page.py`'s `until()` adds the page's open asks (kind, stage, percent, age), the pool's workers (each one's job, heap and last message with its age, now in `workers.mjs`'s `state()`), Save's check, the status line, and the last page errors (uncaught exceptions and unhandled rejections) and console errors since the page opened, names and numbers only, after two gates' unexplained timeouts (WebKit's build file test at Save's check, Firefox's characters tab).
- **The four Booblords' notes are short and for players** (Step 4.3rv-chars, D1061): no ids, quotes or build talk, here and in Azurik-Custom-Content's booblord v5. A note now draws nothing and misses nothing: `record_goldens.recipe_keys` leaves out a recipe's title and notes, and `shipped.entries` leaves out the entry's `variant.json` (no dress reads it; Install holds it to the built-in's sha), so `shipped.json` was re-recorded once and an installed v4 still takes the shipped answers.
- **"Garret again" is "Reset"** (Step 4.3rv-chars, D1061, D1062): the sliders, options and weapon go back to the chosen model's defaults and you stay on it; picking Garret in the list clears everything too, so no second button is needed.
- **The Weapon box lists every weapon before an image, and a pick no sitting saw says "(not tested)"** (Step 4.3rv-chars, D1061; narrows D979 for this box): `picker.view` with no image gives all five, a pick kept, and a model's words become "Should play, not tested", "Rough edges, not tested" or "Not tested" in place of "Fully playable; not seen in game". Only the two structural refusals stay (a carried weapon on a recipe with spring bones, Balthazar's graph on a recipe that edits its own fx).
- The tracker's and the Randomizer's item icons are the game's own art (Step 4.3rv-icons, D1061): one sheet, `web/art/<ART_VERSION>/icons.png` (59 tiles at 40 px, `art/icons.py`, the art family): interface.xbr's status pictures for each of the 20 disc fragments, the 4 gems, the obsidian, each of the 16 placed keys (its medallion) and the 4 element orbs, the game's models for the items with no 2D art; `web/art/icons.css` names the tiles for the lists, the map's canvas draws them too. Spots not shown keep their dot, the map's gem dots stay, the fire barrier and smash wall stay our drawings. The 4 large element fuels, which showed a blank icon, have theirs.
- The memory packs settle themselves (Step 4.3rv-memory, D1061): `mods.settle` is one rule for a tick, an untick, `--pack`, `--no-pack`, a mods file and a link: a pack takes what it needs, loses what needs it, and each move says why in a line (the Patches tab beside the group, with each row's Needs; the CLI before the layers). Only `--pack X --no-pack what-X-needs` stays refused. Direct recipes stay strict (D35). qol_memory_upper256 now raises a body's limit to 127 MiB where it is ticked: a recipe with no `ram` takes the lines of what its own XBE carries, not always 128's (the page and the CLI never sent one), and `ram_packs` went unused. Refusals name the ticks, not Modkit's `--ram`. Default builds' bytes are unchanged.
- A skip category's tick on the Randomizer tab turns on all its skips, whatever their rating (D1061, amends D258's tier filter); unticking one shows the category half-ticked, "6 of 7". A box is derived from what's on, so a loaded setting's categories show too. Toggles are written only where the ceiling differs, so the same picks give the same share and plan; the skip ceiling is unchanged and toggles win (Step 4.3rv-rando).
- The Randomizer tab's specific skips are folded shut by default under a count, "Specific skips · 3 of 36 on", in their categories' order under thin headings; a category's tick opens them, so you see which it turned on (D1061, Step 4.3rv-rando).
- The Randomizer tab's skip groups: Out of bounds (3 turnable) and Super jumps (4) are separate groups (D1061, amends D258's five); a group is the page's only, so no share string, plan or forcing table moves (Step 4.3rv-rando).
- **Moved movement sliders pick a tier, not a re-checked logic** (Step 4.3rv-sliders, D1064, D1066, sliders.txt's option (b)): a share string's or a build's movement sliders now pick the movement tier the seed is filled at, the one whose reach is nearest the asked tier's times their travel factor k, read from a shipped table (`data/slider_tiers.json`, which `scripts/slider_tiers.py` makes from the physics; plain arithmetic, no search or libm, so every host agrees), and the plan is that tier's seed, its digest included, its file keeping the share string as given; nothing is refused, and the first moved-slider plan takes what a tier's fill takes (A74: no bar). The tab's Seed difficulty levels follow the played tier: a level is offered only where the asked and the played tier both offer it, with the played tier's line and note. A seed with any gameplay slider moved (movement, enemies, max HP; not the QoL ones) isn't promised beatable: its spoiler, the tracker's head, the Randomizer tab and a build's first reason say so, and `logic_verified` now means every slider at its default and the plan verified (the page says "promised beatable"). The roll, climb and swim feed no tier (their roll warnings stay); non-movement sliders never touch the logic. Gone as dead (D10): `movement.recheck` and its per-flight re-check (`logic_for`, `logic_of`, `run_floor` and AZ-606's fall-back to garret4's run, `required`, `unjudged`, `weaker`, `taller`, `SafeRange`), `layer.plan_for`'s `--plan` path, `floor_reason`, `unjudged_reasons`, `unreachable`, Seed difficulty's slider rungs (`rung_logic`), `tracking.prime`, `loader.kept_for`, and the physics' certificates, seeded and exact envelopes. Every all-default plan, file and string is byte-identical (`record_goldens.py --check`, `rando_plans.json`, the 80 plans); an old share string with moved sliders still decodes and re-encodes unchanged and plans deterministically, but now gives its tier's seed, not the re-checked plan it gave before (AzurKit has no release, so no player holds one). G3's `seed=0,all` (Frame-perfect, gravity 4.9, walk 2, flicken) moves with it: `pipelines.json`, `resources.json` (its USA and Europe rows) and `close.json` (its `filelist.txt` frame) re-recorded; `europe/pipelines.json`, `rando_plans.json` and `tracker.json` unmoved.
- **The seed's fill about four times quicker, the same plans** (Step 4.3u, round 2's R2.1): the solver judges an escape by its verdict alone, walking one least fixpoint that goes on from where it stopped when a location or flag widens the state; a failed escape resumes at the next round of the same solve from its record; the fast path's backward search and the forward reach grow round to round rather than searching again; the fill keeps its reaches by what they read (the placed items a rule reads and the items held, so a filler item costs no reach) and the process's last known traps. Exact because the grammar has no negation, so every rule is monotone and the state only grows within a solve; `tests/test_randomizer.py` holds RS1's 80 plans (seeds 1 to 20 under four option sets: each attempt, plan sha256 and spoiler) to what the solver before it made, and every compiled rule to holding at any larger state. `rando_plans.json` and every golden unchanged.
- **A seed's build after its plan is about three times faster** (Step 4.3v; ROUND-2's R2.9, the amortize plan's AM-B1): `compose` keeps a file's range writes as runs, each its group's, until another group's write meets one (that file then goes on byte by byte, so the files and conflicts are the byte-by-byte ones), and copies a file's vanilla bytes once, not at each change; the XBR TOC cache holds every level file's, vanilla and built (16 -> 64); and the image a build makes keeps the md5 its layout hashed of each changed file (`iso.Image.md5`, given only for those bytes read back), so the randomizer's read-back hashes no level file again. Every image is byte-identical.
- CI's vanilla tier keeps its images, builds and temp folder on the runner's largest disk (Linux's `/mnt`), frees each passed test's folder and prints the free disk and memory each minute and their lowest (`scripts/ci_resources.py`), after Linux's tier was shut down at about 48 min twice with nothing in its log. The tests' quick bounds compare two sizes or the host's own speed (`tests/_speed.py`), and the pack golden opens as many pages as the host has cores.
- CI's tiers run on xdist's workers (Step 4.4ci6): `--dist=loadgroup` as the local gate, a number per runner from each one's memory (vanilla: Apple Silicon 1, Intel 2, Windows 2, Linux 1; pure: Apple Silicon 2, the others 3), and `--max-worker-restart=0`, as xdist 3.8 replacing a killed worker under loadgroup hangs the run to the job's cap with nothing printed.
- `release.yml`'s jobs ran without harden-runner while `tests.yml`'s vanilla tier blocks egress on every OS. Each now starts behind it, pinned as `tests.yml`'s: the jobs a dispatch's dry run reaches block every host but those their steps reach in the dry run's logs (run 41460058562) and behind `tests.yml`'s block (check: github.com and releases.astral.sh; the apps and the wheel: PyPI too; the site: GitHub's release hosts and jsDelivr for Pyodide too; sums: the artifacts' service, which harden-runner admits anyway), and attest, draft, pypi and pages, which no dry run reaches, only audit, so the first tag's run can't fail on a host nobody has seen (Sigstore, PyPI's upload, Pages); its insights name them for a block later. Permissions unchanged; `tests/test_contracts.py` holds each job's first step, its pin and its policy (Step 4.4ci9).
- The docs say what main does at the session's end (Step 4.4 docs): README's tracker opens within 3 s, the 5 GB store, prebuilt fast search, a dressed character's float digits by computer (D1016) and the four unsigned apps with their first-run steps (D1003, D1022); AGENTS.md's engine halves no host calls yet, the service worker unregistered, the live content source, cross-machine float bits, the perf gate held on this Mac on the 4-core / 8 GB profile (D1017, D1023), D1021's site memory line, nativekit's CI check and the change-note files; devkit's README says the repository is public, not released.
- **No private evidence cites** (Step 4.4pub2, the publish audit's F4): the randomizer's data (tricks.toml, route.toml and the level files' comments and notes) and the character modules' comments named 60 of the private evidence folder's step folders; each now says what it rests on in plain words (a sitting and its row, a step's read, the owner's demo and its trace), with no data value changed (`db_digest` and the plans' goldens unchanged). `tests/test_agent_files.py` refuses, in every tracked file, a cite of anything in the private folder but its tools (`$EV/tools/`), besides the home paths it refused, but w3's one barrier reason the plans hash, reworded with the database's next change. The sittings' ids that confirmations and proofs name (`confirmed`, `proven`, `xemu_ref`, contact.json's L3 refs) stay: they are data.
- **The randomizer owns its truth** (Step 4.12b, D987): the last sync from Modkit (oracle-rando-6, Modkit `8bfef0ca6`: Step 2.221's kept Logic and last fill) is the cut, and the randomizer's database, planner, writer, layer, tracker, Live and drawing are AzurKit's own since; `scripts/record_goldens.py` records the family's goldens from AzurKit's own planner (`rando_plans.json`: seed 2's plans and spoilers; G3's seed cases in `pipelines.json` and `europe/pipelines.json`; `tracker.json`: the tracker's verdicts and the maps' model `web/mapview.mjs` draws of them, under Node), first recorded equal to Modkit's; `scripts/oracle_sync.py`, `tests/oracle/` and `tests/test_oracle.py` are gone, so the suite no longer reads Modkit's checkout; AGENTS.md's "Porting from Modkit" holds the cut, the ported paths and how a later Modkit result is ported by hand. The wheel and the frozen app take their shipped database's Logic as the planner's kept one (the loader's `SHIPPED` hook), so their first plan or track builds none.
- **A shaped seed's plan takes a third of the CPU it did, the same plan** (Step 4.4bk; D1107): `native/reach.c` walks its sets a word at a time, an escape reads only the locations and credits of the nodes it has seen (each graph's location rows by node, `G_LBN`, in the tables), the backward search works out a credit once its node is asked, and on tables whose takes never make a rule false (`H_MONO`, `core.native._monotone`: the randomizer's own are) an escape ends at the first node of the round's backward search it reaches, and the searches from the start (R, the backward one, R_phys's and the trick-free closures) are kept across a call's rounds, each asking again only the edges it was refused; a round reads only the location rows of its safe nodes not yet collected; ABI 2. A call packs its long rows as `array('q')` buffers, decodes a new set's ids at C speed and sums its takes' growth only past a bound; `Logic.reach` validates an option set once, checks a placement by whole sets (its spots listed only to name a refusal) and hands a tracker's collected spots to the kernel, which takes their items first (`native.solve`'s `take_got`, its twin's too); `writer.graph_of`, `Names.target` and `grammar.parse` (an option set's rules parse the same few thousand texts) keep their answers. The answers are the twin's bit for bit (`tests/test_native.py`, random tables made monotone too, under Pyodide as well) and the shaped plans' 7,761 recorded reaches the old kernel's: their C 16.8 -> 2.2 s, the wrapper's CPU 234 -> 94 us a call; a cold shaped plan at the engine's width 2.3-2.7x less CPU (A71's 6.1 -> 2.3 CPU-s), each plan's sha256 as before.
- A worn body's build on the site reads English.xbr ahead before a dress that reads its voices (Step 4.4bi, D1107): the dress reads them from that whole file, which no ask ahead held, so the worker read its 36 MiB in the middle of the dress as three 16 MiB blocks one after another, each 200 ms late in WebKit and the perf gate's lag model; now `sdk._planning` asks for it once the layers under the dress are known, when `characters.reads_voices` says the dress reads them: it runs (no shipped row stands on the build and the browser kept no answer of it) with the body's own sounds, not garret4's. A kept or shipped dress reads no voices and asks for none: asked with the body's files, as first built, they cost the kept and shipped builds their reads (S64/lag 0.06 -> 0.44 s in one A/B). A reshape's build asks as before. At the gate's command beside other projects' load (ABBA, 6 takes a side): S14/lag, keylord's first build, 3.31 -> 2.89 s; S15, S49 and S64 in the lag model, WebKit, Chromium and Firefox, and S14 in those three, unchanged within the box's spread. The image is the same (`tests/test_characters.py`'s staged body build test: its asks, its reads of those files in what it asked for, the kept build's, and `sdk.image`'s image and report).
- **The site's Save hashes only what the page hasn't hashed from the picked image** (Step 4.4ba, D1107): in WebKit and Chromium, which refuse every read of a File changed on disk since its pick, a Save skipped no file and hashed all 2.75 GB of the image's 1,195 files at every Save (2.8-3.5 CPU-s), slowing the browser's own write of the download beside it. The worker now tells the page the sha256 of each file it hashed from the picked File, by the image's id: the build files at the pick's check, then the rest (1,151 files, 1.63 GiB), hashed once an image in the background once the check's ask is answered, by one worker at a time (a Web Lock), and kept with the stamp, so a later visit hashes neither. A Save skips a file only where the Blob's bytes are the File's own at the file's own offset (the build's segments) and the file sits there in the input too (`sdk.image_checks`' rows end with that offset), with the sha256 the page knows equal to the expected one; so the files a build writes or moves (its changed files, a rewritten `filelist.txt`) are hashed at every Save, as is anything a joining bug would misplace, and one read of the File beside them fails a Save by name once the image changed on disk. The one detection given up: a file edited in place with its modification time put back reads silently in WebKit and Chromium (the pick's kept stamp already trusts that id across visits). Firefox, whose reads of a changed File are silent, hashes every file as before. The site's work ahead and a built seed's Tracker view wait while a Save is checked. The saved image is byte for byte the same; `tests/test_site.py` holds the hashed bytes and the failure by name in all three browsers.
- The site's image checks read ahead in Safari (Step 4.4bg, D1107): where each read of a File waits (WebKit; the perf gate's lag model's 200 ms whatever its size), each of an image check's three lanes (`web/hashworker.mjs`: the pick, Verify, a Save's) keeps its next run's read in flight while it awaits or digests one, so the waits overlap: under lag (ABBA, n 6 a side, every take) the pick S03 3.64 -> 2.48 s, Verify S41 1.91 -> 1.46, the Saves S06, S49 and S50 2.9-7.6 -> 1.9-2.5 (each window's medians), the keylord build S14 3.55 -> 0.72. A lane holds two runs at most (capped-site's peak 4.66 -> 5.20 GiB). A build's hash of the files it changed (`web/worker.mjs`, beside Pyodide's heap) still reads one run a lane: reads ahead there made a seed build (S24) 0.31-0.39 GiB heavier at capped-site's peak for no time. Chromium and Firefox keep one read a lane: Chromium's browser process reads the File for a Save's download as for its check, and its Saves took 0.4-0.6 s longer with reads ahead. The digests are the same.
- The skip table ships apart from the randomizer's logic, and the logic's read is counted (Step 4.4bd): the shipped database's file holds its skip table (about 14 KB) ahead of the database, so a launch's restore of a remembered randomizer and `api/setup` check its settings against that table and never read the 87.6 MB logic (the app's launch about 0.3 s sooner): the app's server reads the logic ahead from its start instead, on D1008's intent (Randomize on, a tracked plan), the site's engine as its restore brings Randomize back on, before the page is ready (as the restore did), so a reopen's tracker doesn't read it in its click, and one caller at a time reads it, the others served that read, so the tracker's view or the Randomizer tab after a launch doesn't read it twice; the logic is read as a stream, a `core.progress` note each twentieth of its file, which the Randomizer tab's first ask shows as a moving bar on both hosts (an ask once it is read shows none) and a plan file's spoiler counts; the loader hands the shipped database's key to its `SHIPPED` hook, so it is hashed once; the tab's open asks at once, without the 100 ms wait a setting's change keeps. No built byte moves.
- **A later engine worker on the site instantiates the wasm the page's first one compiled** (Step 4.4bf, the busy-box plan's L4): `web/worker.mjs` lets Pyodide compile its 9.6 MB `pyodide.asm.wasm` in the page's first worker only, which posts the Module; `web/workers.mjs` sends it first to each later worker (B after F's ready, a new B after a change, F after a big build), whose load answers Pyodide's own `instantiateStreaming` from it (a Module that won't instantiate compiles as before). On the busy box at the gate's nice 10, interleaved against main: S59/lag 2.93 s to 2.42 (medians, n 9 and 6), S61/lag 1.54 to 1.30, Firefox's S59 1.41 to 1.00 and S61 1.76 to 0.87 (n 3). No engine byte changes.
- **The site reads an image's id once a pick, not once a message** (Step 4.4bf, the busy-box plan's I): each message to an engine worker brings its own clone of the picked File, so `web/worker.mjs`'s `identify`, keyed by File, read the image's first and last MiB again for each, a read Safari answers about 200 ms late, on the path of B's build after a change. A worker now posts the id it read and `web/workers.mjs` sends it with each later message of the page's File, to every worker. The id is the name, size, time and sample read from that File at its pick, so a look-alike File is still read as itself; given up is a second read of the ends of a File changed in place after its pick (Firefox reads one silently): the build's and the Save's checks still hash every file they vouch for (D35), but not the bytes between files, the volume's descriptor and tables among them. With L4, interleaved against L4 alone: S59/lag 2.42 s to 0.92 (n 6), S61/lag 1.30 to 0.70. In Firefox, interleaved at the gate's command (n 12 a side, main 6): S59 1.13 s on L4 alone, 1.22 with I (main 1.37), S61 0.80 and 0.89 (main 0.78), no take past 2.2 s. With 20 more threads at nice 10 beside each run, as a fullsuite beside the gate's perf (one such run took 7.7 and 11.9 s on S59), S59 is 2.79 s on L4 alone, 2.74 with I and 3.34 on main: not I's, and still past the margin there.
- **A Save made while the image's rest is still being hashed waits for those digests** (Step 4.4bl, D1107): 4.4ba's background hash of the picked image's rest (1,151 files, 1.63 GiB) starts once the pick's check is answered, and a Save clicked before it ended hashed every file the page didn't know yet itself, the same files read and hashed twice at once. In Safari's read model the pick's check ends about 1 s before S06's click, so S06/lag waited on its check (1.46-1.79 s against its download's 1.27-1.59 s). The worker now tells the page while it hashes the rest (`resting`, with its progress, then `rested`), and a Save meanwhile hashes only the files the build wrote or moved and waits for the rest's digests for the others, its bar moving with the rest's; those the rest didn't vouch for (its worker stopped, a read refused) the Save hashes itself, so every file is still checked (D35). Interleaved A/B at the gate's command (ABBA, n 6 a side): S06/lag 1.45 -> 1.26 s, the download's own time; every other Save already ended on the browser's write of the download, its check done first, and is unchanged. The saved image is byte for byte the same.
- **A Save that waits for the image's rest keeps the rest from giving way to a dress** (Step 4.4stack2, D1107, D1073): 4.4ci25's background hash of the image's rest gives way to a character's dress and then reads one run at a time, and 4.4bl's Save waits for the rest's digests, so stacked, a Save made during or after a dress with the rest not done waited on the dress and then on one 128 MiB read at a time. The Save now holds a Web Lock (`azurkit-rest-waited`, `web/hasher.mjs`'s `WAITED`) while it waits, and the rest, seeing it, no longer gives way and reads as the Save's own hash would, its three lanes and their reads ahead started again; a Save that waits on no rest, the pick's check and Verify are unchanged, and the Save still checks every file (D35). Test: a rest that went on one run at a time after a dress reads six at once beside a dress held again once a Save waits on it.
- **A small pool call runs in the app's engine, not in 16 new children** (Step 4.4bh, D1107): the G4 match's one pool call (retarget's 35 clips, 1-3 ms each, 0.07 CPU-s in all) started 16 cold children for 2.6 CPU-s on the app's first dress. `procpool.map_` now runs a call with a `cost`, at the click's priority, that finds fewer children idle than it would split over here first, the cheapest item first, while the CPU seconds its items take (`procpool.CLOCK`: `time.process_time`, which a busy computer doesn't stretch; on Windows the wall clock, as its CPU clock moves only at each tick of about 15.6 ms, where an item under one read 0 and a dear one ran in the engine) plus the next one's guess by its cost, or the rest's floor at the least one's time each, stay under `procpool.SMALL` (0.25 s), and splits whatever is left as before; a call with no `cost` (its first item may be its dearest: paint's two bakes take about 9 s each) or a low one (work ahead, whose items here would hold the engine's GIL beside the player's ask: A30 was 0.07 s slower) splits as before. The answers are each item's in order either way (the match's answer is the same, sha256[:12] `ec264b012321`, here or pooled; keylord's and beamlord's builds with their small calls here are their goldens file for file). Timed at the gate's command (ABBA, n 6 a side): A11 0.24 s quicker in the app (median 1.36 to 1.12 s) and 0.13 s capped, with 2.5-3.9 fewer CPU seconds; alone it can lose the second bar value a slower take happened to show, so it lands on Step 4.4bb's bar polled from the click (with it, A11 shows 3-5 values and its median was 1.75 s against 2.41 s on a busier box). The plan's other half, warming the match's first imports, has nothing to move in the app: the keylord pictures before it import `_player_variants` in the same engine, leaving 2.4 ms of imports.
- **The changed-area gate runs the whole suite only for what every session runs** (Step 4.4dx3): `scripts/fullsuite.py`'s `plan()` sent 13 of the last 29 lane gates to the whole suite (12-15 min each) for four causes: `scripts/ci_resources.py` (7), which `tests/conftest.py` imports only in the `browser` fixture's close; `.github/workflows/` (5), a path no rule named; `src/` changes reaching `azurkit.tools.characters` (1; 3 a few gates earlier), which `scripts/web_tools.py` imports only in functions no hook calls; and conftest itself (3), twice only its `browser` fixture. `graph_of` now reads what each session runs: conftest's and its plugins' top levels and hooks and every function and module they name, however deep (a class, a decorated function, `globals()` or a module passed on reach a module whole; a `__main__` block runs nothing), and only that is whole. A conftest fixture is a node of its own, using what only its body runs, used by the test files that take it (a parameter, a string, a helper that takes it, a computed `getfixturevalue` name it can be); a change to a session module only in functions the session doesn't call (`parts`, `narrowed`) picks their importers and those fixtures' takers (a decorator or a computed default runs as its module loads, so a change to one is its module's: a fixture whose `autouse` goes ran for every test). `.github/`, `native/` and `src/azurkit/core/_native/` are folders read by path, picking their readers. The graph also resolves relative imports (`azurkit.app`'s: `test_memo` and `test_warm` were missed for an `engine` change), reads `devkit/`, `nativekit/`, `shimkit/` and the root, and takes a module run by its path as an import (`tests/_page.py` runs `tests/_static.py`, whose hand rule it replaces and which now picks `test_hosts`, `test_dev_tools` and `test_native` too), even once it is deleted; a rename is its old path deleted and its new one added (the receipt's diff no longer pairs them, which hid the old path's users), and a deleted module (not a test file) that no module still names is the whole suite, as is a Python file outside the graph's folders and the root (under `web/` or `.github/` too), which the graph doesn't read. Every changed-area run now also runs the scan of every tracked text file for a home path or an `$EV` cite (`SCAN`, 0.33 s), which reads whatever a change touches (a workflow, a kernel, a comment) and which the whole suite ran for each change narrowed here. Replayed on the last 40 receipts' change sets, each on its head's files: 4 lane gates whole instead of 13 (the release's `pyproject.toml`, conftest's new top-level constants, `ci_shards`, `.gitattributes`), no test the old rules picked for a changed-area gate lost, about 4,600 s of gate wall saved (510 s a gate, from the receipts that ran the same heavy files). Tests: each helper's picks equal the test files its imports reach, read apart from the graph; a conftest hook's change stays whole; a fixture-only one picks its takers.
- **The site reads community content from GitHub, or from any https repository a player sets** (Step 4.4ci52, D1135): its CSP's `connect-src` gains `https:` (scripts, workers and every other directive stay its own), so Azurik-Custom-Content's list, Install and Update work on GitHub Pages, read at the visit from `sdk.SOURCE` (raw.githubusercontent.com); Content from takes any https address with no warning, remembered until Reset, which goes back to GitHub's. `web/content.mjs`'s `where` refuses plain http elsewhere, an address that isn't one and a folder by name; a read that fails says what failed (no answer: offline, or a host that sends no CORS header; an HTTP status, 404's "no manifest.json there") and the page works on, the last list shown; an Install's file that doesn't come says which. The site's check is given its source (`sdk.content_check`), so an Update comes only from where the entry was installed, as the app's (SEC-8). What it fetches is data the engine parses and checks (recipes, models, pictures, sounds), never code it runs. The perf's site pages remember `./content-source/` (the clone it serves), so no op reads the network, and `SPECIES_LISTED` waits for the list's read to end with no line too. `tests/test_site.py`'s policy test pins the directives, and its released-site test lists, installs and builds booblord from a stand-in for GitHub on another origin, a player's own repository used and remembered, and each bad source and failed read in all three browsers.
