- **A typed seed's first tracker view is compiled while the engine plans it, a fill asks a counted item's spots once a graph, and a slow app launch shows its stages** (Step 4.4ci56; D1107): a Hard plan's first view, read by the app's server after the plan, spent almost all of its 0.46 CPU-s (1.1-1.4 s on a busy computer, after Track's click) compiling on the server's own Logic the option sets it reaches under; the plan's job ahead now compiles them there beside the engine's fill (`tracking.ahead`: the plan's settings, them with no skip, a shaped plan's rungs; `randomizer.tracker_ahead`), as low as the job (D1007) and raised once a click follows it, each compile waiting while another call is in flight in the server or a build runs ahead (their answers pass its GIL), and only on a Logic a call has read (`loader.logic_read`: a read of its own, 87.6 MB at low priority, held that GIL from a relaunch's calls, A02 to 9.2 s in the first A/B). A71's seed asked `_Slots.ok` 651,000 times, 628,000 of them for a counted item's copies, each rescanning every spot though a put changes only its spot and its graph's budget (`still`); a verify's sorted pool is kept a Logic, the database's digest a database and each data file's sha256 by its size and times (`dbcache.entry`, which every `kept_logic` call asks). The same plans (A71's sha256[:12] 8879c01e633e). host.json gives the server's age, and a launch it answers 0.5 s or more into shows "Starting AzurKit" at a third and two thirds until the page is ready (D980). The gate's command, two windows in either order at a normal-priority load of 14-35: A71 2.33 -> 1.96 s (n 9 / 12), A02 1.24 s with no progress -> 1.09 s with it (n 10); A80 1.89 -> 2.64 s (n 12 / 10), which the server's flow run alone doesn't repeat (its click 3.6-5.8 and 3.8-4.8 s, the same work). Tests: `test_a_shaped_plans_first_tracker_view_compiles_no_option_set_the_trackers_work_ahead_compiled`, `test_the_trackers_work_ahead_waits_for_a_logic_its_process_read_and_never_reads_it`, `test_the_databases_key_reads_again_only_a_file_whose_size_or_times_changed_and_follows_it`, `test_a_slow_app_launch_shows_its_stages_on_the_bar_and_a_quick_one_none`.

### Removed
- **The service worker's install no longer posts its progress** (Step 4.3r4p, review F2; D10): no page reads it since the site's first visit stopped waiting for the install, so `web/sw.js` posts only the install's outcome, `installed` or `failed`.
- The Randomizer tab's "Dark areas need" is gone (D1061, amends D144): a dark area always needs Fire 1, unless its own dark-room skip is on, then no fire. `dark_fire` stays in every settings table at 1, as `settings_digest` seeds every stream; default, max and every Fire 1 plan are byte for byte as before. A settings file, share string or plan file at Fire 2 or 3 is refused by name. The goldens' `strict` case moves to Fire 1 (Step 4.3rv-rando).

### Fixed
- A model picked in the Characters tab moves its bar through the view it waits on: `sdk.pickers` counts its body's Weapon box, each weapon's loadout a note ("the weapons, k of n"), where the view's time goes, so a pick no longer shows one value for its whole wait on a busy computer (S10-S12; Step 4.4be).
- The site's Tools tab says what your image is the moment it opens: the pick's own check brings the engine's info of a vanilla image with its sliders' values, so the tab asks the engine nothing (S40: its own ask was 0.5-0.9 s with no bar; Step 4.4be).
- The app keeps a reshaped character's dress for the next launch even when you quit the moment the build says Built: an answer its engine worked out for `swapcache.LONG` (10 s) or more, a reshape's size and plan (minutes), is written to the app's store before the call answers, where it was written just behind the answer and lost when the quit ended the engine first (its plan was made 0.3 s after it was put, about when Built came, and the quit came 0.13-0.18 s after Built, so a busy machine's slower write lost the race), so the next launch's same build dressed her again for 86-149 s instead of reading her in half a second (A32/capped); a quicker answer is still written behind, and Quit still never waits (Step 4.4bc).
- Booblord Human's and Booblord High Human's pictures lose the vase too: the recorder of the Characters tab's shipped pictures draws a look again when the look it is made from changes, not only when its own recipe does (Step 4.3 booblord vase).
- Booblord's pictures in the Characters tab no longer show her standing in a pink vase: the vase her base townswoman carries, which no clip of hers ever drew, is gone from all four of her looks' models (a recipe's new `mesh.drop`), with its vertices and triangles in both her levels of detail and, in her high-detail looks, its doubled texture (Step 4.3 booblord vase).
- AZ-834: an item the randomizer puts in an ice or acid block, behind a force field, in a gem rock or in a gate's region can no longer be picked up through it before it opens, whatever the item: the build gives an item the image holds once (the disc fragments, the air keys, the water power's third level) that reaches further there than the vanilla item the vanilla item's pickup collision in config.xbr's critters_engine (collisionRadius and collisionAspectRatio at its own scale), so it is touched, collides and rests as that item did; w2's IceBlock_01 now holds seed 110585's air fragment (2.2 m) to the obsidian's 1.45 m, and frag_air_1 (6.0 m) in f1's acid block is no longer taken from beside it or from the floor 14 m below. The fill and the plans are unchanged, an item held more than once (fuels, obsidian, gems, powers) keeps its own touch, and a later layer that writes one of those cells is a conflict that leaves the logic unverified (Step 4.3 contained items).
- The app's Characters tab answers a model chosen right after picking your image in a fifth of a second again: the randomizer's logic it reads at once no longer makes Python's memory collector walk it twice, holding the app for nearly a second (Step 4.3 train B2).
- The app's bar moves again through Track's seed fill, its items placed counted on it (never falling back when a seed's fill tries again), as it did before the randomizer's logic began to load at once (Step 4.3 train B2).
- The perf gate's seed pass no longer waits 30 minutes for a build it has already had: A30's pick (a small ask while a build runs) comes on a build of a seed no build has made, at its first stage record, and the pass waits for that build's own answer, as a build of a kept plan answers within the second A30 waited and the pick, a change, forgot it (Step 4.3 train B3).
- The engine's progress notes are one stream: the fill's count of its spots, the logic's checks and the maps' exits are each a `core.progress.note` that the call's `noting` folds into its stage (the sdk's own fold and the planner's `NOTE` hooks are gone), so a seed build's planning stage counts the fill's spots as a plan does; the fill's count runs once through its attempts, a retried one counting on from where the stuck one reached, and a note short of the furthest in its stage keeps that one's count, so no bar goes back (Step 4.3 train B3).
- The app's Track this seed shows its plan's progress from the click, as a build does (it waited 750 ms to ask, by when a quick fill had told its last count), and the fill's count moves with the time it takes: each item a reach places is a step and the rest, placed at once, the last, so the bar runs from a third to two thirds as the seed fills instead of sitting under 37 % (Step 4.3 train B3).
- The site's bottom bar never shows the image's check again once a build is ready: the engine's next answer ends the check's bar, where a build of two packs that finished within a moment of the check showed "Checking your image… 100 %" after it (Step 4.3 train).
- The app's kept G4 matches and character pictures are keyed by `sdk.py`'s code too, so an AzurKit whose dress changed, with no new version, never answers from what an older one kept (Step 4.3 train).
- The site's folder holds an empty `.nojekyll`, so a GitHub Pages build serves each file as it is and makes no page of a content file with front matter (Step 4.3 train).
- AZ-835: the item the randomizer puts on a1's frag_air_1 spot (a1/g0/r1102), which a wind carries the player past, is now taken as the fragment is: seed 1's obsidian was taken on 1 input of 87 and is now taken at the fragment's tick and place, so that seed's last obsidian no longer goes missing while the tracker counts it reachable. The spot's node names a spare pickup row (`small_chromatic_fuel`, which nothing else names) made its item with frag_air_1's reach, so the item's other copies stay as they are and no plan's bytes move; only that obsidian lacks the hum of within 3 m, which has no row to go in (Step 4.3az835).
- **The site publishes only checked content** (Step 4.3g; D992, SEC-3): `scripts/web_tools.py --site DIR --content CLONE` checks the clone before it writes the site's first file (`web_tools.content_files`): the manifest passes `content.check` and each entry `content.verify` (size, sha256, no disc image, a built-in's recipe), as an Install does; each listed path is a plain path (a trailing `/` or a `.` part is refused, so what is checked is what is published), has no `:` and is a regular file reached through real folders (`lstat`: no link is followed, so a listed link or a linked folder can't publish the deployer's files); its suffix is one of an allow-list, the kinds the content repository holds or is to hold: `.bin`, `.glb`, `.gltf`, `.json`, `.md`, `.png` or `.wav` (any other suffix, or none, is refused by name, so no page, script or XML document the browser renders, `.html`, `.svg`, `.js`, `.xml` or `.xsd` among them, reaches the site's origin); and each destination resolves inside `content-source/`. A fault names the file and writes nothing. It writes the bytes it checked, not a second copy of the clone's files.
- A transparently compressed image (`ditto --hfsCompression`, afsctool) saves its own bytes again: the clone a Save makes keeps the input's compression flag and drops only the others, so the file on disk is the build whose sha256 it reports (Step 4.3h).
- The site tells two images apart by a sample of their bytes too (the first and last MiB, the XBE's header among them), not only by name, size and time: a look-alike picked after the vanilla image is checked as itself and never read from the other's kept blocks, and a fresh check of the image is never answered by an older one (SEC-13, Step 4.3h).
- The site writes what this browser keeps installed into its engine file by file, each only where its path is one a manifest may list and installed.json holds its sha256: a planted `../` key, which once wrote over the engine's own Python, is left out with a line in the console and never written (SEC-2, Step 4.3h).
- The tracker's zoomed map no longer shakes with Live on: Follow leaves the map still while you move in the middle of the view and eases after you once you leave it, and Live's bar keeps its size from poll to poll, so a refresh never moves the map (Step 4.3 live tracker).
- Live no longer reads "loading" for good on a level whose one spot's counts don't read (their page moved or unmapped): it keeps that spot's last count, reads its attributes again and stays on the level (Step 4.3 live tracker).
- The tracker's Live puts your dot where you are again: it read the last point you touched the ground, which stands still while you jump, fall or swim (14 m off in w2), and now reads your body (Step 4.3 live tracker).
- When xemu runs another seed or build than the one tracked, Live's line now says how many of the level's item spots differ, names the tracked seed and share string and what to check (the seed, the settings, the packs, the body you wear), or that the image isn't randomized at all (Step 4.3 live tracker).
- The Randomizer tab folds the size of the body you wear on the Characters tab into its share string, as a build of it does, so Track this seed follows a character build's own plan and its share string loads back without hand-set sliders; a share string made for a character build names the body to wear (Step 4.3 live tracker).
- Track this seed plans a body smaller than garret4 as its build does: where no plan fills at the body's slower run, on his run, so it gives the build's plan, not "no plan" (Step 4.3 live tracker).
- The tracker's maps show each item on the floor of the room it stands in: the town temple's ten obsidian-lock rewards, on pedestals, showed six of them on the main floor, and a few spots and gems elsewhere too; the maps now also ship each level's floor table, which Live's Follow reads (Step 4.3 live tracker).
- The hosts' half of the content hardening (SEC-6, SEC-9, SEC-16): the app's and CLI's content fetch reads a file to at most one past its listed size (the manifest to its own cap) and refuses one past it by name; a manifest nested too deep or past its entry cap is refused by name through `content.check` (S1's), the hosts adding no catch of their own; the site's Install walks each entry once, refuses an entry past 256 MiB as listed and reads each file to at most its listed size; the app's three saves (the image, the spoiler, the settings file) share one `writable` check (never the image the build read or a vanilla image, never a file there unless its own dialog chose it), and each dialog's answer is its own (Step 4.3m).
- The Characters tab says "Reading your image…" while the engine reads an image picked after a file it refused, never "Choose your image…" meanwhile (Step 4.3m).
- The perf gate's app profile runs the app from the worktree's wheel in a scratch venv (`tests/_perf.py`'s `venv`; one wheel for the app and the site), so it reads the randomizer database the wheel ships as a player's install does, instead of checking it again as a checkout does: the Randomizer tab's first open (A06) timed 3.3 s that way by hand, against round 1's 7.8 s from a worktree (Step 4.3n).
- After Cancel, the app's next build no longer shares its engine with the image's pictures read: the new engine reads the randomizer's logic, builds, and only then reads the image ahead for the Characters tab, so "Cancel a build, then Build" lost about 0.3 s (2.8 to 2.6 s; Step 4.3 round 3).
- On the site, a model picked in the Characters tab shows its bar moving while the engine works: the engine tells its two steps (your image's model files read, then the models' view), so a pick made while the tab's first answer is still worked (a new engine's, about 1.4 s in Chrome) no longer waits with no progress (Step 4.3 round 3).
- Opening a build in the Tools tab reads its root and the folders on its changed files' paths, not every folder's table: on the site that was 34 of its 36 File reads, each about 200 ms late in Safari (7.4 s under the lag profile), now 3 (Step 4.3 round 3).
- The app's bar for a plan file's spoiler shows from the pick and follows the randomizer's logic read at once, as Track this seed's does: it asked for the progress only after 750 ms, so a spoiler ready at 1.1 s on 4 cores showed one value and no movement (Step 4.3 round 3).
- **The perf gate's site memory counts its own WebKit** (Step 4.3r4a): a WebKit process counts in the capped-site peak only when launchd started it for this run's browser (`launchctl print pid/N` holds the run's mark in its PATH), so another seat's WebKit, started meanwhile, stays out of the figure. Each process is known by its pid and start time, so a pid reused is neither taken for one running before the run nor answered from memory, and one launchctl can't say about is counted: never laxer.
- On the site, a first visit, a return visit and picking your kept image again show the bar moving while the engine starts and reads your image's folders: the worker tells the load's four steps ("Starting the engine") and the kept image's walk ("Reading your image"), on an ask of their own that ends at the worker's ready or stop, so S01, S02 and S04/lag owe no debt once M1 reads (Step 4.3r4b). No build byte changes; the only Python change is one more name in `core/progress.py`'s stages. `tools` (numpy's load) from the prototype stays out: `sdk.pickers` already tells a first pick's steps.
- The frozen Linux app carries its compiled reach library: PyInstaller's `--collect-data` skips every `.so`, so that app ran the Python twin with no error, and `--collect-binaries` would skip it too (only `lib*.so`); `scripts/freeze.py` now names this platform's library itself (`--add-binary`), refuses a platform with none, and `--check` (`apps.yml`, all four OSes) refuses an app whose report says it didn't load (Step 4.3r4e).
- The Pages site's service worker can install (Step 4.3r4j): `web_tools.site` builds its wheel in a temp folder, so `uv build`'s `.gitignore` no longer lands in the site and in `sw.js`'s release, where Pages' artifact, which drops dotfiles, left it a 404 that failed the worker's install.
- Update's trust root (Step 4.3r4j; SEC-8): `content.held_to` refuses an install that takes an entry below its installed version or from another source than its own (the site's check holds the version), no Update is offered for an older manifest, and a URL content source is https, plain http only on this computer, a redirect's end included.
- A character's build on the site no longer leaves about twice its pieces in the page each build (Step 4.3r4s, D1063): the engine handed each piece to the build file as two copies in the worker's JavaScript (through Pyodide's file system), as `placed` saw only a bytes object and a build's pieces are memoryviews of them, and WebKit kept those copies until a GC that a worker building again at once doesn't get to. A whole view of a bytes object is now written from the engine's heap, as Step 4.3r4l meant. booblord reshaped, built again six times back to back in one page in WebKit: +476-480 MiB a build before, +19-23 after; the perf gate's capped-site peak 6.07 GiB before (with the cut-build mode), 5.55 GiB after.
- A reshape on the site keeps one heap (Step 4.3r4s, D1021, D1054): its exact pictures are a heavy ask of the page's engine, run in the worker the page grew, not dressed in a worker of their own beside it (`web/dress.mjs`, gone), which took WebKit's own resident peak from 5.4-6.0 GiB at the build's end to 7.7-8.5 GiB (Chromium 6.0-6.1, Firefox 6.9-7.1); now 5.0-5.9 GiB in WebKit, 3.8-4.3 in Chromium, 4.7-4.9 in Firefox. The page's small asks go to the other worker meanwhile. The engine keeps the last reshape's size and dress (`sdk._reshape_kept`, no seed), so its pictures and its build make one dress between them, whichever comes first: the pictures after its build, or the build after its pictures, take seconds, not minutes. The perf gate's S62 and S63 build booblord reshaped on a fresh page and after her own build, then wait for its pictures; S65 builds the same reshape again in the page, strict (D999).
- The site's second worker no longer stops a heavy build ahead that a change leaves nothing heavy to replace (a model's build when a shape slider moves, Step 4.3r4s, D1054): it ends, its answer unkept, and the reshape's Build runs in the heap it grew, where stopping it left that heap resident in WebKit beside the reshape's own (a fresh page's reshape peaked at 6.07-6.14 GiB of its own memory so, 5.3-5.7 without).
- **The site's image check never waits forever in Firefox** (Step 4.3r4t): `web/hasher.mjs` now holds each hash worker it starts until its answer comes. Firefox collected the Worker object nothing held, and the messages its worker posted last (its digests) never arrived: about one check in 100 never settled (the image's check in a loop, 6 runs of 32 MiB: 3 of 340 calls, each at its last run; none of 300 with the worker held), which is what two full gates' Characters tab waited 300 s on after the USA image was picked. The page's Save check and Verify every file use the same function.
- **A reshape's pictures on the site no longer keep each shape's dress in the worker** (Step 4.3r4t, D1054, D1067): `web/worker.mjs`'s engine kept what a build worked out until the page's kept ask, which only a build wearing a character sends, so after a build with none (B's ahead build of the default recipe among them) each reshape's pictures in that worker kept its shape's size and plan (booblord's plan about 117 MB) until its next build. Any ask but a build's own now ends what a build kept. A probe of the engine's Python under CPython (a build with no character, then booblord's bust at 1.1, 1.2 and 1.3): 2, 4 and 6 dresses held, the process at 4447, 4541 and 4905 MB, before; none held after, at 4000, 4539 and 4085 MB.
- **The site's tests give each persistent WebKit context its own build files** (Step 4.3r4t): Playwright's WebKit kept OPFS in one folder of the user's Library for every browser it launched while each browser's Web Locks were its own, so one test's page load swept another's held build file (the gate on 3fd00b2 waited 600 s on a Save's check that had failed reading it; beside a second WebKit, the build file test failed 8 of 8 runs on the lane and on main). `test_site.Site` sets `CFFIXED_USER_HOME` to `<profile>-home` for WebKit, and the build file test waits for its Save's check to end, then asserts it held, so a failed check fails at once with its line.
- Replace files on the site reads a picked file as one stream and moves the bottom bar with the bytes read ("Reading <file>… N %"), so a 148 MB file's pick, past 1 s in Safari's read model, shows its progress (S54/lag had none); the stream reads as fast as one `arrayBuffer()` in the three browsers, and the entry's sha256 and size are the same bytes' (Step 4.3r4u, D980).
- **A G4 still measuring no longer lands after Reset or a new model** (Step 4.3rv-chars, D1062): its number was written into the box once the dress answered, so Reset didn't end at the defaults and Garret ended with another model's scale; a Reset, a pick or a setup set from a link now drops the answer. A typed number or option during a G4 still gets it.
- The Tools tab's Show its files toggles (Step 4.3rv-memory, D1061): a second click collapses the list, the button reading Hide its files while it is open; the filter's text stays.
- Seed difficulty's line under its select no longer claims the rate of a whole table cell for a mix with only some skips on, as a category's tick is (D1061): it says fewer seeds may meet the promise. No plan moves (Step 4.3rv-rando).
- CI (Step 4.4ci2): the vanilla jobs install the browsers behind the egress block, which runs on every OS (its pre-step starts with the job), with Chrome for Testing's host allowed; the wheel installs in a clean venv with its declared dependencies at the lock's versions, offline from what `uv sync --locked` cached (`web_tools.install`), so it installs on a fresh runner; `scripts/fullsuite.py` imports `fcntl` only in its runner, so Windows collects the landing tools' tests (`tests/test_contracts.py` refuses a top-level import of one platform's own module); the app's tests keep their user folders under their own HOME on a host that sets XDG_*_HOME (GitHub's Linux runners) or on Windows; a share link or a content manifest nested too deep is refused by a scan of its brackets before json parses it (`core/jsondepth.py`), the same on every platform and in every thread, instead of by json's own limit, the C stack's; every Settings opener is as wide as the longest it can say in the font in use, so the packs' releases line up in a wide font (Linux's) too; the vanilla job clones the game data from its new home, Azurik-Modding-Hub/AzurKit-gamedata.
- The site's update check says a new release was found when WebKit's service worker, woken after its idle, shows it a moment after `registration.update()` resolves; it answered "no update" about one check in four.
- On Windows the app checks an image and saves a build: its check read through `os.pread`, which Windows hasn't, so every image's first check stopped there, and closing the app during a stalled save can remove its part file, now opened to allow that. A site built there writes `sw.js` and `host.json` with the same line ends as anywhere.
- The app's server names each file's type from its own table, never `mimetypes`, whose guess on Windows is the registry's: where that said `text/plain` for a `.mjs` a browser refused the page's modules and the app never started. The tests' servers use the same table.
- The goldens hold on the other OSes (D1016, D1023): on any host but the one that recorded them a dress's `characters.xbr` and `fx.xbr` are compared close enough through the new `tests/goldens/close.json` (a changed clip's keys and length by their sums, within 4 ULPs each; every other word of each changed resource exact, every byte outside those resources exact, `filelist.txt` but for their md5s), and the maps by their pixels and JSON, so a real difference still fails; on this Mac they stay byte for byte. Windows' `AzurKit.exe` is found by name (Step 4.4ci4).
- CI's other hosts, tests only (Step 4.4ci5, run 41352543435): Windows' pure job, cancelled at its 180 min cap, hung in `test_a_failures_note...`: 100 frames with CRLFs are 4,100 B against a pipe's 4,096, so its child stopped in its print and the test in its readline; 50 frames, and the pure job prints each thread's stack past 20 min (`faulthandler_timeout`). The frozen tree's owners name Windows' extension modules, its DLLs and Linux's hash-named numpy libraries (libquadmath's LGPL the one GNU licence held, NOTICE.md), and an unknown name prints on lines CI keeps. The shipped pictures compare by pixels off the recording host, as Windows' zlib-ng deflates otherwise (D1016). The cold-logic read's test holds every record the bar is served, as a poll missed the last stage's 0.1 s on a starved macOS runner.
- The app's `serve` on Windows answers its engine's calls (the frozen page, a dress, a save, each of which waited for good): a child process takes its parent's standard handles, and `serve`'s read blocked on its input pipe held that pipe's lock, so the engine's Python stopped at its first look at its own stdin until the app was killed, and then died at `spawn_main`'s `DuplicateHandle` (WinError 5). `serve`'s process makes its standard input NUL before it spawns the engine; `sys.stdin` still reads the pipe (Step 4.4ci5, run 41352543435).
- Three tests held to the machine they first ran on: the app's build-ahead test now runs on a machine with room for it (the peak an in-process build reports is the whole test process's, so under 16 GiB `ahead.room` rightly refused), the Randomize-with-no-seed test waits for its setup to be remembered (its check, which reads the logic, went on past the test and wrote that setup into the next test's HOME), and the perf gate's scratch folder on Windows, which has no `/tmp`, is its temp folder; elsewhere it stays in `/tmp`, out of reach of macOS's daily sweep of `$TMPDIR`.
- The app on Windows no longer loses what it remembers when something holds its config open: Windows refuses to replace an open file, which `_remember` took as a convenience gone and never tried again. A read in the app now waits for the write, and the write outlasts a moment another process (a virus scanner) holds the file. A write it still can't make says why on the app's stderr.
- CI's Macs lost a tier's every later page to macOS's daily sweep of its temp folder: dirhelper deletes what looks 3 days old there at 03:35, and `web_tools.site` copied Pyodide's core with its release's times, so a run that crossed 03:35 UTC served a site with no Pyodide (run 41372636477: 42 of `test_site.py`'s tests, from the first page after 03:35 to the end, each its whole 120 s). A site's files are now as new as the site (a developer's own night run too), and a macOS tier's temp folder is the tier's own, out of the sweep's reach. No player's page reads a temp folder.
- CI's Apple Silicon Mac drew another `icons.png` than the shipped one (run 41415172839: sha256 14bad451eca7, not 7081a41f2d9b) while every other picture matched: the sheet's menu art shrank by float sums that numpy hands to Accelerate's BLAS, whose kernels add in an order of their own on each chip and macOS release, and 1,758 of its 249,920 tile values sit on an exact tie (x.5), so their rounding followed the last bit of a sum (here, 20 other orders of the same sums moved 217 values in 14 of 45 tiles). `art.icons.fit` now sums in integers and rounds each mean half up exactly, so every machine draws the same bytes and the test stays byte for byte on the recording host; 102 values of the sheet moved by one level, so the art is recorded again under `ART_VERSION` 1-e1515f85b2c1. A picture that differs on the recording host now names where: its pixels, their largest difference, their box and, on the sheet, each tile (Step 4.4ci8).
- Off the recording host `tests/test_art.py` judged the icon sheet close enough, at most 512 of its 102,400 pixels other, so a recoloured icon (6 of 59) or a blank obsidian passed on Linux, Windows or an Intel Mac. The sheet's pixels are now the same on every host, as they are drawn: its menu art sums integers (Step 4.4ci8) and its 14 model icons move no pixel with every sine, cosine and hypot moved by up to 4 ULPs (or by 1e-8 of itself), and x86_64 with OpenBLAS under Rosetta draws it byte for byte; so `icons.png`'s decoded pixels are compared exactly everywhere (its bytes still differ on Windows, by zlib-ng), and any changed icon fails by its tile's name. A map stays close enough, now box by box too: at most 1/200 of its pixels and 1/8 of any 40 px box (x86's rounding moves 84 of one box's 1,600 at most, in airship.png), each crowded box named with its count, largest difference and the sha12s (Step 4.4ci9).
- The frozen app on an Intel Mac dresses a species body again: zig linked the macOS kernels' libraries with no room after their load commands, and the freeze's ad-hoc `codesign` (the x86_64 library came unsigned) wrote its 16-byte LC_CODE_SIGNATURE over `az_origins`' first bytes, which then ran as `sbb eax, 0x10000000; add [rax], al` and faulted at 0xf0000000 (tests.yml run 41521288667, macos-15-intel, exit -11); `nativekit` now links both with `-headerpad_max_install_names` (1,984 bytes of room) and `tests/test_native.py` checks the committed libraries keep room for it (Step 4.4ci10).
- CI's other hosts, tests only (Step 4.4ci10, run 41521288667): the site's pages say 4 cores, as GitHub's Apple Silicon runner has 3 and `workers.mjs` starts its second worker only from 4, so the tests of that worker's work waited for one that never started; the pool's path with no second worker keeps its stub row, where the first answers every ask in turn. The reshape test's stub engines answer the page's own preview of the chosen model, which held the first worker whenever the page drew before the shipped pictures' index came. The kept-content test reads each worker's lines apart, by the worker Playwright names: the first's and the second's, three each. The untrusted-content test's bound for the Install that writes three 5 MiB files adds the time this host takes to write them past the Mac's, as Windows' runner wrote them far slower than its CPU's share allowed (0.282 s against 0.201 s). The app's Track bar test clicks in the call that starts its clock, so the 250 ms it allows runs from the click, not from a round trip before it. The build file test reloads the other tab only once the closed tab's lock on the saved build's file is gone, as the next load removes it only then. Windows' tier sat idle to the job's cap with nothing printed: Step 4.4ci12 makes such a hang name itself.
- A hung CI vanilla tier names itself (Step 4.4ci12; run 41521288667's Windows tier sat silent 3.5 h until the job's 330-min limit cut it off, its pytest log never printed): a test past 60 min puts each thread's stack in the log (`faulthandler_timeout=3600`, above the slowest legit test's ~45 min on the slowest runner); `scripts/ci_resources.py --watch` prints the log's last 200 lines and the tier's processes with their ages, then stops them (xdist's workers first, then after up to 2 min what is left, so pytest still writes its summary, its failures and the test each worker was running), once the log hasn't grown in 90 min or 10 min before the job's limit, so the step ends, prints its summary and fails with time left; and an `always()` step prints the log's tail when the step was cut off before printing it, as the job's limit runs no trap.
- A character's build in a browser that makes its build file but gives no sync handle to it (WebKit on Windows, whose `IPC::SharedFileHandle` has no Windows form, so `createSyncAccessHandle` rejects) keeps its pieces as Blobs and now removes the empty file it made, which before stayed in the origin's storage until the next load; the build file test holds that fallback on that browser (Step 4.4ci13, run 41551497620).
- A content file's suffix is one its folder takes (`content.SUFFIXES`): a variant's recipe and the files read beside it (`.json`, `.glb`, `.gltf`, `.bin`, `.png`), clips (`.glb`, `.gltf`, `.bin`), sounds (`.wav`), effects (`.glb`, `.gltf`, `.bin`, `.png`), a pack's `.json`, and a `.md` note in each; any other, such as `.html`, `.svg` or `.js`, is refused by name at Install and on the site, as the content repository's check refuses it (Step S1, D992 SEC-3's allow-list).
- A lint test keeps the page free of HTML sinks: no file under `web/` sets `innerHTML` or `outerHTML`, calls `insertAdjacentHTML`, `document.write`, `createContextualFragment` or `parseFromString`, or holds a `srcdoc` or a `javascript:` URL, so no text from a manifest, recipe or mods file becomes markup on the site's origin (Step S1, D992 SEC-18).
- An Install checks each file as it lands and writes it into the entry's `.part-<id>/` folder, so it holds one file at a time (4 files of 5 MiB: 5.4 MB held, from 21 MB), and a refused file removes the parts and writes nothing; an entry's files, and an Install's (the entry and those it requires), are at most 256 MiB (Step S1, D992 SEC-9; the hosts' fetch reading at most a file's size is 4.3m's).
- A content manifest is checked in time linear in its size: its requires are walked once with a done set and a stack of its own, so an 80-entry lattice that took months is checked at once and a 5,000-deep chain is named, not a `RecursionError`; a manifest holds at most 1,000 entries and 10,000 files, a JSON nested too deep or a requires that isn't a list of ids is refused by name (Step S1, D992 SEC-6).
- A PNG is inflated with bounds (`core/inflate.py`): one past 4096x4096 is refused before a byte inflates, and image data longer than its header's rows (a zlib bomb: 64 MiB of file to about 66 GB) is refused after at most one byte more, in the textures' reader and in a glTF folder's read-back (Step S1, D992 SEC-5).
- A recipe's file fields (a glTF clip's `file`, a mesh's `gltf.file`, a colours or effects picture's and an effect mesh's `file`) are files in the recipe's folder: a `..` part, an absolute path, a `\`, a `:` or a NUL is refused by name when the recipe is read, so a recipe someone hands you reads nothing outside its folder, and a clip file's sha256 mismatch names its first 12 digits (Step S1, D992 SEC-14).
- An Update that renames a file by letter case only (`Note.md` to `note.md`) removes the dropped files before the new ones move in, so on a case-blind disk (APFS, NTFS) the new file stays and `installed.json` lists what is there; a dropped file another entry holds, letter case aside, stays (Step S1, D992 SEC-15).
- An Update removes only the paths `installed.json` lists that are content paths, so a hand-edited record naming `../` or an absolute path never deletes a file outside the content folder; a content path with a `:` (a drive or an NTFS stream) or a name Windows keeps for a device (`CON`, `NUL.json`, `COM1`) is refused, and two entries' paths that differ only in letter case are one file, as the content repository's check holds (Step S1, D992 SEC-15).
- **Firefox's Save shows how far its few-parts Blob is made** (Step 4.4m, D980, D1053): the copy that Blob needs after a build's answer read its parts in one `Response.blob()` with a moving stripe and no count, 0.56-1.18 s after Build keylord (the perf gate's `R14` in Firefox, 1.215 s with no progress); it now reads them in 16 MiB runs and the bottom bar counts each to 100 %, in about the same time (0.79-1.38 s in the gate's runs); a count of each stream chunk took it to 1.84 s.
- **The perf gate's later visit on the site opens in the browser opened again on its profile** (Step 4.4m, D1067, D1073): `S02`'s return visit and `S64`'s build at a later visit opened a page beside the first visit's closed one, whose process WebKit keeps for the site's service worker (registered there) while any page of the site is open and 10 s after, with everything it held resident (a keylord build's 4.12 GiB, of which 1.03 GiB footprint), so the capped site peak summed both visits: 6.89-7.17 GiB, over the 6 GiB line. Opened again on the profile (the dress, the image's stamp and sw.js's release kept), the later visit's processes are its own: the run's peak 4.66 GiB, `S64` still dressed from the kept dress (0.006 s, steady heap 0.93 GiB).
- **A site test's call through `tests/_page.py` that its browser leaves unanswered fails by name** (Step 4.4ci14, D1072): Playwright gives an evaluate, a page's opening and its close no bound, so when Windows' Firefox ended in the middle of the pack golden test [firefox-europe] and its driver never saw it, the test sat silent 2 h 10 min until the tier's stall watch stopped it (run 41610020282). Each such call that `tests/_page.py`'s helpers make (its waits, clicks, fills and builds), a site page's opening and close (`test_site.Site`) and the pack golden test's own evaluates now has one: a wait's evaluate its timeout and 120 s, the others 120 s; past it the call fails naming itself, with the page's state, whether its browser still answers and the processes under its driver, and a browser that answers nothing has its connection closed, so the module's later calls fail at once by the same name. The site's tests' other evaluates and the pages, contexts and routes they make themselves have none yet, and the tier's stall watch still ends those (a known flaw).
- **The site's tests hand the browser the site's files from Playwright's driver's own fetch** (Step 4.4ci14): they sent each file's body to the driver, whose pipe reader joins its whole buffer again at every chunk, so a site page's 35 MB took the driver from 0.1 to about 2 GiB (2.5-3.0 at its peak), kept for the module's later tests; beside WebKit's pages that was more than GitHub's 7 GiB Apple Silicon runner had free, the likeliest reason its WebKit page processes died ("Target crashed", on that runner alone, runs 41551497620 and 41610020282; its logs don't show the kill). Each file is now fetched by the driver from a server on 127.0.0.1, once per browser context, into a request context of its own: the driver stays at 0.35-0.52 GiB, and the pack golden and Characters tests' processes at 3.2-3.4 GiB, were 5.4-5.8 (3 pages at a time, as on that runner).
- **A site test whose page was still loading a file when it closed no longer fails the next test** (Step 4.4ci15): since the driver fetches the site's files itself, a route can still be fetching when its `Site` closes; the close disposes that fetch's request context, the route raised "Request context disposed", and sync Playwright keeps a route's error for its next call, so the module's next test failed at its first call (the full run on 7cb671e: the WebKit hash test and the Firefox Tools tab test, at `Site`'s `add_init_script`, a numpy wheel's fetch in flight). `_Held` now leaves a route its browser context's close overtakes to Playwright, which drops a closed context's requests; that error while the context is open, and every other, still raises. The closes keep their order and their routes, so what a closing page still asks for is dropped, never sent out unrouted, as an `unroute_all(behavior="ignoreErrors")` before the close would. Test: a `Site` closed while a route's fetch is held until the driver drops it leaves the next `Site`'s first call clean, in each browser (without the fix it fails so in all three).
- **The site's tests pass on Windows again** (Step 4.4ci15): `_Held`'s file server kept socketserver's listen queue of 5 while the driver fetches a page's files at once (a load routes 33), and Windows refuses a connect past a full queue where macOS and Linux drop it and the client tries again, so 29 of Windows' site tests failed on `APIRequestContext.get: connect ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1` (tests.yml run 41666183947, windows-x86_64 pure, through the route error sync Playwright keeps for the next call). The server (`test_site._Files`) now queues as many as the system allows (`socket.SOMAXCONN`). Test: 64 connections made before it accepts any are all held and answered (with a queue of 5, the 6th connect on this Mac waits for a retry that never comes).
- **The unanswered-call test reads an open page's state in the time it has outside the test** (Step 4.4ci15): it read the state of a page whose promise never settles with `_page.HEAR` cut to 1 s, and on GitHub's Intel Mac runner WebKit's page didn't answer that read in 1 s (tests.yml run 41666183947, macos-x86_64 pure, `state: unread (no answer in 1 s)`). That part now reads with `HEAR`'s own 10 s, and still requires the state read and named; only the page kept busy 8 s, whose read must go unread, keeps 1 s. The probe of a stalled call's browser (its cookies) has a bound of its own, `_page.PROBE` (10 s), so that part's "its browser still answers" no longer shares the 1 s either.
- **The app's plan file spoiler bar test times the page from the pick it hears** (Step 4.4ci16): it timed the first bar from a clock started in the call before Playwright's pick, so the pick's own round trip counted as the page's, and on GitHub's Intel Mac runner the bar came 1430 ms after that clock against 500 (tests.yml run 41678038275, macos-x86_64 pure, Firefox; here the round trip is 27-55 ms and the page's own part 1-7 ms). The page now notes its pick (the change event) and each request it makes, and the test holds the behaviour on its own clock and order: it asks for the spoiler within 500 ms of the pick, the bar shows the ask's own line before the first progress poll (a kind the page doesn't follow shows only after a poll, or at 1 s), that poll comes under the 750 ms the old wait took, and the count still moves.
- **The kept-dress site test reads the engine's dress notes as the page's workers post them** (Step 4.4ci16): it read "(reading its files)" off the bottom bar, which shows the newest ask's latest record, and on Windows' WebKit the bar went from "Building" to "Reading the models…" to "Dressing keylord… 100 %" (tests.yml run 41678038275, windows-x86_64 vanilla): "Reading the models…" with no count is the models' ask's own line, shown past 1 s as the newest ask (made after the click), so the build's first dress notes came and went under it (here that ask is quicker and its count reaches the build's bar). `HEARD` now also keeps each record's label as the page hears it, and the test holds the first visit's notes to "(reading its files)" and no "(kept from an earlier build)", the later visit's the other way round.
- **The app's community pack test names where a relaunch's setup was lost** (Step 4.4ci16): on Windows' WebKit (tests.yml run 41678038275 attempt 2, windows-x86_64 vanilla) the relaunch's rows never locked to the pack's 150 in 60 s, with no ask open and an empty status; launch 1 would have said "Installed hard v1" and a restore "Restored your last setup", so that page had no setup to restore. How it was lost isn't shown: the page's `api/setup` that never reached the app's config, a later write of the config that read it as empty (`_kept()` gives `{}` on any read error, so `_remember` then writes only its own key), or the relaunch's read of it. The test now holds each step by name: the config holds the setup with the pack after the tick and after each build (the image's path remembered beside it), and the relaunch's page has it to restore, the config printed. The console's "Refused to apply a stylesheet" lines are WebKit's own, one for each `<select>` on a page under the CSP (on macOS too, with no `securitypolicyviolation` event; a blank page under the same CSP logs none), and change no value.
- **A site test's page load has the new page's bound and fails by name** (Step 4.4ci16): `Site.open`'s `goto` had Playwright's default 30 s, and on GitHub's Intel Mac runner the Firefox Tools tab test's load went past it, saying only "navigating to https://azurkit.test/, waiting until load" (tests.yml run 41678038275, macos-x86_64 vanilla). The load is the page's own files (about 0.4 MB, 19-20 of them, in 0.1-0.2 s here; the engine's 35 MB come after it), so it now has `_page.STALL` as the new page does, through `_page.within`: past it the failure names the page's state, whether its browser still answers and its driver's processes.
- **The app keeps what it remembers when Windows refuses a read of its config for a moment** (Step 4.4ci16): `_remember` merged into `_kept()`, which gives `{}` on any read error, so a read refused while another process (a virus scanner on the config just replaced) held the file made the next remember, a build's image path, write its own key alone: the player's setup, theme and Content from lost, a likely cause of the relaunch with no setup in the community pack test (tests.yml run 41678038275 attempt 2, windows-x86_64 vanilla). The read is now tried again on the replace's waits (`app.WAITS`, 1.55 s in all); one refused past them writes nothing and says why on stderr, as a failed write does, and a config that holds no settings object is written over, said on stderr. A page's read (`host.json`, the theme, the start image) is tried again too, as a page handed no setup remembers its next change over the kept one; `host.json` reads the config once.
- **The test of a cancel's next engine no longer hangs in a process that counted no kernel call** (Step 4.4ci16): it waited, unbounded, for the engine's count message before its answer, but its stubbed build reaches nothing, so the message only came from counts an earlier test had left in the same process (`native.CALLS`); run alone, or after tests that left none (the changed-area gate on this lane: gw6 after `test_ahead` and `test_characters`' cases), the engine sends its answer alone and the test sat until the gate's 1800 s cap. The build now counts its own kernel call on counts of the test's own (as `test_native` does) and the test reads `("calls", 1, {"kernel": 1})` then the answer, each within 10 s or failing by name.
- **CI's pure tier prints its room each minute as the vanilla tier does, and on macOS what Playwright's processes hold and how one ended** (Step 4.4ci17): a WebKit page of the pure tier died ("Target crashed") on GitHub's 7 GB Apple Silicon runner as `test_a_packs_settings_lock…[webkit]`'s `Site` waited for its engine (tests.yml run 41744982709), and nothing said the memory, the page's footprint or why WebKit's page process ended. `scripts/ci_resources.py` now runs beside the pure tier too (each minute the free disk and memory, at the end their lowest), and on macOS each line adds the footprint of Playwright's processes (its driver and every process of the browsers it starts, WebKit's pages, network and GPU among them, which launchd starts outside the tier's tree: `proc_pid_rusage`'s phys_footprint, what jetsam and WebKit's memory kill read), together and the largest; at the end, the most they held, each one a crash report or a jetsam event since the tier began says ended (its exception or jetsam's reason, its last footprint and the free memory then), and WebKit's own log lines on a process that ended (`WebPageProxy::processDidTerminate`'s reason, its memory and CPU kills, an unresponsive process), since a WebKit kill writes no report. Names and numbers only, as before.
- **Every page load and reload of the site's tests fails by name past Playwright's 30 s** (Step 4.4ci17): `test_the_page_remembers_its_setup…[webkit]`'s reload went past Playwright's default 30 s on GitHub's Intel Mac runner saying only "waiting for navigation until load" (tests.yml run 41744982709, macos-x86_64 pure): its new document never committed (Playwright logs a navigation once it commits). 19 `goto`s (15 of `test_site.py`'s, 4 of `test_service_worker.py`'s), `_page.first_paint`'s and 2 `reload`s had that 30 s and named nothing else. `tests/_page.py`'s `load` keeps each in it (`_page.ACT`), past which it names the address, the page's state, whether its browser still answers and its driver's processes; `Site.open`'s load keeps `_page.STALL`, as the new page has. Here a reload takes 26-68 ms and the engine is ready 2.3-2.9 s after it in each browser, and the page's own work at a reload is one `localStorage` write (`pagehide`): what WebKit waited on there (its old page's engine workers ending, the routed request) is not in the log, which 4.4ci17's minute lines now give the pure tier.
- **The pack golden builds no more of its pages at once than the machine's free memory holds** (Step 4.4ci17): on GitHub's 7 GB Apple Silicon runner (3 cores; its vanilla tier, one worker, read 1.2-5.3 GiB free a minute, 3.0 the median) `test_each_ported_pack_builds_its_g1_golden_in_each_browser[webkit-usa]` built a page a core, 3 at once, about 3.3 GiB with its driver and pytest, and one of its WebKit pages died ("Target crashed": tests.yml run 41744982709, macos-arm64 vanilla). Each group now takes a page a core and no more than the free memory read before it holds, 1.25 GiB a page past 1.5 GiB kept for pytest's and the driver's growth and 1 GiB of headroom (WebKit's page process at most 1045 MiB with 2 at once on this Mac read as that runner, 2.8 GiB with the driver and pytest at their peak): 2 at once there with 4.0 GiB free, then 1 once pytest has grown (3.0-3.5 GiB), 4 on the Windows runner, every core here.
- **A file pick and a browser's close in the site's tests fail by name past a bound of their own** (Step 4.4ci17): the Windows runner's pack golden in Firefox went silent an hour again, until the stall watch ended its worker (tests.yml run 41744982709, windows-x86_64 vanilla), with 4.4ci14's bounds on every evaluate, page opening and close in place: no firefox.exe was left, the memory back at the runner's idle 13 GiB free, and its driver had never heard it end (Playwright's driver hears a browser end only once its process and every handle of its pipes have closed, and removes its profile only then). Its calls with no bound of this side's were each `set_input_files` (47 in `test_site.py`), now `_page.pick`, failing past the same 30 s on this side too (`_page.ACT`) with the file, the page's state and its driver's processes, and the `browser` fixture's close, now `conftest.close_browser`, failing past 120 s with its browser and driver, whose `sync_playwright` then ends it. Why Firefox ends there is not shown: the runner's temp volume lost 6-15 GiB through that test in each run read (passing ones too), the 13.5 GiB of this one never back, while on this Mac the same test, 4 pages at once as there, keeps at most 29 MiB of Firefox's temporary files and a 46 MiB profile, and a page's Blob of its build's own bytes is at most 1.9 MiB.
- **A browser's close that its driver never answers says what it waits on** (Step 4.4ci18): WebKit's close after the pack golden on GitHub's Windows runner never answered in 120 s (tests.yml run 41779807138, windows-x86_64 vanilla), and the failure named only the browser and its driver. Playwright's driver answers a close only once the browser's process has ended and every handle of its stdout, stderr and protocol pipes has closed, then removes its temporary folders, and Playwright's WebKit on Windows starts each of its processes with its own stdout and stderr and every handle it may pass on (its `ProcessLauncherWin.cpp`), so one that outlives it holds the close, out of reach of the driver's kill. The failure now says whether the driver heard the browser's pipe close and lists, within 10 s in all (`scripts/ci_resources.py`'s `held`), the processes under the driver and each one a parent that ended left running since it began, with ids, parents, ages and memory, the crash handlers (Windows Error Reporting's, Firefox's, macOS's), Playwright's temporary folders since then and the browser's downloads folder with their sizes, and on Windows what Windows Error Reporting wrote since; the CI tier's stall watch lists its processes' memory, the crash handlers, what a parent that ended left running since the tier began and those folders too.
- **The site tests' browser close no longer waits forever on a process WebKit's ended browser left running on Windows** (Step 4.4ci18): WebKit's close after the pack golden never answered on GitHub's Windows runner (tests.yml run 41779807138, windows-x86_64 vanilla), and twice before that tier's worker went silent there until the stall watch ended it (runs 41744982709 and 182336a's, taken for the Firefox pack golden, the next item, whose setup runs the WebKit browser's close), each time with the driver's browser gone, nothing under the driver and a WebKitWebProcess.exe left running by its ended parent, begun 3 and 13 min after that driver. Playwright's driver hears a browser end only once every handle of its pipes has closed; Playwright's WebKit on Windows hands each process it starts its own stdout and stderr, so a page process left after it holds them, and the driver's kill (`taskkill /T` of the browser, 30 s in) can't reach a process whose parent has ended. Once a close has waited 10 s (a close takes at most 0.5 s here), the `browser` fixture now ends each browser's process a parent that ended left running since its driver began, as the driver's kill would have had the parent lived, and a warning names each with its id, parent, age and memory; one under the driver, older than it, not a browser's or not run from Playwright's folders (a user's own Chrome or Edge) runs on. Why WebKit's page process outlives its browser there is not known.
- **The app's build-and-cancel test waits for the build's own steps, not this Mac's seconds** (Step 4.4ci19): on GitHub's Intel Mac runner `test_the_apps_engine_process_answers_small_asks_during_a_build_and_cancel_stops_it` saw no pool child in its 10 s (tests.yml run 41779807138). The swap's first parallel part is the dress's bake, after the engine process's start and the build's check, size, read and pack: 0.6-0.9 s after the post here, 4.9-14.4 s under `taskpolicy -b` and nice 19 beside a load of 30-49, where the test failed so 2 times in 5 with 8 busy loops beside it, once as on CI and once with no stage yet at its fixed 1.5 s. It now waits for the build's first record and then its pool while the build runs, and a build that ends first fails saying what it gave.
- **CI's vanilla tier names the tests that take its time** (Step 4.4ci19): the Intel Mac tier of tests.yml run 41779807138 took 293 min, near its job's 330, where 4a178cc's took 159, and its log, which prints only the tail and the summary, named no test's time. The tier now runs with `--durations=60 --durations-min=60`, whose list of each test phase past a minute comes inside the summary it prints.
- **The pack golden picks every page's image before any of its pages builds** (Step 4.4ci19): on GitHub's Intel Mac runner `test_each_ported_pack_builds_its_g1_golden_in_each_browser[webkit-usa]` had no answer to a pick in its 30 s (tests.yml run 41779807138), the page's change handler already run. The site isn't slow (measured here): the handler takes 1-6 ms and WebKit's `DOM.setInputFiles` makes path-backed files without reading the image; the pick is Playwright's round trips to the page, which the group's pages building beside it starved. Under `taskpolicy -b` and nice 19 with 8 busy loops a pick took up to 8.6 s so (up to 14.0 s with the group at 21 pages, a group's first pick at most 2.4 s), and at most 3.6 s with every pick first, at a heavier load.
- **A character a page builds is dressed once, even when its click waits on the picked image's read** (Step 4.4ci20): the kept-dress site test's first visit heard both "(reading its files)" and "(kept from an earlier build)" on GitHub's Windows runner (tests.yml run 41818469100 attempt 2, windows-x86_64 vanilla): the page built keylord twice. The click's build waited behind the image's read, and an ahead() made meanwhile listed no build (none is listed until the image is read), so the pool forgot that the click had taken the build's key; the read listed it again, and once the build answered, one heap swapped the worker it had grown out as B, which built it once more over the dress it had just kept. A key an ask still waits on (queued for F, F's job, or a job a click took from B) now stays taken while the list drops it (`web/workers.mjs`'s `asked()`), and the ask's end (an answer, a cancel or a stopped worker) frees it if the list doesn't hold it then, so a recipe changed during the build and changed back after it is still built ahead, as before; pinned on stub workers. In WebKit, with the Randomizer tab's Settings view clicked right after Build (an ahead() before the read), the old pool built keylord twice in 10 of 10 visits and this one once in 10 of 10.
- **CI's stall watch says what WebKit's GPU process was doing when it stopped answering** (Step 4.4ci21): on GitHub's Intel Mac runner (tests.yml run 41818469100, macos-x86_64 vanilla) a page's wait on WebKit's GPU process ran out 15 s at the first WebKit minute, WebKit ended that GPU process, each new one left its first message 3 s and was ended too, about 40 times in the hour, and every later WebKit test failed, with nothing saying why. On macOS `scripts/ci_resources.py` now follows WebKit's log as it is written and, the first 3 times a line says a process stopped answering, samples each of Playwright's GPU processes and the process that wrote it at once (each watched thread's heaviest stack, CoreAnimation's queues that `sample` shows on several threads among them, the calls they were most often in: symbols only); each minute's line has each GPU process's share of a core, page-ins and footprint, WindowServer's share and the load; WebKit's lines past the first 20 are counted by the call that wrote them, and the tier's end names the runner's GPU and its Metal support.
- **The bottom bar's moving stripe no longer makes the page draw itself again every frame** (Step 4.4ci21): a call with no count showed a `<progress>` with no value in its own look, which WebKit (Safari's engine) draws again 60 times a second, each a round trip to its GPU process, and Firefox draws again too; 4 such pages took 9 % of a core in WebKit's GPU process, 14 % in their page processes and 4 % in its UI process here (its GPU process's memory 62 to 111 MiB), the GPU process a busy computer starves first. The stripe is now a transform the browser's compositor moves over a plain track (`web/app.css`), for none of either (UI 0.5 %), the same look; with reduced motion it stands still.
- **The bottom bar is written only when what it shows changes** (Step 4.4ci21): its 250 ms tick and the engine's records rewrote the bar's line, percent and row 23 times a second while it showed, half of them with what it already showed (the pack golden's pages here), and each write draws it again, in WebKit a round trip to its GPU process. `progressed` in `web/app.mjs` now writes each part only when it differs.
- **A model picked while the Characters tab still reads the image is answered at once** (Step 4.4ci21): in WebKit, the pick of a reshaped variant (the perf gate's S12) took 1.0-1.3 s with its bar unchanged, failing D980 on a busy computer. The pick started its build ahead in B, the free worker, before it asked for the tab's answer, so that answer (without the image, while the image's is worked out) waited behind F's earlier asks, the tab's first answers on the image (0.5-0.7 s each here). The pick now asks first, so B answers it in a moment and then starts the build ahead.
- **The free-worker pick test picks once F works out the tab's answer** (Step 4.4stack): the shipped answer lists the Characters tab's models about 5 ms before the tab's own ask on the image reaches F, and the test picked keylord as soon as the list showed, so with 4.4ba's Save under it F was still free at the pick in WebKit (3 of 3 runs, where 3 of 3 passed without it); it now waits for F's ask first, as its docstring says.
- **The app's bar follows every ask's record from its click** (Step 4.4bb, D980, D1107): the page asked the app's server for an ask's progress only once the ask had waited 750 ms (a build, a save, Track's plan and a plan file's spoiler aside), so the G4 match of the Characters tab (the perf gate's `A11`, 1.1-1.5 s) showed its first count at 0.76-0.96 s and its second past 1 s, and in b339758's full run 2 of its 6 takes (app and capped) showed one value: no progress. Each ask's record is now polled from its click, every 250 ms while any ask is in flight, and a poll's record goes only to the asks made before the poll, never to a later ask of the same kind. In the gate's A/B on this Mac beside other projects' load (6 takes a side, app and capped), A11's first count shows at 0.15-0.27 s and its second at 0.51-1.01 s, every take moving (were 0.76-0.86 s and 1.03-1.62 s). Test: a 1.2 s call of a kind the bar doesn't follow from the click, its record counting 12 steps, moves the bar to its second value within 1 s of its ask (it took it past 1 s before).
- **CI's stall watch reads its minute line on every OS** (Step 4.4ci22): 4.4ci21's GPU-process line read `os.getloadavg`, which Windows has not, so its test failed there with an AttributeError (tests.yml run 41981438506, windows-x86_64 pure). `scripts/ci_resources.py`'s load is now `unread` where the OS keeps none, and its `proc_pid_rusage` read answers none off macOS; the watch itself reads WebKit's log, `sample`, the footprints and the GPU line only on macOS as before, and its `ps` and `log` reads say "unread" where they can't run. The test reads the line through each OS's own calls, the load as a figure where the OS gives one.
- **The app's bar test asks with a full path on every OS** (Step 4.4ci22): 4.4bb's test of the bar's second value within 1 s asked `verify_all` of '/nowhere/usa.iso', which Windows reads as a path with no drive, so the app refused it at once (`give the image's full path`, as it should) and the counted stand-in never ran: no bar in all three browsers (tests.yml run 41981438506, windows-x86_64 pure). It now asks of a path under the test's own folder and checks the answer is the stand-in's.
- **The build-ahead test stops its job while the job still runs, on every runner** (Step 4.4ci23): its stop of a job ahead came one call after that job's start, and a pack's build there ends within 30 ms, so on GitHub's Windows runner the job passed its last record first and ended with no Cancelled (tests.yml run 41981438506, windows-x86_64 vanilla; here 20 of 20 pack runs so with work ahead at normal priority and the stop 30 ms later, 1 of 20 under `taskpolicy -b` and nice 19). The job taken mid-flight and the one stopped now wait at their first record until their click or stop has come: 40 of 40 runs pass, plain and with the shorter job. The app already stops a job at its next record; one past its last has nothing left to stop, and its answer is never taken.
- **CI's vanilla tier takes the runner's roomiest disk, frees Linux's unused toolchains when short and stops by name before its images when no disk has its room** (Step 4.4ci24): tests.yml run 41981438506's Linux runner was provisioned on AWS (`Cloud: AWS`, us-west-2, where each earlier one was on Azure) and had 9.5 GiB free past the two images, where each of the six Azure runs logged had 78.3; the tier fell to 3.8 GiB free, a USA build's output from full. `scripts/ci_resources.py --room` now prints each disk's free room before the images land and picks the roomiest (Linux's `/mnt` only where it has more); under the two images (4.7 GiB), the most a tier's writes took (9.8 GiB, Windows; Linux 6.1, macOS 6.9, so 10) and a 5 GiB margin it deletes the unused .NET, Android, GHC and CodeQL toolchains first, printing what each freed, and under the first two it stops with an `::error::` naming the need and the room, before an image is rebuilt. `tests/test_tiers.py` pins the choice, the freeing and the refusal.
- **The image-stamp site tests count the checks of the page's workers still running** (Step 4.4ci24): `test_two_files_with_the_same_name_size_and_time_but_other_bytes_get_other_stamps[chromium]` failed on tests.yml run 41981438506's Linux tier with "Worker.evaluate: Target page, context or browser has been closed". The page ends the worker it starts once the pick's check is done about as its last answer comes, and that worker stays in Playwright's `page.workers` until its close is heard, so evaluating each listed worker in turn threw on it: 1 run in 5 here, its close heard 3.6 ms after the read, with the disk and memory nowhere near full (the CI tier's lowest 3.8 GiB free disk and 5.4 GiB memory, each read every 5 s). `tests/_page.py`'s `checks` sums the workers that still answer, an ended one counting none, and both stamp tests use it: 8 runs of 8 passed, and with every worker that ended kept in the list (a probe) the evaluates threw 4 times and both tests passed.
- **The image-stamp tests' check count passes a worker that ends while asked in Firefox too** (Step 4.4ci24, the review's fix): Firefox fails the evaluate of a worker that ends while it is in flight with a plain `Error` ("Worker.evaluate: Worker was closed"), not `TargetClosedError` as Chromium and WebKit do, so the count still threw there. Playwright's driver sends the worker's close before that error, so `checks` now counts any error of a worker no longer listed as none and raises one of a worker still running. Test: a worker the page ends while its count is asked counts none in all three engines (it failed in Firefox before), and a running worker whose count throws fails the count by its error.
- **CI's stall watch says where a crashed browser process faulted** (Step 4.4ci25): the Apple Silicon vanilla tier's WebKit page died in the kept-dress test (tests.yml run 41981438506, macos-arm64 vanilla, "Target crashed"), and the watch said only `EXC_BAD_ACCESS (SIGBUS)`, the page's 0.52 GiB at its highest and 3.6 GiB free 5 s before: no memory kill (jetsam's would be a kill, WebKit's a reason of its own) and nothing of where. `scripts/ci_resources.py`'s crash line now adds the fault's kind and address, the region macOS says the address is in (its kind, size and protection, no path), what the process said as it ended, and its crashed thread's name and innermost 8 frames, so the next one names its cause.
- **The image's rest, hashed in the background after a pick, gives way to a character's dress** (Step 4.4ci25, D1073): 4.4ba's hash of the image's other 1,151 files (1.63 GiB, for a later Save) ran beside a build clicked right after the pick, its six 128 MiB runs in flight in Safari (three in Chromium) on top of the dress: the kept-dress test's WebKit page peaked at 2.7-2.9 GiB (1.8 on 0ca2889, before the stack; 1.7 with no rest hash) and Chromium's at 2.5-2.6 (2.0-2.3), and on the Macs' CI tiers Chromium's largest process rose from 3.3-3.4 to 4.0-4.2 GiB with the stack (run 41981438506). An ask that carries a character (a build wearing one, a reshape's pictures and matches) now holds a shared Web Lock while it runs; the rest's hash starts no read while one is held and, once it has given way, goes on with one read in flight, as the page keeps the dress's heap after it. The same test peaks at 1.7-1.8 GiB in WebKit and 2.1-2.2 in Chromium again. The pick's check, Verify and a Save's hash never wait, and a pick followed by a build with no character (S05, then the Save S06) hashes the rest as before. Test: under Node, the rest's hash reads nothing while the lock is held and one run at a time after, an image's check six at once, never waiting; the kept-dress test sees the lock held during its first dress.
- **A site test's file the driver hears nothing of fails naming its server's part** (Step 4.4ci25): two site loads on GitHub's Intel Mac runner waited 30 s on one file the driver fetched from the test process's file server (tests.yml run 41981438506, macos-x86_64 vanilla: data.mjs in Chromium, icons.css in Firefox, the memory 6 GiB free), the failure naming only Playwright's timeout. Past its bound the fetch now fails saying whether the server took the request and when it answered, the CPU the test process ran meanwhile and its threads' innermost calls, so the next one names where it waited.
- **A build wearing a character builds where the browser refuses Web Locks** (Step 4.4ci25, its review): the ask that carries a character ran only as its shared lock's callback, so in a browser that keeps no site data (Firefox with every cookie blocked: "request() is not allowed in this context") it never ran and the build failed by that refusal. Refused, the ask now runs without the lock (the rest's own lock is refused there too, so no rest runs beside it); the ask's own failure stays its own. Test: with Web Locks refused on the page and in its engine's worker, keylord's build is CPython's in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (red before the fix).
- **The site's content test refuses its four suffixes again** (Step 4.4stack2): the Web Locks test's probe was named `REFUSED`, as the content test's refused suffixes are, later in `tests/test_site.py`, so the content test's four refused-suffix cases read the probe's text and failed with a KeyError; the probe is `LOCK_ASKED` now.
- **The untrusted-content test times each refusal's own work** (Step 4.4ci26): on GitHub's Windows runner the Install refused at its fourth 5 MiB file took 0.444 s against its bound of 0.171 s (tests.yml run 42071325305, windows-x86_64 pure; 0.282 s in run 41521288667), though that runner did the same three writes only 0.008 s slower than the Mac and its CPU work 1.6 x: the time was the runner's waiting, not the Install's work (reasoned, not reproduced: the row failed in 2 of the 8 Windows runs logged, and GitHub's Windows images turn Defender's real-time scan off). The Install checks each file as it lands and writes nothing it could skip, so the product is unchanged. The overflow now counts a line by its calls, its `reason=` and the message refused. Tests: `test_ci_resources_names_playwrights_processes_footprints_and_what_macos_wrote_of_one_that_ended_and_where`, `test_ci_resources_names_what_webkits_gpu_process_did_as_it_stopped_answering_and_its_state_each_minute`.

That step, the game data's clone over SSH (its sparse set too, which fetches the parts) and Azurik-Custom-Content's over HTTPS had no retry at all, and `scripts/web_tools.py`'s downloads of Pyodide's core and wheels none either. A retry on the same route can't outlast a cut port 22, and ssh had no bound on a silent connection. Every fetch (the clone, and the sparse set's through its origin) now goes to `ssh://git@ssh.github.com:443/`, the route GitHub documents for a network that cuts port 22; the one known host is `[ssh.github.com]:443` with github.com's published ed25519 key, which ssh.github.com serves too (checked by `ssh-keyscan` against `GET /meta`), under strict checking; ssh gives up a connect after 30 s and a connection silent 60 s (`ServerAliveInterval=15`, `ServerAliveCountMax=4`), so `scripts/ci_retry.sh`'s next try runs. harden-runner's block for the vanilla job names `ssh.github.com:443` in `github.com:22`'s place. - **A call whose engine fails names the engine's call and where Python was, and an engine that traps starts again and does the page's ask again** (Step 4.4ci44, D1126): `test_each_ported_pack_builds_its_g1_golden_in_each_browser[webkit-europe]` failed on GitHub's Apple Silicon runner (tests.yml run 42236789239, lane p2/4.4ci39-b-start, 1.5 GiB of 7 free at its lowest) with only "access to a null reference (evaluating '__pyproxy_apply(t,e,n,o,s)')". That is no destroyed PyProxy (Pyodide refuses a call of one in JS, before any wasm runs): it is JavaScriptCore's name for a fault of wasm code at an address below the lowest one a process may map (its fault handler's, meant for Wasm GC's struct and array reads), inside the C call of a Python function the worker called, so a trap of the engine's wasm, which Pyodide takes as fatal; bare, it came from a call no mount of the image held, as a build's trap would have said Pyodide's "already fatally failed" (the image's unmount after it). Its message said neither which call nor where. Pyodide writes the Python stack such a trap stopped in to the engine's stdout, which `web/worker.mjs` threw away. Now the worker keeps the engine's first 40 lines out of a call, from that dump's header on (a call's last 40, its first form, lost the header and innermost frames of a dump past 40 lines, as Pyodide's at numpy's import ran 72, and named importlib's frames from mid-stack), and a call that fails says its message, the engine's call (its request's type), the innermost 4 frames of Python's stack (a trap's from that dump, an exception's from its traceback) and the trapping wasm functions by index; after a trap every later call and mount of that engine says that first failure (an image's check, a kept dress and the rest's hash post none of their own), and so does numpy's load (Pyodide refuses even a read of its `loadPackage` then, so a trap of the rest's hash was named "already fatally failed" by the next ask that loads numpy, a shared warm-up), the unmount after a failed call no longer replaces its failure, and an engine that doesn't start says where too. What made the engine's wasm fault on the runner is not shown: the next such failure names its call and place. So the page recovers from such a trap (D1126): each failure a trapped engine posts says so (`trapped`), a Characters, Tools, Tracker or Data ask's too (whose other failures are refusals: a trap there had left the engine dead, each later ask of those tabs refused with its text until a build), and `web/workers.mjs` replaces that worker at once and sends the page's job it ran to the next engine once (a second trap fails it by name; B's own work ahead is dropped, its key not tried again while listed; a trap with no job, a shared warm-up's, restarts it too, 3 times a visit, past which a trap is any failure), and the page says "The engine stopped and started again: building again" meanwhile, its bar dropping the trapped attempt's stage, and keeps where each trap was (`azurkit.recovered`). `test_a_call_whose_engine_traps_or_raises_names_its_call_and_pythons_place_and_the_engine_starts_again` traps an image's check and a build (a read past the heap, the build's 45 Python frames down) and raises in a build, in each browser; `test_a_trapped_engine_is_started_again_and_the_pages_ask_sent_again_once_saying_so` holds the pool's restarts on stub workers and, on a page whose first engine traps in the rest's hash and its next in a build, the warm-up's numpy load naming the rest's trap and the build ending built; `test_a_tabs_ask_whose_engine_traps_starts_the_engine_again_and_is_answered` traps the Characters tab's list and Track this seed's plan, each answered by the next engine.

Gated by `tests/_perf.py`'s S66 and A81 (Reset with keylord's sliders, options and weapon moved); test: `test_reset_puts_every_slider_option_and_the_weapon_back_in_its_click`.

The tab now keeps one ask in flight and asks the choices made meanwhile once it answers, as they are then: that Reset's answer comes in 0.68-0.76 s in WebKit, 0.40-0.56 s in Chromium and 1.07-1.08 s in Firefox (0.06 s in the app, before and after). - **The status line says a refusal of the page's security policy only when it is the page's own** (Step 4.4u4): 4.4u3's headless WebKit shot of the tracker's view with no seed said "The page's security policy refused style-src-elem" in red, where Chromium's and Firefox's said nothing. The refused `<style>` was Playwright's: its WebKit screenshot appends `<style>body {}</style>` a moment to sync animations (Chromium's and Firefox's tools run where the page's policy doesn't reach). The page's own code inserts no style (only `element.style`, which the policy allows) and the policy is unchanged. A refusal's `blockedURI` and `sourceFile` tell them apart, measured in all three browsers: a `<style>` or style attribute the page's script inserts names that script, but a load the page's code asks may name no file when the browser checks it once that code has returned (an image's `src`, a stylesheet's `@import` or `@font-face`, a sound, a frame: 11 such cases across the three, WebKit's `@import` and sound among them), so only an inline style or script with no file of the page's origin behind it is another's (Playwright's evaluate or screenshot; an extension's content script is inferred, not measured); that one now goes to `azurkit.violations` only, which the tests read, so the line no longer alarms a player over another's refusal.

- **The tracker's openers test waits out the openers its plan works out ahead** (Step 4.4ci55): a gate run failed `test_the_apps_page_answers_only_its_launchs_token_on_127_0_0_1` on one busy worker and passed alone: host.json's catalog and the test's own `sdk.catalog()` read moments apart differed in `native.calls`, the process's count of kernel calls. `test_the_trackers_spoiler_on_a_shaped_plan_takes_its_openers_from_the_engine_once_and_no_view_ahead_asks`, which runs two tests before it, plans a shaped seed on a Here engine, whose `engine._plan` starts the plan's openers ahead on a background-priority thread that a Here engine's close doesn't end (the app's end with its engine's process); on a busy machine that thread still reached through the later test's two reads. The openers test now joins it after its server's close. With that thread held until the close (a starved thread's stand-in), the three tests in order failed 2 runs of 10 before and none of 10 after, and the later test saw 39 kernel calls of that thread before, none after.
- **A slider's session in the Characters tab with pauses under 7 s no longer takes the site past 6 GiB in WebKit** (Step 4.4ci48): perf cases S66 and S67 (Step 4.4u2: every number, option and the weapon moved, a 3 s pause, a 30-event drag, Reset, three times in one page) took the capped profile's site peak from 6.0-6.2 GB to 7.7 GB (5.4-7.9 GB a run of that scenario alone, 3.1 GB without them; the product before 4.4u2 alike, 7.4 GB): each settled change started B's build ahead, the next move dropped it by `terminate()`, and WebKit keeps a terminated worker's heap resident (reclaimable, still counted) beside the next B's, two a pass. A change of the tab's own, a number, an option or Reset, that follows another within 7 s, no Build or Track between, now lists its build with a rest of `REST` (7 s, past Build's head start and the engine's answer to the change) before B starts it (`want({rest})` in `web/workers.mjs`); one alone and a model's pick still start as any change: 3.9-4.0 GB. Resting every such change took the head start from a Build 3 s after one number's change (0.23 s to 2.60 s in WebKit, 0.003 s to 2.60 s in Chromium); perf case S72 times that Build. A rest of 3.5 s (5.2-7.5 GB: the answer and the 3 s pause outlasted it), 1.5 s (7.2-7.6 GB), a 1.5 s hold after a dropped build (7.9 GB) and letting a dropped build go on (4.7-5.1 GB, but the page froze 0.26-0.87 s in 2 of 7 runs) fell short. A known flaw: a pause past 7 s before the next move still starts a build that move drops (the same session with an 8 s pause before the drag: 6.50 GiB in 1 of 2 runs, 4.7 GiB in the other).
